**Persistent Node host** (Railway, Render, Fly.io, VPS, Docker) is the best fit: `npm ci && npm run build && npm run start` (`next start -p 3000`) behind an HTTPS reverse proxy; same env vars; `DATABASE_URL_LISTEN` can be the direct URL. Behind nginx/Caddy disable response buffering for `/order/*/events` (the app sends `X-Accel-Buffering: no` and a ping every 25 s).

**Scheduler for the dispatcher** (anything that can make an HTTP call every minute):

| Scheduler | How |
| --- | --- |
| Vercel Cron | above |
| GitHub Actions | `on: schedule: - cron: "*/5 * * * *"` running `curl -fsS -X POST $URL/api/internal/notifications/dispatch -H "Authorization: Bearer $SECRET"` (secrets in repo settings; minimum interval 5 min) |
| Supabase `pg_cron` + `pg_net` | `net.http_post(url, headers => jsonb_build_object('Authorization','Bearer <secret>'))` every minute |
| cron-job.org / uptime pinger | POST/GET with the Authorization header |
| Same server | `* * * * * curl -fsS -X POST http://localhost:3000/api/internal/notifications/dispatch -H "Authorization: Bearer $SECRET"` |

The scheduler is a safety net (retries, changes made in SQL, crashed requests), not the primary path for checkout. One call drains the whole queue, so a per-minute schedule is enough.

## 11. Troubleshooting

Order of checks for "something did not update / send": (1) did the DB row really change and commit? (2) is the restaurant's `features` switch on (section 7)? (3) is the env var set and the server restarted? (4) only then look at code. Live updates and notifications are both driven by the committed row change, so a missing change upstream looks like a bug downstream. Confirmed once already: "live status not updating" was an order that had not been updated in the DB.

| Symptom | Cause / fix |
| --- | --- |
| Dispatch route 503 | `NOTIFICATIONS_DISPATCH_SECRET` empty/missing in that environment; redeploy/restart. |
| `npm run build` crashes with `uncaughtException EPERM: operation not permitted, open '.next	race'` (Windows) | Another Next process still has `.next` open — a `next dev`/`next start` that is running or still shutting down. Stop it (check `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='node.exe'"`), wait for it to exit, rerun. To build while a server keeps running, use a separate output folder: `$env:NEXT_DIST_DIR='.next-verify'; npx next build`. |
| `npm run build` fails at "checking validity of types" with `.next-verify/types/... Cannot find module '.../page.js'` | A stale verify build (`NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify`, section 12) kept type stubs for a page that has since moved (`(site)/page.tsx` → `(site)/(home)/page.tsx`, 2026-10-10; seen 2026-10-12), and a `next build` into that folder had added `.next-verify/types/**` to `tsconfig.json`'s `include`. Fixed: `tsconfig.json` now has `exclude: [".next-*"]`, so no scratch build folder is ever type-checked, even if Next re-adds its `include` entry. If it recurs, delete the `.next-*` folder (scratch only, gitignored) and drop any `.next-*` entry from `include`. |
| `tsc` says `Cannot find module 'exceljs'` / `'pdfkit'` | Declared in `package.json` but not installed in this clone; run `npm install` (lockfile unchanged). |
| `next dev`: a super-admin setting saves (it is in the DB) but the storefront keeps the old behaviour, usually right after a new config field was added to `shared/contract/settings.ts` | The storefront cache lives on `globalThis` and its `load` closure keeps the module versions from when the process started; hot reload does not replace it, so it keeps parsing the website config with the OLD zod schema, which silently drops the new field. **Restart `npm run dev`.** (Not an issue in production: the process starts with the current code. Checked 2026-10-19 with the phone-bar `dock` field: DB had it, a fresh server showed it, the long-running dev server did not.) |
| `tsc` says `Cannot find module '@/components/admin/confirm-dialog'` | `useConfirm` moved to `@/components/ui/confirm-dialog` (rename); update the import. |
| Dispatch route 401 | Header must be `Authorization: Bearer <secret>` without placeholder brackets; on Vercel Cron `CRON_SECRET` must equal the secret. |
| Status changed but no email/push | Nothing ran the dispatcher (section 9/10). Check `notification_events` (`status`, `attempts`, `last_error`). |
| Emails/push never sent for one restaurant, states `skipped` | That restaurant has `notifications`, `emailNotify` or `pushNotify` = false in `restaurants.features`. |
| No confirmation email at order placement | By design: it goes out on `confirmed` (`rules.ts`). |
| Status changed "in the DB" but the page did not move | Check the row: `select order_number, status, updated_at from orders where order_number = '...'`. Usual cause: update never happened / not committed (wrong order number, wrong database, open SQL-editor transaction, a transition the trigger rejected). |
| No "Live" marker / `events` 503 | `DATABASE_URL_LISTEN` unset, or it points at the 6543 transaction pooler. |
| Verification/reset code email not arriving | Not in the outbox: it is sent inline (`customer-auth.ts#sendVerificationCode`). A provider failure is now logged in the server terminal as `[notifications] verification code email to … failed <provider status: reason>`. Also: max 3 codes / 5 min per customer, and the env must be loaded (restart the dev server). Emitlo `DOMAIN_MISMATCH` = `EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` is not on the credential's sending domain (here `techodos.com`, not `mail.techodos.com`). |
| Emails not arriving | `onboarding@resend.dev` only sends to your Resend account email; verify a domain for real customers. Slow during a rush = the 2/s pacing, not a bug. |
| No "Notify me" button | One of the five `NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_*` is missing, push is off for the restaurant, or the deploy predates the vars. |
| "Notify me" succeeded, `push_state = sent`, but no notification on the device | FCM accepted it, so the fault is on the device. Check the worker is **activated**, not "waiting", in DevTools → Application → Service Workers (fixed by `skipWaiting`/`clients.claim`, but a browser that still runs a pre-fix worker needs one hard reload or "Unregister"); then OS-level notification settings for the browser (Windows Focus Assist, macOS Do Not Disturb, Android OEM battery optimisers). Notifications are never suppressed for being on the page any more — the worker shows them all (section 7). |
| Notification appears only on the order page | A worker from before the 2026-09-22 rewrite is still active: the Firebase SDK handler suppresses display whenever any window of the site is visible. Reload once (or unregister the worker) so the new one takes over. |
| Many `customer_push_tokens` rows for one device, most `is_active = false` | Token churn: something is re-registering the worker and re-issuing a token on every render. Fixed by keeping the Firebase config in a ref in `push-opt-in.tsx`; the dead rows are harmless history. |
| App errors on branches/locations after pulling | DB still has `restaurant_locations`: run `npm run db:migrate` (0016). |
| Config error "Invalid email configuration" | `EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` is not a valid email. |
| Reservation booked but no email | (1) migration 0017 applied? (`select column_name from information_schema.columns where table_name='notification_events'` has `reservation_id`); without it no event is queued. (2) `notification_events` row for the reservation and its `email_state` / `last_error`; `skipped` = no guest email, no `RESEND_API_KEY`, or the restaurant's email switch is off. (3) dispatcher running (section 9/10). |
| Guest opens My Orders and sees nothing after ordering | The order is not in progress any more (guests only see current orders), the browser lost the `rp_cart` cookie, or it is a different browser than the one that ordered. |
| Env change ignored | Restart the dev server / redeploy. |
| Adding to the cart shows "That record already exists." (`23505` on the active-cart index) or "Unable to start a cart" | Cart lookup / insert bugs, fixed 2026-09-26, three layers: (1) **root cause**: `services/cart.ts#openCart` built its DB context without the signed-in customer's id, so once a cart was linked to a customer RLS hid it from that very lookup (`cart_is_owned` needs `app.current_customer_id()`), the code saw "no cart", inserted, and hit the unique index. `openCart` now uses `cartContext(restaurant.id, token, customerId)` like every other cart call; any new cart read/write must carry the customer id. (2) `repositories/carts.ts#getOrCreateCart` inserts with `on conflict do nothing` and the loser takes the winner's cart (two overlapping adds). (3) A token held by a cart this visitor cannot read (another account's after a sign-out / account switch, or another restaurant's) raises `CART_TOKEN_TAKEN`; `openCart` starts a fresh cart under a new token and `web/storefront.ts#openStorefrontCart` stores it in the `rp_cart` cookie. `clearCustomerSession` (sign-out) also clears `rp_cart` and `rp_cart_n`. Migration `0023_cart_token_per_restaurant.sql` makes the active-cart unique index per `(restaurant_id, session_token)` (0010 claimed per-restaurant but indexed the token alone), so one browser can hold a cart at two restaurants (the cookie is shared across restaurants on a host, e.g. localhost). Tests: `tests/cart-race.test.ts`, `tests/cart-open.test.ts`. |
| `next build` fails with `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length') at WasmHash._updateWithBuffer` | Corrupted **persistent webpack cache** (`.next/cache/webpack`), not a code error: confirmed 2026-09-26, the same build failed with the old cache and passed after clearing only that folder; a rebuild on an unchanged fresh cache is fine, so it breaks when sources change between builds (Next 15 + Windows + Node 24). **Fixed permanently:** `package.json` `prebuild` runs `scripts/clear-webpack-cache.mjs`, so every `npm run build` starts with a clean webpack cache (honours `NEXT_DIST_DIR`; the rest of `.next` is kept). Calling `next build` directly skips the npm hook: clear `.next/cache/webpack` by hand if it recurs there. |
| A page shows "Something went wrong" and the browser console has `ChunkLoadError: Loading chunk … failed` (400 on `/_next/static/chunks/...`) | Not a code bug: `.next` was rebuilt (`next build`, `rm -rf .next`) while a `next start` server was running from it; the server still serves the old manifest but the old chunks are gone. Restart the server (`npm run start`). Never rebuild `.next` under a running production server; build elsewhere or stop it first (seen 2026-09-26 on `/account/sign-up`). |
| `permission denied for schema auth` (staff sign-in, or any admin page load right after signing in) | Hosted Supabase blocking `auth` schema access for `app_service` and/or `app_runtime`, see section 6. Both fixed (migrations `0017`–`0019`; `signInStaff` also no longer writes `auth.users`). Customer sign-up and staff creation still write `auth.users` and are not fixed. If this recurs after a fresh Supabase project, re-run `0018`/`0019` (their `grant service_role to app_service/app_runtime` — verify with `select has_schema_privilege('app_runtime','auth','USAGE')`). |
| Need a new `/admin` login, or to reset one, but there is no staff-management UI yet | `npm run db:create-staff -- --email <email> --name "<name>" --role <owner\|admin\|manager\|staff> --password <password> [--restaurant <slug, default bella-napoli>]` (`scripts/db/create-staff.ts`). Runs as `DATABASE_URL_MIGRATOR` (owner), which is required — the app's own `createTeamMember` path writes `auth.users` through `app_service` and is blocked on hosted Supabase (section 6/16), same reason this had to be a standalone script instead of an admin action. Upserts by `(restaurant_id, email)`, so re-running it for an existing email resets that person's password. |
| `npm run db:migrate` throws `0001_extensions_auth_context.sql was modified after being applied` | Found 2026-09-22, unrelated to any code change: the file's current checksum (CRLF line endings on disk) does not match what was recorded when it was applied (2026-09-19) — `git diff` shows no difference from HEAD, so this is a stored-checksum/line-ending drift, not an edited migration. Blocks the runner for *every* migration, not just new ones, until the stored checksum in `schema_migrations` is reconciled with the current file (or the file's original line endings are restored) — not fixed yet; new migrations in this session were applied by hand (see `0017`/`0018`) instead of through `npm run db:migrate`. |
| Staff sign-in fails "Invalid email or password" even though the password is right | Check `select tm.email, tm.user_id from team_members tm where tm.email = '...'` — if `user_id` is `null`, the row is orphaned from `auth.users` (see the incident below). Fix: `update team_members tm set user_id = u.id from auth.users u where lower(u.email) = lower(tm.email) and tm.user_id is distinct from u.id;` |
| Placing an order as a **signed-in customer** fails `A referenced record is missing or still in use.` (Postgres `23503`, `order_status_history_changed_by_fkey`) | Fixed 2026-09-26. Since `0021` a customer's `userId` is its `customers.id`, and `createOrder` put it into the DB session as `app.current_user_id` (`auth.uid()`); the insert trigger `app.record_order_status` writes that into `order_status_history.changed_by`, a FK to `auth.users` (staff only). Guests never hit it (no `userId`), so it surfaced once ordering required sign-in. **Rule: a customer is identified to the database by `customer_id` (and the cart token) only; `app.current_user_id` / `RequestContext.userId` in a DB session is for staff.** `createOrder`, `createReservation` and the customer order reads now open their session with `userId: null` (`input.userId` still only decides `is_guest`). Guarded by `tests/customer-db-identity.test.ts`. Reproduced/verified with a rolled-back insert: customer id as `request.jwt.claims.sub` → 23503, no sub → OK. |
| Admin write fails `A referenced record is missing or still in use.` (Postgres `23503`) on `order_status_history`/`customers`/`deliveries`/`media`/`reviews` | Same incident as above, different table: the FK on `changed_by`/`user_id`/`driver_user_id`/`uploaded_by`/`responded_by` was repointed at `public.users` instead of `auth.users`. Re-run the FK-scan query in the incident note; `0022_restore_remaining_auth_users_fks.sql` fixed the five known at the time — a new one showing up means another column got repointed the same way. |
| `insert into auth.users` fails with `null value in column "id" ... violates not-null constraint` | `auth.users.id` has **no column default** on this database (found 2026-09-22, part of the same incident) — every insert must generate it explicitly: `insert into auth.users (id, email, ...) values (gen_random_uuid(), $1, ...)`. Already fixed in `scripts/db/create-staff.ts`, `repositories/team.ts#createTeamMember`, `auth-service.ts#createCustomerAccount`; `scripts/db/seed.ts` was already explicit. Check any *new* insert into `auth.users` does the same. |

- **Opening hours / "open 24 hours" (2026-09-26):** a window with `open === close` is the 24h marker (`isWithinWindow`, `serviceStatus`, reservation slots all honour it). `parseOpeningHours` also normalises `00:00–23:59` (how the DB is usually typed) to that marker; without it the storefront showed "Closing soon" from 23:14 and closed for the 23:59 minute. `formatHours` prints "Open 24 hours"; `seo.ts` emits schema.org `00:00`–`23:59`. To make a location 24h for testing, set each day in `restaurant1s.hours` to `[{"open":"00:00","close":"00:00"}]` (or `23:59`), then restart the dev server (section 15 snapshot cache). Bella Napoli's two locations are set to 24h in the hosted DB for testing.

## 12. Commands and tests

`npm run dev` · `npm run build` · `npm run typecheck` · `npm test` (vitest; DB suites need Postgres) · `npm run db:migrate|db:seed|db:reset|db:verify|create-staff` (section 11) · `npm run notifications:dispatch [-- --loop]` · `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright).

**Verifying while the user's own server runs:** `next build` / `next dev` write `.next`, and rebuilding it under a running `next start` breaks that server (ChunkLoadError, section 11). `next.config.ts` reads `distDir` from `NEXT_DIST_DIR` (default `.next`), so run checks with `NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify next build` / `next dev -p 3100` (gitignored `.next-*/`) and delete the folder afterwards. `tsconfig.json` excludes `.next-*`, so a leftover folder can no longer break `npm run build`'s type check (section 11).

DB-free verification: `npm run typecheck`, then `npx vitest run tests/architecture.test.ts tests/config.test.ts tests/database-registry.test.ts tests/pricing.test.ts tests/notifications.test.ts tests/notifications-concurrency.test.ts tests/notifications-dispatch-control.test.ts tests/order-events.test.ts tests/reservation-notifications.test.ts tests/my-orders.test.ts tests/push-service-worker.test.ts tests/tray.test.ts tests/local-cart.test.ts tests/menu-selection.test.ts tests/create-order-sql.test.ts tests/customer-session.test.ts tests/customer-profile.test.ts tests/order-access.test.ts tests/storefront-services.test.ts tests/geocoding.test.ts`, `npx next build`, then `next start` and curl the `/*` GET routes. After structural changes always run `npm run typecheck` and `npx vitest run tests/architecture.test.ts`.

## 13. Future: moving the backend out, one database per restaurant

**Moving the backend out.** `src/server` and `src/shared` import no framework code (the architecture test fails otherwise). To run them as a standalone service: (1) move both into the backend package (keep the `@/server` / `@/shared` aliases); (2) add an HTTP layer (Fastify/Hono/Express or route handlers): each server action becomes a route `schema.parse(body)` → the same service function → `jsonError()`; (3) replace `web/session.ts#getVisitorContext` with middleware that builds the same `RequestContext` from the cookie/Authorization header (`server/auth/tokens.ts` already signs/verifies JWTs; share `AUTH_SECRET`); (4) in the frontend replace `openStorefrontCart`, `getVisitorContext` and the service calls in pages with an API client (components and `shared/` untouched; `revalidatePath` stays in the frontend). Expose the same SSE `/events` endpoint.

**One database per restaurant (not implemented).** `SingleDatabaseDirectory` answers every question with the one configured database. The seam is complete: repositories call `getDb({ restaurantId })`, the registry asks a `DatabaseDirectory` which `DatabaseConfig` holds that restaurant, and `DatabaseManager` keeps one `Database` per config `key`. To enable: (1) store the mapping centrally (JSON file or a `restaurant_databases` table: `restaurantId, slug, key, runtimeUrlRef, serviceUrlRef`; only *references* to env/secret names); (2) implement `DatabaseDirectory` (e.g. `RegistryDirectory`) and construct it in `manager()` in `registry.ts` (`tests/database-registry.test.ts` shows the contract); (3) resolve slug → restaurant first (`getRestaurantBySlug`, `listPublicRestaurants`, sign-in flows call `getDb()` with no restaurant); (4) close gaps: repository functions that look rows up by id only (`getCartById`, `getLocationById`, `updateMenuItem`, …) rely on `ctx.restaurantId` (`grep -rn "getDb(ctx)" src/server/repositories`), make the directory throw on `null` for tenant repositories in dev/test; (5) migrations and seeding run per database; (6) auth needs nothing (sessions carry `restaurantId`); (7) run one notification dispatch and one LISTEN per database.

## 14. Known gaps (do not rediscover)

- `createOrder`, `createReservation`, `resolveItemSelection` are SQL + rules transaction scripts inside repositories (splitting needs the DB suites and a local database; do it as its own change).
- `auth-service` queries `auth.users` directly for **staff** (`signInStaff`; writes there are impossible for any runtime role — confirmed independently on both branches, see the incident note in section 6 and DECISIONS.md §26 — so `repositories/team.ts`'s `createTeamMember`/password-set and staff creation only work via `scripts/db/create-staff.ts`, the migrator connection, never a live request) and `customers` directly for **customer** login (since 0021, see section 16 — this table is fully app-owned, so signup/Google/password-set all work normally through `app_service`, no such restriction).
- Comments in applied migrations mention old `src/lib/...` paths and `restaurant_locations`; migrations are checksummed and were not edited.
- ID-only repository lookups use `getDb(ctx)` and rely on `ctx.restaurantId` being set by the caller.
- ~~Media URLs use `/api/media/...` but no route handler exists.~~ Fixed 2026-10-08: `app/api/media/[...path]/route.ts` serves `.uploads/` (images only, no `../` escape; `tests/media-route.test.ts`) and `web/media.ts#hasPublicImage` accepts `/api/media/` URLs. Only used when Supabase Storage is not configured.
- Customer sign-in/registration UI (built 2026-09-22, see section 16): `/account/{sign-in,sign-up,google-phone}`, Google OAuth, email verification, all working end to end on the current `customers`-owns-its-login design (0021).
- Admin UI (`/admin`, section 17) covers everything except staff management (deliberately excluded so far) and website/CMS — statuses/rows for those still change via SQL, so delivery for anything not yet in `/admin` needs the dispatcher script (dev) or a scheduler (prod).
- No `vercel.json` yet (cron not configured).
- FCM has no server-side idempotency (mitigated by the notification `tag`).
- No ESLint config; `tsc` + the architecture test are the guardrails.
- `npm run db:verify`'s "application roles cannot read the migration table" check fails (`app_runtime` currently has SELECT on `schema_migrations`) — predates the `users` rework, not caused by it; nothing in the codebase grants this explicitly so it likely comes from a Supabase default. Low priority (that table has no sensitive data, just migration filenames/checksums) but worth a `revoke select on schema_migrations from app_runtime` migration at some point.
- Google sign-in's **error** path (`account/google/callback/route.ts`, e.g. an expired/invalid `state`, or `completeGoogleAuth` throwing) always redirects to the standalone `/account/sign-in?error=google` page, not back to `returnTo` — unlike the success path (section 16's "Continue with Google returns to wherever it was clicked" note), which does. Low priority now that sign-in is a standalone page again rather than a dialog (section 16), but still an inconsistency worth fixing together with a real error affordance on that page if anyone revisits this.

## 15. Storefront cache (in-memory snapshot, one instance = one restaurant)

Decision record: `DECISIONS.md` §19.

```
instrumentation.ts ─ register() ─► startStorefrontCache() ─► loadStorefrontSnapshot()
                                                               └► repositories/storefront.loadStorefrontData()
storefront GET ─► web/storefront ─► services/{storefront,catalog,restaurants,reviews}
                                       └► snapshotForRestaurant()/snapshotForSlug() ─► memory (0 SQL)
carts, checkout, orders, payments, reservations, customers, write paths ─► services ─► repositories ─► db
```

- At startup `instrumentation.ts` loads a frozen snapshot of the public storefront (restaurant, website, pages, locations, delivery zones, active menu with variants/add-ons, top-50 reviews + summary) for `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_RESTAURANT`. Storefront GETs then send **no SQL**. Refresh every `STOREFRONT_CACHE_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS` (atomic swap, single-flight, chained timer; the old snapshot is kept when a refresh fails or times out). A failed first load fails startup: production exits non-zero, dev keeps the server up and requests error until the cause is fixed. `STOREFRONT_CACHE_ENABLED=false` restores per-request database reads.
- The cache lives on `globalThis` (Next bundles the startup hook and routes separately; dev reloads re-evaluate modules). No SQL in `server/cache` (architecture test).
- New public read-mostly data: add it to `repositories/storefront.ts` → `cache/types.ts` + `storefront-snapshot.ts` → a `read*` function in `storefront-queries.ts` → the service (cache branch + DB branch). Anything transactional or price-deciding stays off the snapshot.
- The snapshot prices the tray PREVIEW (cart page, checkout page: menu via `catalog#getOrderableMenuItems`, zones via `restaurants#getDeliveryZones`, promo codes via `coupons#findPreviewCoupon` — active coupon definitions are in the snapshot since 2026-09-28 and coupon admin writes invalidate it). It never decides what is CHARGED: `createOrder` re-reads restaurant, customer, menu, zones and coupon (with usage limits) inside its own transaction. `server/cache` must still not be imported by cart/checkout/pricing directly (architecture test) — go through the catalog/coupon/restaurant services.
- Snapshot objects are frozen and shared: never mutate what a service returns; copy first.
- After an admin write call `getStorefrontCache().invalidate()`; `server/services/menu-admin.ts` (section 16) does this on every category/item/variant/add-on write.
- One restaurant per instance: since section 37 no URL names a restaurant, so there is nothing else to serve.
- The startup hook is the one place outside `server/config` allowed to read `process.env.NEXT_RUNTIME` (Next replaces the literal at compile time so the Edge bundle does not pull in `pg`).

**Still database-bound on purpose:** `/reservation` (one range query for availability, side by side with the signed-in customer's profile read), `/checkout` (one read: the account's profile + addresses + verification, one transaction), placing the order (one transaction), sign-in (one read), the layout's active-orders widget for a signed-in visitor (one `count(*)`, `getActiveOrderCount`), order tracking. The tray (cart) and the signed-in header state need no database at all.

**Speed facts (hosted Supabase pooler, ~0.3 s per round trip, so one DB transaction of 5 statements is ~1.5 s):** `next start` serves cached storefront pages in ~25–150 ms with 0 SQL; `next dev` compiles each route on its first visit (4–12 s) and renders unoptimised afterwards (0.3–1.8 s), so judge speed on a production build. The snapshot load takes ~7–17 s at start, before the server answers. The cache is per process: on serverless hosts every cold start repeats the load.

**Observing SQL without editing code:** run the server with `NODE_OPTIONS="--require <spy.cjs>"` where the spy patches `pg` `Client.prototype.query` through `Module._load` (match any module exposing `Pool` and `Client`, not only the request string `"pg"`) and logs each statement with its duration. Each Next dev recompile spawns a worker that prints its own "installed" line; exclude it when counting statements.

**If startup fails with `[cache] storefront cache initialization failed`:** the log line carries the database error. `relation "…" does not exist` (code 42P01) means the code and the database schema disagree (for example a migration applied without the matching code, or the reverse): compare `schema_migrations` with `db/migrations/` and check the table names.

## 16. Customer accounts (sign-in, Google, email verification)

**Status (2026-09-22): built and applied, wired into the header/UI.** Sign-in/sign-up/Google/email-verification work end to end from their own pages (`/account/...`), the checkout gate is live, migration `0018` is applied, and phase 2 (header auth state, cart icon, floating current-orders widget, reorder) is also done — see section 8's "My Orders vs Current Orders" for that half.

**The session identifies the customer; page views never ask the database (2026-09-28).** The customer JWT (`rp_customer_session`, HS256 with `AUTH_SECRET`) carries `customerId`, `restaurantId`, `name` and now `emailVerified`. `auth-service.ts#resolveCustomer` is synchronous and reads only those claims — it used to run `getCustomerById` (a full transaction) and `getStorefrontCustomer` was not request-cached, so a signed-in page view paid ~4 customer lookups (layout summary, visitor context, page). Consequences: a claim can be stale, so (1) `verifyEmailCodeAction` and `updateProfileAction` re-sign the cookie (`web/session.ts#refreshCustomerSession`); (2) anything that ACTS on the account still reads the row (profile, addresses, codes), and placing an order locks and re-checks it inside the order transaction (`createOrder`: row must exist at this restaurant, `is_email_verified` when `requireVerifiedEmail`); (3) a token signed before the claim existed resolves `emailVerified: null` (unknown) — `isSignedInWithVerifiedEmail` then does one read, the checkout page reads it with the profile anyway. A deleted account keeps a working header until the cookie expires or they sign out, but can do nothing with it. **Sign-in** (`signInAction`) = restaurant from the snapshot + ONE read (`id, password_hash, full_name, is_email_verified`) + scrypt; it used to be five transactions. **Sign-up** sends its verification email in `after()` (after the response). `assertCanPlaceOrder` is now sync and only checks the session (SIGN_IN_REQUIRED); EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED comes from the order transaction. Tests: `tests/customer-session.test.ts` (one transaction per sign-in, claims round-trip, no-DB resolve), `tests/order-access.test.ts`.

**OTP verify + where a customer lands after signing in (2026-09-29).** (1) Checking a code is ONE statement in ONE transaction: `repositories/email-verification.ts#attemptVerificationCode` (CTE: find the active code `for update`, count the attempt, consume it when the hash matches, `update customers set is_email_verified = true`) returning `expired | locked | wrong | verified`; `services/customer-auth.ts#verifyEmailCode` maps those to the same messages as before. It was four transactions (~12 pooler round trips, several seconds) and racy; `recordVerificationAttempt`, `consumeVerificationCode`, `markCustomerEmailVerified`, `isEmailVerified` are gone. Tested for real in a rolled-back transaction on the dev DB (~260 ms) and DB-free in `tests/email-verification.test.ts`. (2) **No `router.refresh()` after a cookie-setting action.** Next 15 answers any server action that sets a cookie with the current route freshly rendered and clears the client router cache (`x-action-revalidated`, `pathWasRevalidated` in `action-handler.js`), so the header already shows the signed-in state. The verify flow used to render three times (VerifyEmailForm refresh, then push + refresh) before the next screen appeared. Sign-in, sign-up, verify, and Google-phone now do one `router.replace(dest)` (replace, so Back doesn't reopen the sign-in form) and prefetch `dest` on mount. `VerifyEmailForm` keeps its button disabled ("Verified") while the caller navigates. (3) **`signInAction` sends the code itself** (`after()` → `ensureVerificationCode`) when the account is unverified. The form no longer calls `ensureVerificationCodeAction` (which was a second request plus an account read). (4) **Landing = `shared/return-to.ts#afterAuthPath(returnTo)`**: the page they came from, else the storefront home `/`, **never `/account`** (that screen is gone since master's 2026-09-28 change: `/account` redirects home, the profile drawer holds profile/addresses/sign-out, and the dock shows "Orders" when signed in). The header pill and mobile-dock sign-in links carry `?returnTo=<current path>` (`signInHref(slug, pathname)`; the dock takes `signedIn`). Checkout already sends `returnTo=/checkout`, so a customer who had to sign in from checkout (including the verify step) goes straight back to place the order. The Google callback and `GooglePhoneForm` use the same rule. The code hint now says the code expires in 10 minutes, which matches `CODE_TTL_MINUTES`; it used to say 60 seconds, which is really the resend cooldown.

**Forgot password (2026-09-29).** A "Forgot password?" link next to the Password label in `SignInForm` swaps the form for `components/storefront/forgot-password-form.tsx`. Because it lives inside `SignInForm`, it works on `/account/sign-in` and in the checkout/reservation `AuthDialog` alike. It has three steps:
1. **Email:** `requestPasswordResetAction` → `services/customer-auth.ts#requestPasswordReset`. It gives the same answer whether or not an account has that email, so no account enumeration. It is rate limited per caller (`password-reset-request-ip`, 5 per 15 minutes) before the lookup, so unknown emails count too. For an existing account it calls the existing `sendVerificationCode(..., "reset")`, which uses the same code table, 10-minute TTL, per-customer limits and Resend provider. The email template takes `purpose: "verify" | "reset"` for its wording.
2. **Code:** `verifyPasswordResetCodeAction` → `verifyPasswordResetCode` → the existing one-transaction `attemptVerificationCode` (expired, locked after 5 attempts, or wrong; same messages; resend has a 60-second client cooldown). An unknown email gets the same "That code is not correct." as a wrong code. Success returns a 15-minute **reset token** (`auth/tokens.ts#signPasswordResetToken`, purpose `password-reset`). It carries no `sub`/`customerId`, so it can never pass as a session.
3. **New password + confirm:** `resetPasswordAction` → `completePasswordReset` → `auth-service.ts#resetCustomerPassword`. That is one transaction: lock the row, check that the token's `ph` (sha256 fingerprint of the current `password_hash`) still matches, write the new scrypt hash, and set `is_email_verified = true`, because the code proved inbox ownership. It then sets the session cookie and the form calls the sign-in form's `finish()`, which lands on `returnTo` or home. The fingerprint makes the token **single-use** without a new table: after any password change it no longer matches.

There is no migration. `email_verification_codes` has no `purpose` column, so reset and verify codes share it. A code only proves inbox ownership either way, so they are interchangeable. Add a column only if the two ever need different lifetimes or limits. Google-only accounts (no `password_hash`) can use this to add a password. Known limit: existing sessions on other devices are not signed out (JWT sessions cannot be revoked without a denylist). Tests: `tests/password-reset.test.ts` (DB-free: enumeration-safe request, rate limit, code outcomes, token is not a session, single use, cross-restaurant token refused, schema rules, email wording). The same change fixed the verification email's HTML saying "expires in 60 seconds" (the text part and the real TTL are 10 minutes).
**Manual end-to-end** (needs a real inbox): `/account/sign-in` → Forgot password? → your account's email → code from the email → new password twice → you land signed in. Sign out and sign in with the new password; the old one must fail. Re-submitting the same reset step fails with "already been used".

**Customer login lives on `customers` itself — there is no separate customer identity table (as of `0021_customers_own_login.sql`, 2026-09-23).** `customers` gained `password_hash`, `is_email_verified boolean`, `google_sub`, `auth_provider default 'password'`, `last_sign_in_at`. A customer's login was always effectively restaurant-scoped (a `customers` row already only exists at the restaurant they ordered from), so putting the login fields on the row itself instead of a shared identity table needs no cross-restaurant lookup step. Two partial-unique indexes enforce one account per email/Google-sub per restaurant among non-guest rows: `customers_restaurant_email_key` (`restaurant_id, lower(email)) where not is_guest and email is not null`) and `customers_restaurant_google_sub_key` (`unique (restaurant_id, google_sub)` — NULLs don't collide, so guests/no-Google rows are unaffected). `repositories/customers.ts` has the auth-facing functions: `getCustomerByGoogleSubOrEmail` (privileged bootstrap, matches by either), `linkGoogleToCustomer`, `createGoogleCustomer`, `markCustomerEmailVerified` — plus the ordinary `getCustomerById`/`upsertCustomer`. `email_verification_codes.customer_id` (renamed from `user_id`) FKs straight to `customers(id)` now. There is no `User` type or `Customer.userId` field any more — `Customer` (`shared/contract/models.ts`) carries `emailVerified`/`authProvider` directly.

**Why this changed twice in two days, and why auth.users is staff-only again:** `auth.users` (Supabase's shim, 0001) can never be granted real INSERT/UPDATE for `app_service`/`app_runtime` — confirmed independently on **two different branches** sharing this hosted dev DB (`GRANT ...` on it runs without error but silently grants nothing; only `supabase_auth_admin` can actually write it). The first fix (2026-09-22 → 23, `0019_replace_users_table.sql`/`0020_merge_user_auth_into_users.sql`, now superseded) replaced `auth.users` entirely with an app-owned `users` table shared by staff and customers alike. That collided with a second, independent fix on a parallel admin-portal branch (`0017_grant_app_service_auth_users.sql`/`0018_app_service_auth_schema_usage.sql`/`0019_app_runtime_auth_schema_usage.sql` — different files, note the numbering collision since both branches picked "0017-0019" independently) which kept staff on `auth.users` and tried granting `service_role` membership instead — also confirmed not to actually enable INSERT/UPDATE (same "no privileges were granted" wall), but that branch's admin/staff code (`auth-service.ts`, `team.ts`, `scripts/db/create-staff.ts`) was built assuming `auth.users`, and running both branches' migrations against the one shared DB left it in a mixed state (the `users`-table FKs got silently reverted to `auth.users` and `users` emptied by whichever branch's script ran last — there is no real "merge" between two schema designs solving the same problem differently). **Resolution, at the requester's explicit direction:** stop needing a shared identity table for customers at all (this section) — `auth.users` goes back to being staff/admin-only, exactly as the other branch already has it, untouched by anything here; `scripts/db/create-staff.ts` (that branch) still creates staff via the migrator connection, never a live request. The two systems don't share a table any more, so they can't collide on one again. See DECISIONS.md §26 for the full incident writeup.

**Migration `0018_customer_auth.sql`:** the surviving piece is `email_verification_codes` (RLS on, no `app_runtime` policy, `app_service` only — same pattern as `notification_events`); verifying a code sets `customers.is_email_verified`.

**Config:** `googleAuth` section (`GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`) — same optional-until-configured pattern as `push`/`email`; `config.googleAuth` is `null` and "Continue with Google" is hidden on every page until both are set. No Google SDK dependency: `server/integrations/google.ts` does the OAuth2 authorization-code flow by hand and verifies the returned `id_token` with `jose` (already a dependency) against Google's published JWKS.

**Email verification:** `server/services/customer-auth.ts` (`sendVerificationCode`, `ensureVerificationCode` — only sends when no still-active code exists, `verifyEmailCode`). A 6-digit code, HMAC-SHA256-hashed with `AUTH_SECRET` (not scrypt — codes are short-lived, rate-limited, and don't need scrypt's cost), 10-minute expiry, 5 max attempts, sent through `getEmailProvider()` (`services/notifications.ts` — the same Resend provider the outbox uses, but called synchronously since a customer is waiting on screen, not through `notification_events`). Enforced inside `createOrder` (`requireVerifiedEmail`) for every order, guest or account — see "Guest checkout" below for how a guest proves it without signing in. `checkout-form.tsx` turns the `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED` code into the inline `<VerifyEmailForm>` step for a signed-in-but-unverified customer, or the `GuestVerifyDialog` modal for a guest.

**Guest checkout with an OTP modal, account creation optional (2026-10-10, supersedes "Ordering requires a signed-in, verified customer" below — reverted at the requester's direction: the mandatory-sign-in redirect hurt conversion).** `/checkout` no longer redirects a guest to sign-in (`checkout/page.tsx` drops the `if (!customer) redirect(...)`; `LocalCartPanel`'s "Checkout" button no longer does either). A guest fills the same fullName/phone/email fields the form always had; clicking "Place order" opens `components/storefront/guest-verify-dialog.tsx` (modeled on `auth-dialog.tsx`: Radix `Dialog`, `animate-overlay`/`animate-dialog`) instead of submitting:
- **Phone- and email-exists check, as you type** (500 ms debounce in `checkout-form.tsx`; the guest email input is controlled for this): `checkGuestContactAction({ phone?, email? })` → `customer-auth.ts#checkGuestContact` (rate limited `guest-contact-check-ip`, 40 / 15 min — it reveals whether an account exists, which the requester asked for, so it must not become an account-sweeping endpoint) → `isPhoneTaken` (`repositories/customers.ts#findAccountByPhone`, privileged bootstrap lookup) and `isEmailTaken` (the existing `getCustomerByEmail`, which already skips guest rows). A real account's phone/email shows "… already has an account. Please sign in to use it." under the field and blocks Place order locally (no OTP sent). Not just UX: `upsertCustomer`'s `on conflict (restaurant_id, phone) do update` has no "only if guest" guard, so guest checkout calling it with a stranger's phone would otherwise overwrite that account's name/email. Every guest action (`sendGuestVerificationCode`/`resendGuestVerificationCode`/`verifyGuestCode`, via `upsertGuestContact`) re-checks both server-side before touching the row (`PHONE_HAS_ACCOUNT`/`EMAIL_HAS_ACCOUNT`).
- **"Already have an account? Sign in"** sits under Place order for guests (desktop summary card and the phones' sticky bar, which is why the form's bottom padding is `pb-36` for guests vs `pb-28`). It links to `signInHref(slug, "/checkout")`, so signing in lands back on checkout with the cart intact.
- **Modal positioning/theme (fixed 2026-10-10; the first version sat above and left of centre and looked generic).** Two causes: (1) it combined Tailwind v4's `-translate-x-1/2 -translate-y-1/2` (the CSS `translate` property) with `animate-dialog`, whose keyframes ALSO set `transform: translate(-50%,-50%)` (fill-mode `both`) — the two compose, so the box moved -100% of its size. `animate-dialog` is only for `confirm-dialog.tsx`'s pattern (`left-1/2 top-1/2` + the keyframe, no translate utilities); never add translate utilities next to it. (2) `Dialog.Portal` had no container, so it rendered under `<body>`, outside `.theme-root` (where the restaurant's DB theme variables live). Fonts and radius fell back to defaults. Now: portalled into `.theme-root` like `Sheet`; the Overlay is the fixed, full-viewport scroll container that flex-centres the panel (`min-h-full items-center`), so a panel taller than the viewport (phone keyboard, short screens) scrolls instead of clipping (Radix's RemoveScroll sits on the Overlay, so it may scroll while the page cannot); motion/react enter + exit (opacity only under reduced motion); only theme tokens (`--color-surface`, `--color-hairline`, `--radius-panel`, `--shadow-raised`, `--font-display`, `--brand-tint`, scrim from `--color-ink`). Behaviour: backdrop taps never dismiss (would lose an OTP/password); Escape closes unless a password is typed or a request is running; focus starts on each step's first field and returns to the trigger as soon as it closes (Radix only restores on Content unmount, which the exit animation delays until focus has fallen to `<body>`); the step resets on every open (the parent closes it after `onDone`, which never calls `onOpenChange`). Password step: persistent labels, show/hide toggles (`aria-pressed`), live "at least 8 characters" hint, mismatch shown after the confirm field blurs, reserved message lines (no layout shift), loading label, Back.
- **A guest sees their own order (fixed 2026-10-10; found when a guest's order page said "We cannot show this order").** Guest order access used to be the RLS guest policy (0008: `orders.cart_id → carts.session_token = app.cart_token()`), but the tray moved to a browser cookie with no database cart, and `getVisitorContext` always returns `cartToken: null`. So a guest had no proof of ownership at all, which only surfaced once guests could order. The order page, live tracking (`/events`), "Current orders", the header/dock count and the floating tracker were all affected. Fix, no migration: the existing signed order-access token (`auth/tokens.ts`, the email-link `?t=`, 60 days, one order, never a session) gained an optional `orderNumber` claim (`onum`; email-link tokens without it still verify). `placeOrder` takes `hooks.rememberGuestOrder`; for a visitor with no `customerId` it signs a grant right after the order commits (before the online-payment step that can throw, so a failed payment start still leaves a viewable order), and `placeOrderAction` passes `web/session.ts#rememberGuestOrder`, which keeps the last 5 in the httpOnly cookie `rp_guest_orders` (`~`-joined, newest first). The token is never in the action's response or the URL. Readers: `getGuestOrderGrants(restaurantId)` (signature checks only, request-cached; junk or forged entries are dropped); `getVisitorContext` adds `guestOrderIds` for a guest (`RequestContext.guestOrderIds`); `guestOrderAccessToken(restaurantId, orderNumber)` supplies the token where a page takes `?t=` (order page, `/events`; the page passes it on to live updates, cancel and reorder). `repositories/orders.ts` `countActiveVisitorOrders`/`listVisitorOrders` have a guest branch by those ids, a privileged read (the 0008 policy only knows the cart token) pinned to `restaurant_id`, with uuid-shaped ids capped at 10. Another browser still sees nothing without the email link. An account created in the OTP modal is signed in before the order, so it gets no guest grant. Verified in a browser with a grant for a real order: page, Current orders and widget show it, while a browser without the grant and a forged grant are refused. Tests: `tests/order-access.test.ts` (grant with/without number, never a session, tamper refused).
- **Checkout order-summary card no longer hangs below the screen (fixed 2026-10-10).** The desktop `<aside>` is `lg:sticky lg:top-[calc(var(--header-h)+1.5rem)]` with no height limit; once the card grew taller than the space under the header (the guest "Sign in" line pushed it over on ~860px windows), sticky pinned its top and its bottom stayed off-screen until the page ended. Now the card is capped at `lg:max-h-[calc(100dvh-var(--header-h,4.5rem)-3rem)]` (`--header-h` is published by `site-header.tsx`) as a flex column. The item list (already its own scroll area) shrinks first, down to one item (`lg:min-h-[4.5rem]`). Every other child is `shrink-0` (`lg:[&>*:not(ul)]:shrink-0`); without that, flex shrank the `h-14` Place order button to a sliver. The card body only scrolls as a last resort on very short windows (below ~650px tall). Verified 1280×620/720, 1366×768, 1440×900, 1280×1300: card bottom on screen when stuck, Place order 56px, totals + sign-in line visible without inner scroll from 720px up.
- **Verified in a real browser 2026-10-10** (Playwright + system Chrome against `next dev` on 3100/3101 with `NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify[-b]`, one process per restaurant since `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_RESTAURANT` is baked in): Zaytoun (`#2F5D46`, Playfair) and Bun Intended (`#5B2EFF`, Poppins) at 1280×800 and 390×844, plus reduced motion and a 390×420 viewport: centred to ±0 px, backdrop = viewport with blur, above the header, scroll locked/restored, focus trapped/returned, every step and error state; and the real `/checkout` as a guest (seeded `rp_tray_<slug>` cookie): no redirect, sign-in link and returnTo, phone/email-exists messages, blocked submit sends no action. Server actions other than the read-only contact check were intercepted (no DB writes). `bella-napoli` does not exist on the hosted DB. A temporary harness route was used and deleted.
- **OTP step**: `VerifyEmailForm` gained optional `sendAction`/`verifyAction` props (default to the existing session-based actions — every other caller, sign-in/sign-up/checkout's signed-in-unverified step, is unchanged) so the same component works with no session at all. The guest modal binds them to `resendGuestVerificationCodeAction`/`verifyGuestCodeAction`, which upsert the guest row by phone (`isGuest: true`) and call the same `sendVerificationCode`/`verifyEmailCode` core functions a signed-in customer's verification uses. Verifying sets `customers.is_email_verified = true` on that phone-keyed row; since `createOrder`'s guest branch (`repositories/orders.ts`) upserts by the **same phone** when the order is actually placed, it resolves the identical row and finds it already verified — no `guestCustomerId` has to round-trip through the client payload at all. `services/checkout.ts#placeOrder` now passes `requireVerifiedEmail: true` unconditionally (was `Boolean(visitor.customerId)`); `createOrder`'s guest (`else`) branch gained one guard clause mirroring the existing signed-in-account branch: `if (input.requireVerifiedEmail && !customer.emailVerified) throw EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED`.
- **"Save your details?" step** (shown once, right after the code verifies): "No" places the order as a guest, same as always. "Yes" shows a password field in the same modal (no redirect) → `registerAsAccountAction` → `signUpCustomer` (`customer-auth.ts`, wraps `auth-service.ts#createCustomerAccount` — the exact function normal sign-up already uses, which already upgrades a same-phone guest row via `on conflict ... where customers.is_guest` instead of erroring, since the row's email already matches what the OTP step upserted) → `setCustomerSession` signs them in immediately → `refreshCustomerSession({ emailVerified: true })` corrects the fresh session's claim (`createCustomerAccount` hardcodes `emailVerified: false`, correct for an ordinary signup, wrong here since the OTP already proved it — the DB row itself is untouched by the upgrade, already `true`). Either branch then auto-submits the checkout payload the modal's caller kept in a ref — nothing is re-typed, and closing the modal without choosing loses nothing (all client state, untouched).
- `assertCanPlaceOrder` (the old unconditional `SIGN_IN_REQUIRED` gate) is deleted — its only caller was `placeOrderAction`, and the `requireVerifiedEmail` check inside the transaction is now the single real gate for every order, guest or account.
- Tests: `tests/order-access.test.ts` keeps only `safeReturnTo`/`signInHref` (the `assertCanPlaceOrder` describe block is gone with the function). No DB-free test yet for the new guest actions — would need the local Postgres (section "Local Postgres" below) since they go through real upsert/verification SQL; `tests/customer-account-takeover.test.ts` already covers the underlying "guest row upgraded vs. real account refused" SQL behavior in `createCustomerAccount` (unchanged by this work).

**Fulfillment (Delivery/Pickup/Dine-in) picker removed from Menu and Cart, kept on Checkout (2026-10-10).** The toggle that let a customer switch order type on the Menu hero (`menu/page.tsx`, the `<Link>` tab group keyed by `?orderType=`) and on `LocalCartPanel` (drawer + full `/cart` page, `orderTypePicker`) is gone — both pages no longer render it. `checkout-form.tsx`'s own Step 1 picker (`orderTypeState`) is untouched and is the only place order type is chosen now; it was already fully self-sufficient (re-prices and re-validates the branch/zone on submit from `orderTypeState` alone, regardless of what the tray recorded), so removing the upstream pickers needed no other change. `activeOrderType`'s fallback chain (`?orderType=` → `tray.orderType` → `config.ordering.defaultOrderType`) and `MenuOrderTypeSync` are deliberately left in place — still needed for item availability/pricing display on the menu and any old bookmarked `?orderType=` link — they just have no UI to set them explicitly now (`explicitOrderType` will simply never be true from the menu page itself again).

**Ordering requires a signed-in, verified customer (2026-09-26 → reverted 2026-10-10, see "Guest checkout" above; kept here for history).** The rule was `services/customer-auth.ts#assertCanPlaceOrder(visitor)` (now deleted), called first in `checkout/actions.ts#placeOrderAction`: no customer session → `SIGN_IN_REQUIRED`, signed in but unverified → `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED`. **returnTo:** sign-in / sign-up pages still read `?returnTo=`, sanitised by `shared/return-to.ts#safeReturnTo` (only `same-origin storefront paths`, never absolute/protocol-relative/cross-tenant or the auth pages themselves; the one sanitiser, also used by the Google flow and google-phone); forms push there after sign-in / after verifying a new account, Google carries it, and the sign-in ↔ sign-up links keep it (`signInHref`) — unchanged, still used by the header's sign-in link and the standalone `/account/sign-in` page.

**Profile drawer, saved addresses, account data at checkout (2026-09-26).** No migration. Gender / date of birth live in `customers.metadata.profile` (JSON inside the existing `metadata` column: there is no `gender` or `date_of_birth` column, look at `select metadata from customers`; `tests/customer-profile.test.ts` checks the read-back, after a regex missing its backslashes made the date always read as unset, 2026-09-26) ({gender, dateOfBirth}; mapped onto `Customer.gender`/`dateOfBirth` by `mapCustomer`, unknown values read as unset); addresses use the existing `customer_addresses` table (RLS `customer_addresses_self`). One service, `services/customer-profile.ts` (`getCustomerProfile`, `updateCustomerProfile`, `saveCustomerAddress` create/update, `deleteCustomerAddress`; every call is scoped to `visitor.customerId`), schemas in `validation/customer-profile.ts` (phone = the shared `e164Phone`; DOB real date 1900..today; gender enum `CUSTOMER_GENDERS`; address label/street/area/city required: area is what matches a delivery zone), repository `updateCustomerProfile` (phone + jsonb merge; 23505 -> friendly CONFLICT), `updateAddress`/`deleteAddress` (explicit `customer_id` predicate + RLS). Actions: `account/actions.ts` `getProfileAction`/`updateProfileAction`/`saveAddressAction`/`deleteAddressAction`. UI: `components/storefront/profile-drawer.tsx` on the shared `Sheet` (right panel md+, bottom sheet on phones; Escape/outside click/scroll lock from Radix), opened by the header account pill when signed in (guests still go to sign-in); loads on open (no layout DB read), name + email read-only, mobile/gender/DOB editable (Update profile), addresses add/edit inline + delete with confirm; footer = **Sign out** for every customer, whether they signed in with Google or a password (2026-09-28: the Google-only "Request account deletion" email flow was removed at the requester's direction, along with `requestAccountDeletionAction`, `services/customer-profile.ts#requestAccountDeletion`, `notifications/templates/deletion-request.ts` and `tests/account-deletion.test.ts`). **Checkout** reads the same profile: email shown read-only (account email), phone = `PhoneInput` (flag + calling code picker, default the restaurant's `country`, emits E.164) read-only when the account has a mobile (`PhoneInput` `disabled` = the locked look: same surface as the read-only email, muted text, lock icon, picker without its arrow; the number is `readOnly`, not `disabled`, so it stays focusable/selectable), required otherwise; delivery address = saved addresses as radio cards (default preselected) + "Use a new address" (the normal fields; a new address is used for that order only and is not saved automatically). **Server is authoritative:** `services/checkout.ts#placeOrder` uses the account's saved phone and email over the browser's and passes `accountCustomerId`; `repositories/orders.ts#attachAccountCustomer` links the order to that row (`for update`) instead of the phone-based `upsertCustomer` (still used for guest rows / DB tests), and when the account has no mobile stores the entered one **in the order transaction** (saved only if the order commits; a number owned by another customer -> CONFLICT). The online-payment request built after the order (`placeOrder`, Stripe / JazzCash) uses the same resolved `phone` / `email` as the order, never `input.phone` / `input.email` (a stash-pop merge with the payments work, 2026-09-26, conflicted on the imports and silently kept the browser's values there). Note `customers.phone` is NOT NULL and sign-up / Google-phone always collect one, so "account without a mobile" only happens if data is changed by hand; the flow still handles it. Tests: `tests/customer-profile.test.ts`.

**Google sign-in and the mandatory-phone problem:** `customers.phone` is `not null` and part of `unique (restaurant_id, phone)`, but Google never provides a phone number. `completeGoogleAuth` therefore does **not** create a `customers` row itself: if one already exists for `(restaurant, google-linked user)` it signs in directly; otherwise it mints a short-lived (`signGooglePendingToken`/`verifyGooglePendingToken` in `auth/tokens.ts`, purpose `google-pending`, 15 min) token and the browser is sent to `/account/google-phone?token=...` to collect a phone before `finishGoogleSignup` creates the `customers` row and the real session. Do not "fix" this by inventing a placeholder phone — it would collide with the next Google-only customer at the same restaurant under the unique constraint.

**Flow:** `GET /account/google` (redirects to Google, stores CSRF `state` in a short cookie `rp_google_state` via `web/session.ts#setGoogleState`) → Google → `GET /account/google/callback` (`code`+`state`, verified against the cookie via `consumeGoogleState`) → either signed in or redirected to `/account/google-phone`.

**"Continue with Google" returns to wherever it was clicked, not a hardcoded `/account` (added 2026-09-23, still true after the dialog revert above):** the OAuth round trip has to leave the page (redirect to accounts.google.com and back) regardless of whether sign-in itself is a dialog or a standalone page — this piece isn't about the dialog, it's about the redirect target. The Google link in both `SignInForm`/`SignUpForm` appends `?returnTo=<usePathname()>`; `account/google/route.ts` reads it and `beginGoogleSignIn` stores it in a short cookie (`rp_google_return_to`, `web/session.ts#setGoogleReturnTo`/`consumeGoogleReturnTo`, same TTL/pattern as the state cookie); `account/google/callback/route.ts` consumes it and redirects there on success (falling back to the storefront home via `afterAuthPath` since 2026-09-29; it was `/account`), or forwards it as a `returnTo` query param to `/account/google-phone` when a phone number is still needed (`GooglePhoneForm` then `router.replace`s there, else home). `sanitizeReturnTo` (in both `account/actions.ts` and `google-phone/page.tsx` — small enough it wasn't worth sharing) rejects anything not shaped like `same-origin storefront paths` (no open redirects) and rejects the sign-in/sign-up/google-phone pages themselves (landing back on a "please sign in" page while already signed in is a confusing loop). The Google **error** path still always falls back to the standalone `/account/sign-in?error=google` page rather than reopening the dialog on the original page — a known gap (section 14), left alone since the success path is what mattered.

**There is no `/account` screen any more, and every sign-in returns to where it started (2026-09-28).** Checkout and reservation already passed `returnTo`. The header pill (desktop and the mobile menu sheet) and the mobile dock now use `signInHref(slug, pathname)`, and the "My orders" empty state uses `signInHref(slug, "/orders")`. So a customer who signs in or signs up from a menu item, the home page and so on lands back on that page. Where no `returnTo` exists (a direct visit to `/account/sign-in`), `SignInForm`, `SignUpForm`, `GooglePhoneForm` and the Google callback fall back to the storefront home, never `/account`. `account/page.tsx` is now just `redirect("/")`, so old links do not 404 (without it, `/account` would fall through to the CMS `[pageSlug]` route). Signed-in customers use the header profile drawer for profile, addresses, orders and sign-out, and the mobile dock shows "Orders" in place of "Account". Email verification is still required at sign-in and inline at checkout. Do not add an "already signed in, redirect away" check to the sign-in or sign-up pages: `signUpAction` sets the session cookie before the email-code step, and the re-render it causes would bounce the customer off the page before they verify.

**Session cookie:** `rp_customer_session` (`CUSTOMER_COOKIE`) was defined since the original build but never set anywhere — `getStorefrontCustomer`/`getVisitorContext` could only ever read it. `web/session.ts#setCustomerSession`/`clearCustomerSession` (new) are what `account/actions.ts` calls after `signInCustomer`/`createCustomerAccount`/Google/`finishGoogleSignup` succeed. A customer session is restaurant-scoped from signing in (the JWT carries `restaurantId`), consistent with `customers` being per-restaurant — the same Google login needs its own `customers` row (and so its own "needs phone" step) at every new restaurant.

**The same "RLS context missing `customerId`" bug hit two more places (fixed 2026-09-23, found right after the `resolveCustomer` fix below made real signed-in sessions reach these code paths for the first time):**
- **`cart_items`/`carts` writes** (`new row violates row-level security policy for table "cart_items"` when a signed-in customer added to cart): `cart.ts#cartContext(restaurantId, cartToken)` never included `customerId`, but `cart_is_owned`'s RLS check (0005) is `(customer_id is not null and customer_id = app.current_customer_id()) or (customer_id is null and session_token = app.cart_token())` — once a cart has `customer_id` set (any signed-in customer's cart, set by `openCart`), the token-based branch no longer applies and the customer-id branch needs `ctx.customerId`, which was never passed. Every `cartContext(...)` call site in `cart.ts` (`addToCart`, `updateCartItem`, `removeFromCart`, `emptyCart`, `applyCoupon`, `changeOrderType`, `changeCartLocation`) now takes the cart's `customerId` and passes it through; `findCart`/`readCart` (`web/storefront.ts`) and `checkout.ts#placeOrder` now also pass the signed-in visitor's `customerId` so the *read* doesn't get RLS-blocked either (a signed-in customer's cart could no longer even be found, not just written to). Guest carts (`customer_id is null`) are unaffected — this only bit carts already linked to a customer.
- **`email_verification_codes` reads** (a verification code always says "expired" even entered seconds after being sent): `getActiveVerificationCode` (`repositories/email-verification.ts`) was the one function in that file still on the default `app_runtime` read; that table is RLS-enabled with **no** `app_runtime` policy at all (server-only, same as `notification_events` — 0018), so it always returned zero rows regardless of timing. Fixed by switching it to `write`/`asService` like the other three functions in the file already were.

Both are the same lesson as the `users`/`resolveCustomer` fixes above: a table with no `app_runtime` RLS policy, or a policy keyed on something `ctx` doesn't carry, needs every read *and* write routed through `write`/`asService` — the default `getDb(ctx).queryOne`/`.query` convenience is `app_runtime`-scoped and silently returns nothing instead of erroring, which is what made all of these look like "sometimes it just doesn't work" rather than a clear failure. **When a repository function reads or writes a table and the result seems wrong/empty/blocked for no reason, check the table's RLS policy and what `ctx` fields it needs before assuming the query itself is wrong.**

**Sign-in now also gates on email verification, not just checkout (added 2026-09-23):** `signInAction` already returned `emailVerified` in its result but `SignInForm` ignored it. It now checks `result.data.emailVerified` after a successful sign-in: if false, the action itself sends a code (`ensureVerificationCode` in `after()`, same "only send if none active" helper checkout uses; since 2026-09-29, before that the form called `ensureVerificationCodeAction`) and renders the same `<VerifyEmailForm>` inline instead of finishing sign-in; verifying calls the same `finish()` (toast + close dialog/navigate) that a normal sign-in does. Works in both the modal (`AuthDialog`) and the standalone `/account/sign-in` page since `VerifyEmailForm` doesn't care which one it's in. Google sign-in is not gated this way — Google emails are marked verified at creation (`createGoogleCustomer`/`linkGoogleToCustomer` always set `is_email_verified = true`, since Google already verified it) so there is nothing to gate. (`upsertGoogleUser` named here originally no longer exists — see the `0021` note above; the behavior is unchanged, just on `customers` now.)

**`resolveCustomer` silently signed every customer back out (fixed 2026-09-23):** `customers`' only SELECT policy is `customers_self: id = app.current_customer_id()` (0006), and `app.current_customer_id` is only set from `ctx.customerId` (`database.ts#applyContext`). `resolveCustomer` (called by `getStorefrontCustomer` on every `/account`, `/orders`, checkout page load) looked the customer up by `user_id` via `getCustomerByUserId` — passing `userId`/`restaurantId` in ctx but never `customerId` — so under `app_runtime` (RLS-enforced) `app.current_customer_id()` was always null and the policy blocked every row, no matter how valid the session cookie was. Google sign-in (or any sign-in) would succeed, set the cookie, redirect to `/account` — which then immediately bounced back to `/account/sign-in` because it could never find the customer. Fixed by having `resolveCustomer` use `getCustomerById(session.customerId, { customerId: session.customerId, restaurantId })` instead — `session.customerId` is already in the JWT, so there's no need to rediscover it by `user_id`, and passing it as `ctx.customerId` satisfies the RLS policy the way every other owner-scoped query already does (section 6's RLS note). **This bug predated all the `users`-table churn above and affected every customer sign-in method, not just Google** — grep any future "signed in but immediately signed back out" report for `app.current_customer_id` first. (`getCustomerByUserId`, named here originally, no longer exists post-`0021`; its bootstrap-lookup role is now `getCustomerByGoogleSubOrEmail`, same privileged `write`/`asService` shape.)

**Phone numbers:** `server/validation/common.ts` gained `e164Phone` (validates + normalises to E.164 via `libphonenumber-js`, new lightweight dependency) alongside the older, looser `contactPhone` (kept for reservations, which this phase did not touch). Checkout (`placeOrderSchema`) and sign-up now use `e164Phone`; checkout `email` is now **required** (was optional). `components/storefront/phone-input.tsx` is a country-code-select + local-number field emitting E.164; it is the one place a phone gets typed with a country now — reuse it for any future phone field rather than a bare `<input>`.

**Phone country picker: Radix `Select`, not a native `<select>` (changed 2026-09-23).** Requirement: once a country is picked, the closed field shows only "🇵🇰 +92" (flag + calling code), while the open dropdown still lists "🇵🇰 +92 Pakistan" (flag + code + name) for every country. A native `<select>` can't do this — its closed box always renders the matched `<option>`'s own text, so closed and open can't differ. Rewritten on `@radix-ui/react-select` (already an installed dependency, unused anywhere else — no new dependency added): `Select.Value`'s children are set explicitly from component state (`{flagEmoji(country)} +{callingCode}`) rather than left to auto-render the selected `Select.Item`'s content, so the trigger and the list are independently controlled. `Select.Item` children are unchanged (flag + code + name). The emitted E.164 value and `splitValue`/`emit` logic are untouched — only the trigger/list markup changed.

**Verification code resend has a 60-second cooldown (added 2026-09-23).** `VerifyEmailForm` (shared by sign-up, the sign-in gate, and the checkout gate) now starts a `secondsLeft` countdown at 60 on mount (a code was just sent by the caller right before this form renders) via one `setInterval` for the component's lifetime, clamped at 0 — not recreated every tick, so there's no double-counting or drift from repeated effect setup. "Resend code" is disabled and reads "Resend code in Ns" while `secondsLeft > 0`; clicking it while disabled is also a no-op in `resend()` itself (defense in depth, not just relying on the disabled attribute). A successful resend resets `secondsLeft` back to 60; the same interval keeps counting it down. This is a client-side UX cooldown only — the server-side rate limit on `sendVerificationCode` (3/5min per user, 8/15min per IP, `server/services/customer-auth.ts`) is the real guard against abuse and was already there.

**Testing locally (no DB-free test file yet — a gap):**
```powershell
npm run typecheck && npx vitest run tests/architecture.test.ts && npx next build   # done as of 2026-09-22
# once 0018 is applied:
npm run dev
# /account/sign-up  → create an account → 6-digit code arrives by email (RESEND_API_KEY) or check
#   `select code_hash, expires_at from email_verification_codes order by created_at desc` if email is unconfigured
# /checkout with items in the cart, signed in and unverified → gate appears in place of the form
# /account/google needs GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID/SECRET with an Authorized redirect URI of
#   <NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL>/account/google/callback registered in Google Cloud Console
```
Not built yet: `tests/customer-auth.test.ts` (signup/signin/verify/Google, all DB-free with a fake email provider — follow the `tests/notifications-dispatch-control.test.ts` pattern), header auth state, cart icon, the floating current-orders indicator, and reorder (see section 14).

**Header changes (2026-09-24):** `site-header.tsx` no longer auto-appends "My orders" to the nav (it used to inject it into every restaurant's configured nav items regardless of `websites.config.navigation`) — nav is now exactly `config.navigation.items`, nothing added. Orders are still reachable via the account page's "My orders" link and the floating current-orders widget, so removing it from the top nav loses no functionality, just the persistent link.

**Cart badge can drift stale — fixed by resyncing on cart-page view, not just cart actions.** `web/session.ts#getCartCountHint` is a cookie **hint** only (`rp_cart_n`), deliberately not a DB read on every page view; every cart mutation (`cart/actions.ts`) calls `setCartCountHint` with the real post-mutation count, so it self-heals on the next action — but *viewing* the cart page was never itself a resync point, so a hint that drifted (e.g. from an add-to-cart that optimistically implied a count but then failed — like the `cart_items` RLS bug this session, section 16) could show a stale badge indefinitely with no action ever "fixing" it. `cart/actions.ts#resyncCartCountAction` + `components/storefront/cart-count-sync.tsx` (an invisible client component, one-shot `useEffect` guarded by a ref) now resync the cookie to the cart page's real `cart?.itemCount ?? 0` on every view and `router.refresh()` once, so the header catches up immediately rather than waiting for the visitor's next mutation. Cart pages are the only place this was added — it doesn't apply the same live-read cost everywhere, consistent with the hint's original "no DB read on ordinary page views" intent (only the cart page, which already loaded the real cart anyway, pays for the resync).

**Secure-cookie session silently dropped on LAN mobile testing (fixed 2026-10-10).** Sign-in "succeeded" (toast fired) on a phone hitting a `next start` server at its LAN IP (`http://10.66.188.160:3000`) but immediately bounced back to the sign-in form — same account worked fine on the laptop at `http://localhost:3000`. Cause: `web/cookies.ts#cookieOptions` set `secure: config.app.isProduction`, and `next start` always runs with `NODE_ENV=production` regardless of HTTPS. Chrome treats `localhost` as a secure context even over plain HTTP (so a `Secure` cookie sticks there), but a bare LAN IP is not a secure context — the browser silently drops a `Secure` `Set-Cookie` sent over plain HTTP to anything but `localhost`/127.0.0.1. The sign-in server action still succeeded and returned its JSON (hence the toast), but no cookie was ever stored on the phone, so the next page read (e.g. `checkout`'s `if (!customer) redirect(signInHref(...))`) found no session and sent it straight back to sign-in — looks exactly like "login didn't work" even though it did. **Fix:** `cookieOptions` is now `async` and reads the real request's `x-forwarded-proto` header (set by any real HTTPS reverse proxy — Vercel, nginx, Caddy, section 10) to decide `secure`, falling back to `config.app.isProduction` only when that header is absent (a bare `next start`/`next dev` with no proxy in front, which is exactly the LAN-testing case — now resolves to plain HTTP there, so the cookie survives). Every call site (`web/session.ts`'s four session/Google cookie setters, `web/admin.ts#selectAdminBranch`) now `await`s it. No change for real deployments behind a proxy — `x-forwarded-proto: https` still forces `secure: true` there. **Restart the `next start` process** after pulling this fix — it does not pick up until the server restarts, same as any code change under `next start`.

**OTP paste handling and email code legibility (2026-10-10).** The code UI is a single 6-char `<input maxLength={6}>` in both `VerifyEmailForm` and `ForgotPasswordForm`'s code step (not six separate boxes) — pasting into it already worked for a clean 6-digit paste, but native `maxLength` truncates the raw pasted text *before* the existing `onChange` strips non-digits, so pasting something with a space or dash (e.g. a code copied as "123 456" or trailing whitespace) silently dropped trailing digits. Both inputs now have an `onPaste` handler that reads `clipboardData` itself, strips non-digits, slices to 6, and calls `preventDefault()` so the native truncation never runs; `onChange` also now slices to 6 as a second guard. `autocomplete="one-time-code"` + `inputMode="numeric"` were already in place for mobile autofill — no change needed there. The verification-code email (`notifications/templates/email-verification.ts`) now renders the code in a monospace font (digit clarity, 0/O and 1/l) with `user-select:all` so a tap-and-hold selects just the code. No server-side change — generation, hashing, expiry, attempt limits and resend cooldown (section above) are untouched.

**Current-orders widget (`current-orders-widget.tsx`) now animated with a "Track your order" bubble (2026-09-24):** icon changed from `ShoppingBag` to `MapPin` (reads as live tracking, not just "your cart/orders"), plus a `animate-ping` pulsing ring behind the button (the same visual language as a live-location dot). A dismissible bubble ("Track your order", closes with an X) appears next to the button whenever there's an active order; dismissal is remembered per-tab in `sessionStorage` (key `rp_track_bubble_dismissed`) so it doesn't reappear on every page nav within the same session, but does reappear in a fresh tab/session — deliberate, not a bug (there's no server-side dismissal state, and re-showing occasionally for a still-active order is the safer default over never re-surfacing it). This made the component client-side (`"use client"`; it was previously a plain server-safe component) since it now needs `useState`/`useEffect`/`sessionStorage` — its props (`restaurantSlug`, `count`) are unchanged, so the `layout.tsx` call site needed no changes.

## 17. Admin panel (`/admin`)

Staff-only UI. Phase 1: login, a protected shell, dashboard, orders, menu. Phase 2: reservations, reviews. Phase 3: kitchen view, customers, coupons, delivery zones, locations, settings, payments. **Moved to `/admin` (2026-09-28, one admin per restaurant)** — old `/admin/...` links redirect via `src/app/admin/[[...path]]/page.tsx` (`adminPath(config.app.defaultRestaurantSlug, sub)`). The route list below is unchanged apart from that prefix.

```
src/app/admin/
  login/                        page.tsx (client form, reuses the storefront AuthShell) + actions.ts (signInAction → web/session.signInStaffSession)
  (dashboard)/                  route group: layout.tsx guards with requireStaffForAdmin(), applies the restaurant's brand
                                  theme (getAdminTheme, see below), renders AdminSidebar/AdminHeader
    page.tsx                    dashboard: status counts + recent orders (orders.view)
    orders/                     list (filters/pagination) + [orderNumber] detail; actions.ts: updateOrderStatusAction
    kitchen/                     board (redesigned 2026-10-17): counters (New/Preparing/Ready/Late 20m+) + three lanes
                                  New (pending, confirmed) · Preparing · Ready (ready, out_for_delivery), oldest first;
                                  ticket = urgency bar + timer (green <10m, amber 10–19, red 20m+), type icon, items with qty
                                  badges, notes callout; OrderStatusControl layout="ticket": ONE primary next step
                                  (shared/order-flow.ts#nextOrderStep) + "More" (skip-ahead, cancel). Order page uses
                                  layout="full". Both offer only `forwardOrderStatuses(status, orderType)` — "Out for delivery"
                                  is never offered on pickup/dine-in (it used to be, on every ticket);
                                  KitchenAutoRefresh polls router.refresh() every 20s (no SSE — that stream is one-order-scoped, see section 8);
                                  the whole screen is ONE statement since 2026-10-04 (see "Admin order flow performance")
    reservations/                list w/ status tabs (upcoming/pending/confirmed/seated/completed/cancelled/no_show/all), inline ReservationStatusControl
                                  (plain select — no rank rule, any status → any status); actions.ts: updateReservationStatusAction ('reservations.manage')
    reviews/                      moderation queue, status tabs (pending/approved/rejected/all); ReviewModerationControl (approve/reject + reply/feature,
                                  independent actions matching moderateReview's combined patch shape); actions.ts: moderateReviewAction ('reviews.manage').
                                  Moderating calls getStorefrontCache().invalidate() (approved reviews feed the storefront snapshot, same as menu writes)
    menu/                       categories + items; items/new, items/[itemId] (variants, add-on groups); actions in menu/actions.ts + menu/items/actions.ts
    customers/                   list (search/sort) + [customerId] detail (stats + order history); view only, no repo mutation exists beyond checkout's
                                  own upsertCustomer, and none was asked for
    coupons/                     full CRUD, inline edit (CouponManager); coupons are excluded from the storefront snapshot — no cache invalidation
    delivery-zones/               full CRUD, inline edit (DeliveryZoneManager); zones ARE in the snapshot — every write invalidates the cache
```
