  - **Verified 2026-10-07 with two production builds side by side:** Zaytoun `#2F5D46` on `.next-verify` port 3100, and Bun Intended `#5B2EFF` on `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_RESTAURANT=bun-intended NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify-b` port 3101. **`NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is baked in at build, so each restaurant needs its own build.** On home, menu, cart, checkout, orders, current-orders, reservation, reviews, locations and sign-in:
    - `.theme-root`'s `--color-brand` equals that restaurant's DB primary;
    - the checkout's Place order button, selected order-type card and summary header compute to exactly that colour;
    - neither restaurant's HTML contains the other's primary/accent or the old fixed palette;
    - there is no horizontal overflow. Phones keep the fixed bottom Place order bar. Order type and payment are native radios (`sr-only` inside a label, focus ring via `has-[:focus-visible]`). The unlocked multi-branch picker is now a native `<select>` (name · km · Recommended). With `branch` locked, pickup orders get their own "Pickup branch" card, and the payment step number follows (3 or 4). Tip = preset pills plus a "Custom amount" pill that reveals the input. The trust box shows only true claims per method (`card_online`/`wallet` go to the hosted page of the restaurant's configured `settings.payments.onlineProvider`; cash says nothing is charged now).
  - **Bug fixed in the same change:** the page prices without a tip, but `calculatePricing` adds the tip untaxed to the order total, so "Total due" and the Place order amount left out a chosen tip. The form now shows `sumMoney([pricing.total, tip])` (`totalDue`). Also fixed: "Please add the city." (`errors.city`) only appeared under the branch picker, which never shows while the city is empty. It now appears under the City field when the picker is hidden, and for a saved address it appears in the saved-address error line.
  - Verified with Playwright at 375/390/414/768/1024/1280/1440 (no horizontal overflow) against `NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify next start -p 3100`. The instance serves `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_RESTAURANT` (currently `zaytoun`), and any other slug 404s. Zaytoun has multi-branch ordering, so a minted test cart also needs `locationId` plus an `rp_dest_<slug>` destination cookie (`encodeDestination`), or checkout redirects to `/menu`. The locations table is `restaurant1s` (migration 0016), and customer verification is `customers.is_email_verified`.
- **Buffet-only restaurants get a buffet menu page (2026-10-03).** When `restaurant.features.alaCarteEnabled` is false (the whole menu is buffet packages — rule 12), `menu/page.tsx` uses `PageHero size="lg"` (new size, `min-h-[min(62svh,560px)]`; `md` and `sm` unchanged), eyebrow "Buffet dining", default title "The Buffet" (a configured `page_content` title still wins), then a paper "How it works" band before the menu: the restaurant's own `description`/`shortDescription` (generic one-line fallback only when both are empty), seating length and party size from `settings.reservations` (`defaultDurationMinutes`, `minGuests`–`maxGuests`), a "Reserve a table" button when `features.reservations` is on (the buffet itself is booked through reservations, section 18), and the cover photo. A la carte restaurants, including ones that also sell a buffet package, are unchanged.
- **A buffet package opens as a printed buffet card (2026-10-03).** Clicking a buffet package (Hi-Tea, Dinner Buffet — any item with `isBuffetPackage`) no longer shows the dish layout with the description as one grey paragraph. `DishDetail` runs `shared/buffet-menu.ts#parseBuffetMenu(item.description)`; when it returns sections it renders `components/storefront/buffet-menu-card.tsx` instead: package name, per-head prices from the item's **variants** (e.g. "Adult" / "Children (3–10 years)", `delta` variants added to the base price as in `domain/menu-selection`; no variants = "Per person" base price), "+ tax" when tax is on and not included, then every section's dish names (no per-dish prices) in CSS columns (`sm:columns-2`, page `lg:columns-3`, `break-inside-avoid`), notes and the "subject to change" footer; the photo becomes a banner, the customizer/Reserve sits under the card. The intercepted sheet opens at the new `Sheet size="xl"` (`min(60rem,94vw)`) for buffet packages. **Description format** (no schema change; hint + example placeholder in the admin item form when "Buffet package" is ticked): `Heading:` line then one dish per line, or `Heading: dish, dish`, or `## Heading`; `Note: …` lines go under the card; bullets stripped. Prose (no headings, any line > 60 chars, or < 3 lines) returns null and keeps the old paragraph. Tests: `tests/buffet-menu.test.ts` (DB-free). The Villa seed has no buffet-package items, so to see it add one in admin.

## 18. Online payments (Stripe card, JazzCash wallet)

`server/integrations/payments.ts` is the `PaymentProvider` abstraction (`isConfigured`, `supports(method)`, `createIntent`). `getPaymentProvider(method, onlineProvider)` — **`onlineProvider` (the restaurant's `settings.payments.onlineProvider`, one of `"none"|"stripe"|"jazzcash"`) picks the gateway for `card_online`/`wallet`**; without it, the first provider in the list whose `supports()` matched would win regardless of what the restaurant configured (harmless while only Stripe existed, wrong the moment a second online provider — JazzCash — also claims `wallet`). Cash/terminal methods always use the cash provider regardless of `onlineProvider`.

- **Stripe** (`card_online`) is real, via Stripe Checkout (hosted page) — plain REST + `fetch`, no `stripe` SDK dependency, same "no SDK, no extra dependency" convention as `integrations/resend.ts`. `server/integrations/stripe.ts`: `createCheckoutSession` (bracketed form-encoding for nested params — `toFormBody`), `retrieveCheckoutSession` (re-fetches the session from Stripe's API with our own secret key — the actual trust boundary for the return route, since a `session_id` in a URL alone proves nothing), `verifyWebhookSignature` (Stripe's documented `t=...,v1=...` HMAC-SHA256-over-`"ts.rawBody"` scheme, timing-safe compare, 300s replay tolerance). **Test-mode keys are free and instant** at Stripe's dashboard (Developers → API keys) — unlike JazzCash, no business/merchant onboarding needed to get `sk_test_...`/`pk_test_...` keys; that's why Stripe was the first gateway actually wired end-to-end (2026-09-26) despite JazzCash being requested first — JazzCash's sandbox was unreachable/slow to obtain credentials for at the time. PKR is a supported Stripe presentment currency, so no currency conversion is needed even though Stripe doesn't operate/pay out in Pakistan (irrelevant in test mode; revisit before any live key).
- **Env:** `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` (both required, though the publishable key isn't actually used by the Checkout-redirect flow — kept for a future embedded-Elements option), `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (optional but recommended — see Return vs webhook below). Admin settings → Payments → "Online payment provider" = **Stripe**, `card_online` enabled.
- **Stripe flow:** `createIntent` returns `{redirectUrl}` (a plain GET, unlike JazzCash's POST form) to Stripe's hosted Checkout page; `checkout-form.tsx` does `window.location.href = redirectUrl` (`PlaceOrderResult.payment.kind === "redirect"`, vs `"form"` for JazzCash — the two gateways need different client-side navigation, hence the discriminated union). `restaurantId`/`orderId`/`orderNumber` travel in the Checkout Session's `metadata` (and again in `payment_intent_data.metadata`, so a `payment_intent.*` event would carry them too) — the webhook has no restaurant slug in its URL (it's one global endpoint, not per-tenant), so it resolves the restaurant from metadata alone, not from a request path.
- **Return vs webhook — both call `confirmOnlinePayment`, safely (it's idempotent):** the customer-facing return route (`checkout/pay/stripe/route.ts`, `GET`, Stripe's `success_url`) re-fetches the session and redirects the browser to the order page immediately; a customer who closes the tab mid-payment never reaches it. The webhook (`api/internal/payments/stripe/webhook/route.ts`, `POST`, configure in the Stripe Dashboard or `stripe listen --forward-to localhost:3000/api/internal/payments/stripe/webhook` locally, events `checkout.session.completed` + `checkout.session.async_payment_failed`) is Stripe's recommended source of truth and fires regardless. Skipping the webhook (leaving `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` unset) still lets checkout work; it just means a dropped redirect leaves an order looking unpaid until staff notice.
- **JazzCash** (`wallet`) is real, end-to-end, but **untested against JazzCash's actual sandbox — built 2026-09-26 with no sandbox credentials in hand.** `server/integrations/jazzcash.ts` implements their publicly documented Hosted Checkout Page (HCP) Mobile Wallet flow (`pp_TxnType = MWALLET`): builds the `pp_*` request fields + HMAC-SHA256 `pp_SecureHash` (salted with the Integrity Salt, over every non-empty `pp_`/`ppmpf_` value sorted by key — `computeSecureHash`, exported only for `tests/jazzcash.test.ts`), and verifies a return the same way (`verifyReturn`). **Verify the exact field list/order against the Integration Guide PDF JazzCash sends with sandbox credentials** — a hash mismatch shows up as `pp_ResponseCode "999"` on the return; adjust the field list there if so.
- **Env:** `JAZZCASH_MERCHANT_ID`, `JAZZCASH_PASSWORD`, `JAZZCASH_INTEGRITY_SALT` (all three, or JazzCash stays unconfigured), `JAZZCASH_ENV` (`sandbox`|`live`, default sandbox). Get them from JazzCash's sandbox merchant self-onboarding. A restaurant also needs admin settings → Payments → "Online payment provider" = **JazzCash**, and `wallet` in its enabled methods, before "Wallet" appears at checkout — `getCheckoutOptions` (`server/services/checkout.ts`) gates `wallet` exactly like `card_online` (`features.onlinePayments` AND `onlineProvider !== "none"` AND the provider actually configured); it used to skip that gate entirely for `wallet`; showing "Wallet" with nothing behind it.
- **Flow (both gateways):** `placeOrder` (`server/services/checkout.ts`) calls `getPaymentProvider(...).createIntent`, whose result becomes `PlaceOrderResult.payment` — a discriminated union: `{kind: "redirect", redirectUrl}` (Stripe, plain GET) or `{kind: "form", formAction, formFields}` (JazzCash, needs a real browser POST). `checkout-form.tsx` branches on `kind`: `window.location.href = redirectUrl`, or `submitWalletForm()` builds a hidden `<form>` client-side and submits it. `returnUrl` is built from `provider.id` (`/checkout/pay/<provider.id>`), not the payment method, since one method (`wallet`) could map to different providers later. The order is already committed at this point (same as a cash order) — a failed `createIntent` call does **not** undo it, only marks the payment `failed` and shows a "contact the restaurant or choose cash" error; this mirrors an unpaid COD order, not a rollback.
- **Return:** JazzCash POSTs to `pp_ReturnURL` = `/checkout/pay/jazzcash` (`route.ts`, `POST` only); it verifies the hash (never acts on an unverified payload) and looks the order up via `ppmpf_1` (orderId) + `pp_BillReference` (orderNumber) through `getOrderForAccessGrant` (same privileged, service-role lookup pattern as the email order-access-token — the verified hash IS the proof). Stripe redirects (`GET`) to `/checkout/pay/stripe`, which re-fetches the session from Stripe instead (see above) — different verification mechanism, same shape. Both call **`confirmOnlinePayment`** (`server/services/checkout.ts`, renamed from `confirmWalletPayment` once Stripe needed it too), which is idempotent (a resolved payment is left alone, so a retried/duplicated callback is harmless) and **only ever changes `payment_status`** (`orders.payment_status` + the `payments` row, via `setOrderPaymentStatus` — fixed 2026-09-26 to write both; it used to touch only `payments`, so nothing else in the app — order lists, the SSE trigger, admin filters — ever reflected a call to it, and it was dead code until JazzCash's callback started using it). `orders.status` (the staff-driven kitchen state machine, section 5 rule 4) is untouched: a paid online order still needs a staff `confirm`, same as cash. Both return routes end by redirecting the browser (303) to the order tracking page, which already shows the true `payment_status` on the next `trackOrder` read (no query-param banner needed) and gets it live via the existing SSE trigger (`orders.payment_status is distinct from new.payment_status`, migration `0015`) since the redirect target is a fresh page load anyway.
- **No DB migration needed for either gateway:** `payment_method.wallet`/`card_online` and `payments.provider` (free `text`) already existed; only `settings.payments.onlineProvider`'s zod enum gained `"jazzcash"` (JSONB, no schema change).
- **Testing:** `tests/jazzcash.test.ts` (hash build/verify round-trip, tamper rejection, non-`000` codes) and `tests/stripe.test.ts` (webhook signature verify/tamper/wrong-secret/replay) are both DB-free and cover the correctness-critical, pure-function part of each gateway. Neither can exercise a real sandbox/test-mode call end-to-end here: JazzCash still has **no sandbox credentials in hand** (built 2026-09-26 against their publicly documented scheme — verify the exact field list/order against the Integration Guide PDF they send with credentials; a hash mismatch shows up as `pp_ResponseCode "999"`); Stripe's return/webhook routes need a public HTTPS URL (ngrok over `localhost`, or `stripe listen` for the webhook specifically) since both gateways redirect/POST to a real internet address, never `localhost`. Once credentials exist for either: set its env vars, set a restaurant's `onlineProvider` accordingly and enable the matching method, place an order, and watch `select payment_status, provider, status, failure_reason from orders join payments on payments.order_id = orders.id where order_number = '...'` after the redirect back.
- **Local gateway testing without a deploy (ngrok):** ngrok v3 is installed per-user at `%LOCALAPPDATA%
grok
grok.exe` (this Windows box has no winget/choco/scoop — it was installed from the official zip and added to the user PATH; authtoken in `%LOCALAPPDATA%
grok
grok.yml`). Use the account's free static domain so the URL registered with the gateway never changes: `ngrok http --url=<domain> 3000` alongside `npm run dev`, set `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL="https://<domain>"` in `.env.local` (restart `next dev` — env is read at start), register `https://<domain>/checkout/pay/jazzcash` as the JazzCash Return URL, and open the storefront **through the ngrok URL**, not localhost. `next.config.ts` has `allowedDevOrigins: ["*.ngrok-free.dev", "*.ngrok-free.app"]` so `next dev` serves HMR/dev assets to the tunnel (dev-only, ignored by builds). Switch `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` back to `http://localhost:3000` (and re-register the deployed domain with JazzCash) when done / on deploy. **Sandbox credentials obtained 2026-10-17** (in `.env.local` only, never committed). **Gotcha fixed then:** `pp_TxnDateTime`/`pp_TxnExpiryDateTime` used the server's local clock — fine on a PKT dev box, but on a UTC host (Vercel) the 1-hour expiry landed ~4 h in the past from JazzCash's view, so every payment would fail as expired; `formatTxnDateTime` now always formats in Pakistan time (fixed UTC+5). `computeSecureHash` also now signs only `pp_`/`ppmpf_` keys (as documented), so a stray non-`pp_` field on the return can't break verification; a failed verification logs `pp_ResponseMessage` too, which is where a sandbox `999` checksum error explains itself. **Pending results:** `verifyReturn` returns `pending` for JazzCash codes `157` (Mobile Account pending) and `124` (awaiting OTC payment); the return route then leaves the payment Pending (redirect `?payment=pending`) instead of marking it failed — previously every non-`000` code became `failed`. **Sandbox test wallets:** `03123456789` → success, `03123456780` → authentication error, any other number → pending; CNIC last 6 digits `345678` for all. No IPN route exists yet (leave the portal's IPN URL empty): a customer who pays then closes the tab before the return leaves the order Pending until staff mark it. **Sandbox findings 2026-10-17 (merchant `MC898028`) — wallet payments blocked on JazzCash's side, not ours:** every request and hash was accepted as sent (visible in the portal's *API Call Log*, `Sandbox/CallLog/CallLogAPI`, each row's eye icon shows request + response JSON — use it first when debugging). REST v2.0 `ApplicationAPI/API/2.0/Purchase/DoMWalletTransaction` → `110` "Provided Transaction Type and Version are not allowed" (v2.0 MWALLET not enabled for the merchant; JazzCash must enable it). REST v1.1 `ApplicationAPI/API/Payment/DoTransaction` (`MWALLET`, number in `ppmpf_1`) and the hosted page with `pp_TxnType=MWALLET` → `199` for every number, including the success test number. The portal's own testers fail the same way. The hosted page with `pp_TxnType` empty (JazzCash's own sample) bounces straight back with `110` "Please provide a valid value for pp_MSISDN". Other facts learned: `ppmpf_*` rejects dashes (our UUID order id in `ppmpf_1` must be sent without them); v2.0 signs a fixed field set (extra fields like `pp_Version`/`pp_TxnType`/`pp_BankID` → "valid value for pp_SecureHash" errors); status lookup `ApplicationAPI/API/PaymentInquiry/Inquire` (`pp_TxnRefNo`, `pp_MerchantID`, `pp_Password`, hash) works and reports `pp_Status`/`pp_PaymentResponseCode`; the `CustomerPortal/.../merchantform/` endpoint's firewall rejects curl and automated browsers ("Request Rejected") — only a real browser can drive it, while `ApplicationAPI` REST works from curl. **Demo mode (`JAZZCASH_ENV=demo`, added 2026-10-17 because the sandbox account can't complete a wallet payment):** `buildWalletCheckout` points the form at `DEMO_CHECKOUT_PATH` (`/checkout/pay/jazzcash/demo`, relative) instead of JazzCash; that route (`app/(site)/checkout/pay/jazzcash/demo/route.ts` + `demo-page.ts`, plain server-rendered HTML, 404 unless demo) verifies OUR request signature (`verifyRequestSignature` — so amount/return URL can't be tampered), shows a JazzCash-styled page with a DEMO bar (mobile number + CNIC, Pay/Cancel, the test-account table), decides the result with `demoOutcome` (sandbox's table: `03123456789`+`345678` → `000`, `03123456780` → `199`, others → `157` pending; Cancel or an expired request → `199`), then auto-posts a response signed by `buildDemoReturn` to `pp_ReturnURL` — the REAL return route verifies and applies it, so everything after the gateway (hash check, `confirmOnlinePayment`, order page, admin payments, `DEMO…` transaction id) is the production path. Credentials are optional in demo (defaults `DEMO`/`demo`/`jazzcash-demo-salt`). It marks orders paid with no money moved: never set it in a real deployment. Tests: `tests/jazzcash-demo.test.ts` (logic + route end-to-end, tamper → 400, non-demo → 404).

## 20. The tray (cart) is a cookie; placing the order is the one database transaction

**Gotcha (fixed 2026-10-17): placing an order must not bounce /checkout to the menu.** `placeOrderAction` clears the tray cookie + `revalidatePath`, so Next re-renders `/checkout` inside the same action response; that page's empty-tray `redirect("/menu")` then raced the client's hand-off to the gateway (JazzCash form POST / Stripe redirect) and, over a slower link (ngrok, real networks), won — the customer landed on the menu and never saw the payment page (on fast localhost the gateway usually won, so it hid). Fix: the action also sets `placed_<slug>` (`markOrderPlaced`, httpOnly, 120 s) and the page, on an empty tray, shows an "Order received" notice (`OrderPlacedNotice`, link to the order) instead of redirecting while that marker exists (`readPlacedOrder`; only accepts an order-number-shaped value). Test: `tests/checkout-placed-order.test.ts`.

Decision record: `DECISIONS.md` §28 (and §29 for the session/transaction-framing side). Rebuilt 2026-09-28: there is no database cart at all any more (the `carts`/`cart_items` tables still exist, unused; `repositories/carts.ts` is gone).

**An empty tray still keeps its choices (fixed 2026-10-18).** `local-cart.tsx#encodeCart(cart, defaultOrderType)` writes the cookie whenever the tray holds ANY choice — lines, a coupon, a branch (`locationId`) or an order type other than the restaurant's default (`InitialTray.defaultOrderType`, from `config.ordering.defaultOrderType`) — and deletes it only when nothing was chosen. It used to write only for lines/coupon, so with an empty cart the order-type doors and the auto-picked branch fell back to the defaults on every full reload. Every server reader decodes the cookie and works from `lines`, so an item-less cookie is still an empty cart; `clearTray` after an order still deletes it. Tests: `tests/local-cart.test.ts` ("encodeCart keeps an empty cart's chosen order type or branch").


```
QuickAddButton / ItemCustomizer / cart page ─► useLocalCart() ─► document.cookie "rp_tray_<slug>"      (no request)
layout (every page)   ─► web/storefront#getInitialTray ─► decodeTray(cookie) ─► services/cart#viewTray  (snapshot, no DB)
/cart "Apply" promo   ─► checkCouponAction ─► previewCoupon ─► coupons#findPreviewCoupon                (snapshot, no DB)
/checkout             ─► readTrayView + getDeliveryZones (snapshot) + priceTray (snapshot)  + getCustomerProfile (1 DB read)
"Place order"         ─► placeOrderAction ─► placeOrder ─► repositories/orders#createOrder   ═ ONE transaction, 5 round trips:
                          1 begin+role+context · 2 ONE read (restaurant, customer FOR UPDATE, whole tray's menu, [zones], [coupon+usage])
                          3 insert order · 4 ONE write (all lines, all add-ons, payment, [delivery], [coupon usage] as CTEs) · 5 commit
                       ─► clearTray(slug)
```

- **Cookie format** (`shared/tray.ts`): `v1!<orderType>!<locationHex|_>!<couponB64|_>!<line>*<line>…`, line = `<itemHex>.<variantHex|_>.<qty>.<addonHex~qty,…|_>.<noteB64|_>`. Cookie-safe characters only (no percent-encoding), UUIDs as 32 hex, ~40 chars per plain line; `TRAY_COOKIE_MAX_CHARS` 3800 (browsers cap a cookie at 4 KB) — `addLine` returns `false` and the UI toasts "Your tray is full" rather than silently losing a line; max 50 lines. Not HttpOnly (the browser writes it); `decodeTray` is tolerant — anything malformed is dropped line by line, never thrown — and nothing in it is trusted for money.
- **Display vs. contents:** the cookie holds ids and quantities only. How a line looks (`TrayLineDisplay`: name, image, variant, add-on names, unit price, add-ons total, `problem`) comes from the server on a page load (`getInitialTray` → `viewTray`, current menu prices) or from the add button's own menu data for a line just added. `LocalCartProvider` starts from the server's `initial` (so SSR and the first client render match — no empty-then-filled flash, no `hydrated` flag any more), writes the cookie on every change, adopts a new `initial` when the server re-renders the layout with a different cookie, and calls `router.refresh()` on focus/visibility when another tab changed the cookie.
- **One resolver:** `server/domain/menu-selection.ts#resolveMenuSelection` (pure) decides availability, variant/add-on rules, defaults and prices for BOTH the preview (snapshot `MenuItem`s via `catalog#getOrderableMenuItems`) and the order (rows from `repositories/menu.ts#orderableItemsSql` inside the order transaction). It is a rule-for-rule port of the old `resolveItemSelection`. A preview line that fails resolution is shown with its `problem` and blocks Checkout until removed.
- **Checkout page:** 404-free redirect to `/menu` when the tray is empty; pricing = `priceTray` (same `calculatePricing`); a promo code that no longer applies is dropped with a visible `couponNotice` and the form submits only the code actually shown as applied (`placeOrder` uses `input.couponCode`, never the tray's stale one). One DB read: `getCustomerProfile` (customer row + addresses + `emailVerified`, one transaction, `repositories/customers.ts#getCustomerWithAddresses`).
- **`createOrder`** (`repositories/orders.ts`): the statement budget is fixed — 5 round trips for any order (1 line or 50, pickup or delivery with a coupon), +1 only when a signed-in customer's account has no mobile yet (`saveFirstAccountPhone`, one `update … where not exists (clash)` statement). Order-item ids are generated in Node (`randomUUID`) so add-ons can reference their line inside the same batched statement. Data-modifying CTEs are safe here: FKs between them are checked at statement end, `payment_to_order` only recomputes `payment_status` (still `pending`), `sync_delivery_from_order` only reacts to a status change. `orders.cart_id` is written `null`. The read projects only needed columns (`ORDER_RESTAURANT_COLUMNS`, `ORDER_CUSTOMER_COLUMNS`, `orderableItemsSql`) — see section 6 on response size.
- **Measured (hosted pooler, 2026-09-28, every commit rewritten to rollback):** place order = 1 transaction / 5 round trips for 1, 3 or 5 lines and for delivery + coupon; server time for all of it < 5 ms (`explain analyze`: order insert with its 7 triggers 2.9 ms); wall 1.7–2.4 s on a steady link, up to ~3.5–4 s under pooler jitter. The same order before this work: sync the tray (1 transaction, 21 statements for 3 lines, ~8 s) + place order (~6 transactions incl. 3 nested, ~60 statements). Sign-in: 1 transaction / 3 round trips, ~0.9–1.2 s (was 5 transactions). Checkout page profile read: 1 transaction / 4 round trips, ~1.1–1.6 s. Add to cart / change quantity / apply promo: 0 DB.
- **Reorder** (`order/actions.ts#reorderAction` → `services/orders.ts#reorderLines`): reads the past order (its own DB reads — proof of ownership + lines), resolves them against the current menu, and returns lines + displays; `ReorderButton` adds them with `addLines` (reports how many fit).
- **Legacy cookies** `rp_cart` / `rp_cart_n` (database-cart era) are only deleted on sign-out now. Sign-out keeps the tray (it is the browser's, not the account's).
- **Gaps:** a tray is per browser (not synced across a customer's devices — that would need the database again); the e2e spec (`e2e/customer-flow.spec.ts`) still describes the older cart UI copy and was not re-run here (no running app + test account); the DB-backed `tests/checkout.test.ts` was ported to line arrays but needs the local test Postgres to run.
- **Testing:** `tests/tray.test.ts` (codec: round trip incl. unicode notes, cookie-safe charset, tolerance of tampered input, size limit), `tests/local-cart.test.ts` (reducers, `toTray`/`fromInitialTray`), `tests/menu-selection.test.ts` (resolver rules), `tests/create-order-sql.test.ts` (real `createOrder` against a recording fake: one transaction — a nested one throws —, 3 statements inside it for any order shape, account/verification/phone rules, menu prices), `tests/customer-profile.test.ts` (`placeOrder` → one `createOrder` call), `tests/storefront-services.test.ts` (preview on snapshot, order on DB).
- **Cart lines merge by (menuItemId, variantId, add-on set, note) — fixed 2026-10-01, was previously "every add is its own line" on purpose (old comment: "mirrors the order").** Adding an item already in the tray (same variant/add-ons/note) now increases `quantity` on the existing `LocalCartLine` in `addLocalLine` instead of pushing a new one (`components/storefront/local-cart.tsx`) — the merge key ignores add-on order (`addonsKey` sorts `addonId:quantity` pairs) but is exact on the note text, so a different special instruction intentionally stays its own row. `fromInitialTray` runs the same merge (`mergeDuplicateLines`) on whatever the cookie decodes to, so a tray saved before this fix self-heals — collapses to one row with a summed quantity — the next time that cookie is read, no migration needed. Nothing server-side changed: `toTray`/`encodeTray`/`createOrder` already only ever saw whatever `LocalCart.lines` contained, so once lines merge before being written to the cookie, checkout pricing and the order's `order_items` rows are correct for free.
- **Dish-card quantity control (2026-10-11, at the requester's direction):** once a dish has **any** row in the tray (plain, or with a variant / add-ons / note from the dish sheet), `QuickAddButton` swaps its round "+" for a brand-filled pill `[trash|−] qty [+]` (`data-testid="quick-qty-<slug>"`). `qty` is the total across all of that dish's rows; the buttons act on the **most recently added row**: "+" re-adds that exact row's input (variant, add-ons, note, display) through `addLine`, so it merges into it and keeps the cookie-full check (disabled at 99); "−" = `updateQuantity(row, qty - 1)`, and at 1 it removes that row. At a total of 1 the left button is a trash (the "+" button returns). Before the first add, dishes with required choices keep the "+" link to their sheet; a different configuration is still added from the sheet. The pill is brand-coloured (`--brand-primary`), not a fixed olive, per the theme-token rule in section 19. Hit areas: 40×44 px (`h-11 w-10`) on tiles, 32×36 px on compact menu rows. The fly-to-cart animation and the "added" toast run on the first add only.

## 21. Delivery location & branch selection (Google Maps)

Checkout's delivery step (2026-09-27) lets a customer use their current GPS location, search an address (Google Places Autocomplete), or drag a pin on a map to set the exact delivery point; the resolved city then filters `restaurant1s` (branches) down to that city, ranks them by straight-line distance and recommends the nearest one. No schema change was needed — the columns already existed (`restaurant1s.city/latitude/longitude`, `customer_addresses.latitude/longitude`, `orders.delivery_address` jsonb) — this was wiring, not new tables.

- **Env:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` (optional). Google Cloud Console → enable **Maps JavaScript API, Places API** on the key (Geocoding API is not needed — see the Nominatim subsection below); restrict it to HTTP referrers for the site's domain(s) (+ `localhost` while developing) — Maps browser keys are meant to be public, referrer-restricted, not secret. Config: `config.maps` (`server/config/index.ts`) → `{ apiKey } | null`; a server component reads it and hands it to the client as a prop (same pattern as `config.push.web` for Firebase) — client code never reads `process.env` directly, keeping the "process.env only in server/config" rule.
- **No key configured → no regression:** `LocationPicker` (`components/storefront/location-picker.tsx`) renders nothing when `apiKey` is null; checkout and the address book fall back to exactly the plain text fields that existed before this feature.
- **`shared/geo.ts`:** pure, isomorphic `haversineKm`/`sortByDistance` — the only new geometry code; no npm mapping/geo package was added.
- **`components/storefront/location-picker.tsx`:** loads `https://maps.googleapis.com/maps/api/js?libraries=places` once per page (a module-level promise, so mounting it twice — checkout **and** the profile drawer's address form — never double-injects the script). "Use current location" (`navigator.geolocation`), a Places `Autocomplete` search box, and a draggable `Marker`; any of the three reverse-geocodes (`Geocoder`) to `{addressLine1, area, city, postalCode, latitude, longitude}` and calls `onResolve`. Handles permission-denied, no-geolocation-support, and geocode-failure inline (never blocks the manual fields below it). `@types/google.maps` is a dev-only devDependency (types only; the JS itself loads via the script tag, not npm) — install it again if `node_modules` is ever reset from a lockfile that predates it.
- **Branch ranking (`checkout-form.tsx`):** `customerPoint` = the chosen saved address's lat/lng, or the picker's last resolve; `customerCity` same precedence. Branches are filtered to `restaurant1s.city` (case-insensitive) matching `customerCity`, then `sortByDistance` when a point is known. The nearest is auto-selected (`useEffect` keyed on the ranked id list) but a customer can tap any other card in the same city to override it; zero matches shows "We don't have a branch in `<city>` yet." instead of silently keeping the old branch. Only shown at all when the restaurant has more than one active location — a single-branch restaurant never sees this step. **Since 2026-10-12 it is effectively off:** with `BranchingFeature` off checkout passes only the primary location (single-branch mode, section 23), and with it on the branch is locked from the menu.
- **How the branch pick actually takes effect:** selecting a card calls the tray's `useLocalCart().setLocationId` (no server action — there is no database cart, section 20) then `router.refresh()`; the checkout page re-reads the tray cookie and re-prices with that branch's zones, and `placeOrder` passes `tray.locationId` to `createOrder`. `local-cart.tsx#commit` writes the cookie synchronously for exactly this: written only from its effect, the refresh could go out with the old cookie and the provider's "adopt the server's reading" effect would undo the pick (found merging master's DB-cart version, which called `setLocationAction`, 2026-09-29). Delivery-zone matching (`matchDeliveryZone`, area/postal-code strings — unchanged, still no geo-radius logic) keys off that same `locationId`.
- **Coordinates on the order:** `placeOrderSchema`/`CreateOrderInput.address` already had optional `latitude`/`longitude` fields wired to the `delivery_address` jsonb snapshot; the checkout form now actually populates them from the picker/saved address, so an order's exact delivery point is preserved even if the customer later edits or deletes the saved address.
- **Saved addresses (`profile-drawer.tsx` → `AddressForm`):** same `LocationPicker`, reusing `saveAddressAction`/`saveCustomerAddress`/`server/repositories/customers.ts`. Fixed in the same change: `updateAddress`'s SQL silently dropped `latitude`/`longitude` on edit (only `saveAddress`/create wrote them) — editing a saved address with a new pin now actually persists the new coordinates.
- **Country restriction:** both pickers pass the restaurant's `phoneCountry` (already computed for the phone input) as the Places `componentRestrictions.country` — no new prop needed.
- **Not built (ask before adding):** true polygon/geofence delivery zones (zones still match on free-text area/postal-code strings, per section 5 rule — reuse `matchDeliveryZone`, do not invent a second zone system); a staff UI to draw/set branch geo-coordinates (locations already have `latitude`/`longitude` columns and admin `LocationInput` already accepts them — only the admin locations form would need map fields, not a schema change).
- **Bug, two fix attempts, real root cause found 2026-09-27: `LocationPicker` crashed the whole checkout page with `NotFoundError: Failed to execute 'removeChild' on 'Node'`, cascading into `Uncaught Error: Rendered more hooks than during the previous render`.** The hooks error is a **symptom, not a separate bug** — every component on the page was audited line by line and none conditionally calls a hook; React's own fiber bookkeeping gets corrupted when a DOM mutation throws mid-commit, and the *next* render after that throws the hooks-mismatch as an unrelated-looking secondary crash. So the real fix is only ever about the `removeChild` error. First attempt (still worth keeping, but insufficient alone): `LocationPicker` mounts/unmounts every time checkout toggles between a saved address and "new address" without tearing down its Google Maps objects on unmount, so an in-flight geocode/dragend/`place_changed` callback could land after unmount and touch removed DOM nodes — fixed with a `mountedRef` guard on every async Maps callback and a real cleanup (`google.maps.event.clearInstanceListeners`, `marker.setMap(null)`, nulling every ref). **That fix did not stop the crash because it wasn't the actual cause.** The real cause: `<div ref={mapDivRef}>{status === "loading-script" ? <Loader2/> : null}</div>` — `new google.maps.Map(mapDivRef.current, ...)` takes ownership of that div's contents and mutates them directly and imperatively, while React *also* believed it owned that div's one child (the loading spinner) and tried to update/remove it independently. The instant Maps touches that node, React's picture of its children is stale; the next time React's own reconciler tries to remove what it thinks is still there, the node is already gone/moved, and `removeChild` throws mid-commit — which is what corrupts the fiber tree and produces the "more hooks" error on the following render. **Fixed by a strict DOM-ownership rule, not a workaround**: both `mapDivRef` and the new `autocompleteContainerRef` (see below) are *bare* containers that never receive React-rendered children — any loading/status UI is an absolutely-positioned **sibling** overlay in a wrapping `relative` div, never a child of the Maps-owned node. This is the general fix for this entire bug class: **never render React children inside a container a third-party imperative library (Maps, D3, a jQuery plugin, ...) also mutates** — reuse this sibling-overlay pattern, don't put a loading spinner or placeholder *inside* the library's own container again.
- **Same change: migrated off deprecated `google.maps.places.Autocomplete`.** Google closed it to new customers March 2025 (existing usages still work, but recommends `PlaceAutocompleteElement`). `location-picker.tsx` now creates `new google.maps.places.PlaceAutocompleteElement({includedRegionCodes, placeholder, noInputIcon: true})` and mounts it into `autocompleteContainerRef` (imperatively, `replaceChildren` — same bare-container rule as the map), listens for `gmp-select` (`AbortController` for cleanup instead of a manual listener reference), and on selection calls `event.placePrediction.toPlace().fetchFields({fields: ["location"]})` for just the coordinates, then **reuses the existing `placeMarker`/`reverseGeocode` pipeline** (the classic, non-deprecated `Geocoder`) to resolve the full address — so search, drag-the-pin and "current location" all resolve an address through the exact same code path instead of maintaining two parsers for two different Place-field shapes (legacy `address_components`/`long_name` vs. the new API's `addressComponents`/`longText`). Requires no new npm package or API version — `PlaceAutocompleteElement` ships in the same `libraries=places` bundle as the legacy widget; `@types/google.maps` ≥ 3.66 has its types.
- **Same change: fixed "Google Maps JavaScript API has been loaded directly without loading=async."** `loadGoogleMaps()`'s script URL gained `&loading=async` (Google's own recommended query param, alongside the `<script>` tag's existing `async` attribute) — the module-level `mapsLoader` promise singleton (one script tag for the whole page, reused by checkout and the profile drawer's address book) was already correct and is unchanged.
- **"Use current location" (reworked 2026-10-12, `location-picker.tsx#locateCustomer`):**
  - **`getCurrentPosition` runs synchronously in the tap (2026-10-12).** An earlier version awaited `navigator.permissions.query` first. That loses the user gesture Safari needs to show its "wants to use your location" prompt. The Permissions API is now only read AFTER a `PERMISSION_DENIED`, to pick the message: `denied` → blocked for this site; anything else → the device/OS refuses the browser (iOS Location Services → Safari Websites, macOS/Windows location off) → "Turn on Location Services for your browser…".
  - **In a bottom sheet (phones):** the map frame stops native `pointerdown` propagation, so panning the map or dragging the pin no longer drags the drag-to-dismiss `Sheet` (motion listens natively on the sheet; a React handler is too late). Playwright touch test: a drag on the map moves the sheet 0 px, the same drag on its title moves it 54 px.
  - **iOS input zoom:** `ui/input.tsx#inputStyles` is `text-base` (16px) on phones, `sm:text-sm` above. 15px made iOS zoom into every Input/Select/Textarea and stay zoomed. Google's search element gets `font-size: 16px` on `(pointer: coarse)`.
  - **Testing on a phone needs HTTPS:** a phone opening the dev box by LAN IP over `http://` is not a secure context, and browsers refuse location there without prompting (the picker says so). Use a tunnel (cloudflared / ngrok) or a deployed preview. Also check which server is on :3000: `next start` serves the last build, not current code; verify on a throwaway `next dev -p 3100` with `NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify`.
  - **Order of checks:** no `navigator.geolocation` → "not supported". Not `isSecureContext` → "needs https" (a phone opening the dev box by LAN IP over http is silently denied). Permissions API says `denied` → the "Location is blocked… set Location to Allow, then tap Try again" message without calling the API (the browser will not prompt again). Otherwise `getCurrentPosition`, which is what shows the browser's own permission prompt.
  - **Accuracy:** precise GPS first (10 s); on timeout / unavailable it retries with network location (`enableHighAccuracy: false`, 15 s, `maximumAge` 5 min), because laptops and indoor phones often have no GPS fix.
  - **Errors:** each failure says what to do (allow the site, turn on device Location/GPS, try again) with a "Try again" link.
  - **After "blocked":** it retries by itself once access is granted: the permission `change` event, plus a re-check on window focus / `visibilitychange`, because Chrome does not fire `change` for every settings path.
  - **Reverse geocode fails** (e.g. billing off): `onResolve` still gets the coordinates with empty address fields. Checkout, the profile address form and admin merge field by field, so the pin is kept and typed fields stay. In the branch location sheet, "Deliver here" then reads "Add your area and city below", and the typed-area submit carries that pin.
  - **Browser-tested with Playwright** (`permissions` / `grantPermissions`, `geolocation`): allowed → pin; blocked → message + Try again; allowed + focus → auto-retry. `Browser.setPermission` over CDP did NOT change what the page saw; use `ctx.grantPermissions`.
  - **As of 2026-10-12 the `.env.local` key returned `REQUEST_DENIED: You must enable Billing on the Google Cloud Project`** for Google's Geocoding Service — this is the reason reverse geocoding was migrated off Google entirely (2026-10-14, see the Nominatim subsection below); the Maps JavaScript API/Places key is unaffected and still needs only billing-free Maps JavaScript API + Places API enabled.
- **"Pin placed, but we couldn't look up the street address" is now a Nominatim failure, not Google's.** The console logs `[LocationPicker] reverse geocode failed: <error>` from `location-picker.tsx`, or the server logs `[geocoding] reverse geocode request failed/returned <status>` from `server/services/geocoding.ts` — check that one next (network egress to `nominatim.openstreetmap.org`, or a misconfigured `NOMINATIM_BASE_URL` if self-hosting).
- **`PlaceAutocompleteElement` renders as a black box on a light page when the OS is in dark mode**, because it themes itself from the OS. `LocationPicker` sets the element's `color-scheme` from its container's computed value, and sets `background: transparent; border: none` so the container's styling shows.
- **Follow-up fix: `TypeError: e.maps.Map is not a constructor`.** With `loading=async`, the script's `onload` does not mean the API is ready. At that point `google.maps` can lack `Map`/`Geocoder`/`Marker` or even `importLibrary` itself, which caused a follow-up `TypeError: e is not a function`. Readiness is signalled only by the `&callback=__locationPickerMapsReady` URL param. After that, the classes come from `google.maps.importLibrary(...)`, called on `google.maps` (not destructured). `loadGoogleMaps()` waits for that callback, then awaits `importLibrary("maps" | "geocoding" | "marker")` (plus `"places"`, where a failure only warns because search is optional) before resolving. A failed load also resets the singleton so the next mount can retry. Any new Maps consumer must go through `loadGoogleMaps()` and must not check `window.google?.maps` directly.
- **Bug found 2026-09-28: the whole picker (map, current-location, drag-pin) would flash the loading spinner then silently disappear (`status` → `"unavailable"`) whenever *anything* inside the init `.then()` threw — most likely `PlaceAutocompleteElement`'s constructor, e.g. before "Places API (New)" finishes propagating on a freshly-restricted key (Cloud Console: up to 5 minutes) — and the outer `.catch(() => setStatus("unavailable"))` swallowed the real error with no console output, so there was nothing to debug from.** Fixed: the `PlaceAutocompleteElement` setup is now in its own inner `try/catch` (a search-widget failure no longer takes down the map/current-location/drag-pin, which don't depend on it), and both that catch and the outer one now `console.error`/`console.warn` the actual thrown error. **If the picker still disappears, open the browser console — it now names the real cause** (a `RefererNotAllowedMapError`/`ApiNotActivatedMapError` from Maps itself, or a `[LocationPicker]` log from this component) instead of failing silently.
- **Bug found and fixed in the same change: `apple-icon.tsx` (Next's file-convention icon route) serves at a content-hashed URL** (e.g. `/apple-icon-1sjdum`, confirmed via `next build` output and `.next/server/app/...`), but `layout.tsx#generateMetadata` hardcoded the unhashed `/apple-icon` — a 404 on every restaurant, not just one. Fixed by moving the icon to a plain route handler at a fixed path (`apple-touch-icon.png/route.tsx`, same pattern as `manifest.webmanifest/route.ts` — a literal dotted folder name, not one of Next's reserved convention filenames, so no hash). `layout.tsx` now points `icons.apple` at `/apple-touch-icon.png`. **Do not reintroduce `icon.tsx`/`apple-icon.tsx`/`opengraph-image.tsx`/etc. file-convention routes when `generateMetadata` also needs to link the same URL explicitly** — those conventions always hash their served path; use a plain `route.ts`/`route.tsx` at a fixed name instead.
- **Bug found and fixed in the same change: booking a table while signed in failed with the generic "That record already exists."** `customers` has **two independent unique keys**: `(restaurant_id, phone)` (`customers_restaurant_id_phone_key`) and `(restaurant_id, lower(email))` (`customers_restaurant_email_key`) — the second one is real (confirmed against the hosted DB with `pg_constraint`; SKILL.md previously, wrongly, called it a plain index). `repositories/reservations.ts#createReservation` always resolved the customer via `upsertCustomer` (phone-based), even for a signed-in visitor whose account already has a customer row keyed by their account **email**. If they typed a phone that didn't match their account's phone on file, `upsertCustomer` saw "no row for this phone" and tried to `INSERT` a second row — which then collided with the account's own email and threw the generic 23505 message, with no hint it was actually an email clash, not a phone one. Root-caused by dry-running the real insert in a rolled-back transaction against the hosted DB (`begin; ...; rollback;`), which reproduced `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "customers_restaurant_email_key"` directly. **Fixed by giving reservations the same identity resolution checkout already had**: `repositories/orders.ts`'s `attachAccountCustomer` (looks the account up **by id**, updates its phone only if it has none yet, refused with a clean CONFLICT if that phone belongs to a different account) moved to `repositories/customers.ts` and is now exported and shared; `reservations.ts#createReservation` takes `accountCustomerId`/`saveAccountPhone` and calls it exactly like `orders.ts` does, instead of ever re-deriving a signed-in visitor's identity from a phone field. `upsertCustomer` itself also got a smaller, separate fix: its old "select, then insert-if-missing" was non-atomic (two near-simultaneous **guest** requests for the same new phone could both insert and race each other) — it is now one `insert ... on conflict (restaurant_id, phone) do update ...` statement. **Known remaining gap:** a *guest* (no account) reusing an email already tied to a different phone's guest row on the same restaurant can still hit the email constraint via `upsertCustomer` — rare in practice (matches the pre-existing 2026-09-22-era behavior), not fixed here; ask before building a two-key guest upsert.
- **Reservations require sign-in only to submit, not to browse (2026-09-27; corrected same day — see below).** Unlike checkout (gated at the page level), `reservation/page.tsx` renders for guests too — a guest can pick a date/time/guests and fill in name/phone/email freely. The gate is on the **"Request table" submit**, not the page: `reservation-form.tsx#handleSubmit` checks `isSignedIn` first and, if false, immediately sends the browser to `signInHref(slug, "/reservation")` with a "Please sign in first to book a table" toast, before running any field validation or calling the server (client-side state — the chosen date/time/guests — is lost on that round trip; only the server-side cart survives an equivalent redirect in checkout, there is no equivalent for a reservation draft). `server/services/reservations.ts#bookTable` also throws `SIGN_IN_REQUIRED` itself if `visitor.customerId` is missing (the client check is only UX; this is the real enforcement, same split as `assertCanPlaceOrder`/`placeOrderAction`), and the form's server-response handler redirects on that code too, as a fallback for a session that expires between page load and submit. Reservations do **not** require a *verified* email to submit — only sign-in. The form uses the same `PhoneInput` (E.164, country-code picker) and locked-field pattern as `checkout-form.tsx`: a saved phone or a verified account email is shown read-only ("· from your account") for a signed-in customer; a guest (or an unverified account) gets plain, editable fields, same as before this change. `bookTableSchema.guestPhone` changed from `contactPhone` to `e164Phone` to match. `services/reservations.ts#bookTable` resolves a signed-in visitor's account by id (`attachAccountCustomer`, above) and — mirroring `checkout.ts#placeOrder`'s `savedPhone`/`email` precedence exactly — always uses the account's own saved phone/email over whatever the client sent when one is on file, so a locked field can never be spoofed by editing the request payload. **First attempt at this feature wrongly gated the whole page** (`redirect()` in `page.tsx` before any guest could see the form) — reverted same day once the actual requirement (gate the button, not the page) was clarified; if this needs revisiting, gate the submit, not the route.

## 22. Sales Reports (admin)

`/admin/reports` (nav group "Business", permission `analytics.view` — already existed in both the SQL and TS permission catalogues for owner/admin/manager, so no migration was needed). Summary cards (total sales, total orders, completed, cancelled, average order value, total discounts), a daily sales-trend line chart and an orders-per-day bar chart (`recharts`, already an installed-but-unused dependency — no new package), a payment-method breakdown (completed orders only), a 10-row "most recent in range" table, and a CSV export button. Date filter: preset pills (Today/Yesterday/Last 7/Last 30/This month/Previous month, `AdminStatusTabs`, reused) plus a plain GET `<form>` for a custom `from`/`to` range — everything is a query param (`?preset=&from=&to=`), no client state.

- **`shared/reports.ts`** (pure, isomorphic): `resolveReportRange(preset, from, to, timezone)` turns a preset or custom pair into restaurant-local calendar dates (`fromDateKey`/`toDateKey`, inclusive both ends; uses `shared/hours.ts#zonedNow` for "today" in the restaurant's own timezone, not the server's) — shared by the page and the export route so a download always matches what the page is showing. `buildSalesCsv(rows, restaurant)` builds the CSV text (CRLF, Excel-friendly, quoted cells with commas/quotes/newlines).
- **`repositories/orders.ts#getSalesAnalytics`**: four queries (overall summary, status breakdown, payment-method breakdown, daily trend) sharing one date-range predicate and one params array so the boundary logic cannot drift between them. The range is computed in SQL (`($2::date)::timestamp at time zone $4` .. `($3::date + 1)::timestamp at time zone $4`), never in JS, so "today" always means today in the restaurant's IANA timezone regardless of where the server runs. **"Sales" always means `status = 'completed'` orders only** — cancelled and in-progress orders are counted in `totalOrders`/`statusBreakdown` but never in `totalSales`/`totalDiscounts`/the payment breakdown/the trend's `sales` series, so a cancelled order can never inflate revenue (the exact mistake item 1 of this skill's bug list warns about, just for reporting instead of a status badge).
- **Export, three formats** (`reports/export/route.ts`, GET, same `analytics.view` permission, `?format=csv|xlsx|pdf`, defaults to csv): all three read the exact same rows (`getOrdersForExport`, reusing `listOrders`'s existing `dateFrom`/`dateTo` filter, page size 5000, rather than a second query) so they can never disagree with each other. `OrderSummary` gained optional `subtotal`/`discountAmount`/`taxAmount` (only populated by `listOrders`) so every export format can show them without a second query shape. **Note the asymmetry**: `listOrders`'s date filter casts its params straight to `::timestamptz` (the session/connection default timezone), while `getSalesAnalytics` (the summary cards/charts) converts through the restaurant's own timezone. Near a local midnight boundary the export's row set and the dashboard's totals can disagree by up to the UTC offset; left as-is rather than changing `listOrders`'s existing filter (used by the Orders list page too) for this one caller.
  - **CSV**: `shared/reports.ts#buildSalesCsv` (pure, isomorphic — no new dependency).
  - **XLSX**: `server/services/report-exports.ts#buildSalesXlsxBuffer`, `exceljs` (added for this feature; chosen over the `xlsx`/SheetJS package because current npm `xlsx` is stuck on an old, unpatched-on-npm release — SheetJS moved newer releases to their own CDN after a dispute with npm's policies). `package.json` has `"overrides": {"uuid": "^11.1.1"}` because `exceljs` pins an old `uuid` with a (here, inapplicable — exceljs never passes the vulnerable `buf` option) moderate advisory; the override silences it without a breaking exceljs downgrade. One worksheet, typed/number-formatted money columns, autofilter.
  - **PDF**: `server/services/report-exports.ts#buildSalesPdfBuffer`, `pdfkit` (+ `@types/pdfkit` dev dependency) — landscape A4, a summary strip (same figures as the page's stat cards) then a hand-drawn table (pdfkit has no built-in table layout) that paginates itself when a page fills up. No headless-browser/HTML-to-PDF dependency (`puppeteer` etc.) was needed for a report this simple.
  - Both buffer-builders are framework-free (no Next/React) and take the already-fetched rows, same shape `buildSalesCsv` takes — verified once with an ad hoc `tsx` script against mock rows (XLSX's ZIP magic bytes, PDF's `%PDF-` header) since there is no local database to exercise the live route end-to-end (section 6).
- **Not built:** a dedicated customer-facing breakdown by order type (only payment-method and status are broken down); report caching (every view re-queries — fine at this data volume, revisit if a restaurant's order table gets large).

### Order sound notifications (admin)

New-order and cancelled-order chimes, audible on any admin page (not only `/orders`), gated behind `orders.view`.

**Event-driven (2026-10-17), polling only as a fallback.** Originally this polled `GET /admin/orders/activity` every 3s unconditionally — section 8's SSE stream was scoped to one order id, so a multi-order staff feed had no event source to follow. It now follows `GET /admin/orders/events` (SSE), the same LISTEN/NOTIFY infrastructure as the customer order-tracking page, extended to a restaurant-wide feed:

- **Migration 0042** re-adds `notify_order_insert` (`after insert on orders`, reverted once already in 0038 as "no consumer yet" — now it has one) and extends the `order_changes` NOTIFY payload with `order_number`, `location_id`, `created_at`, `is_new`. The single-order customer watcher (`watchOrderChanges`) ignores the extra fields and the new insert-time event (it filters by `id`, and no visitor is on a tracking page before that order exists).
- **`repositories/order-events.ts#watchRestaurantOrderActivity(restaurantId, handlers)`**: same shared channel/connection as `watchOrderChanges`, filtered by `restaurant_id` instead of one order id.
- **`services/order-events.ts#openOrderActivityStream(restaurantId, locationId, ctx, sink)`**: subscribes first, catch-up query second (same ordering as `openOrderStream`, same reason — closes the race where an order commits between page-load and subscribe). The catch-up query reuses `listOrderActivitySince`/`getOrderActivitySince` (unchanged — still the poll fallback's query too) over a trailing margin (`ACTIVITY_CATCH_UP_MARGIN_MS`, same idea as the poll's old `CURSOR_SAFETY_MARGIN_MS`), run again from `onReset` if the shared LISTEN connection drops. A duplicate event is possible and harmless (the client already dedupes); a silent loss is not.
- **`orders/events/route.ts`** (GET, `orders.view`, SSE): same shape as `order/[orderNumber]/events/route.ts` — `retry:` line, one event per change (`event: activity`), a `: ping` every 25s, 503 if `DATABASE_URL_LISTEN` is not configured.
- **`orders/activity/route.ts`** (GET, `orders.view`) is unchanged and still exists — it's now only the fallback the client uses when the SSE route is unavailable, not the primary path.
- **`components/admin/order-sound-notifications.tsx`**: `OrderActivityWatcher` opens the `EventSource`; only when it closes immediately (403, or 503 because `DATABASE_URL_LISTEN` isn't set — browsers don't retry a closed `EventSource`) does it start the old `setInterval(poll, POLL_MS)` fallback. A transient drop is left to the browser's own SSE reconnect (`retry: 5000`), not the poll. `handleEvents` (dedupe by `orderNumber:status:isNew`, chime, `pageShowsActivity` refresh, activity-count banner) is shared by both transports — one event at a time from SSE, a batch from the poll — so the behavior described below is identical either way regardless of which transport is active.
- `SoundToggleButton` (bell icon, `AdminHeader`, next to Sign out) is a **per-browser** preference (`localStorage rp_admin_sound_enabled`, default on) — deliberately not a `restaurants.settings` key, because this is "does this device make noise," not a tenant-wide business setting. A `CustomEvent` (`rp-admin-sound-pref`) keeps every mounted instance in the same tab in sync (the native `storage` event only fires cross-tab).
- **The list staying stale after the chime (reported 2026-10-10, predates the SSE switch):** `OrderActivityWatcher` already called `router.refresh()` when `pageShowsActivity` said the current screen shows one of the changed orders (dashboard/orders/kitchen/that order's page), but a staff member could still see the chime play with the list not visibly updating — a background refresh giving no feedback if it is ever slow or coalesced is not provable from reading the code alone, so rather than chase that blind, the fix makes the *feedback* reliable instead of trusting the silent auto-refresh as the only signal: `useOrderActivityCount()`/`bumpActivityCount`/`resetActivityCount` (same `CustomEvent` pattern as the sound preference) track new/cancelled events seen since last acknowledged. `OrderActivityBanner` (rendered in `(dashboard)/layout.tsx`'s `<main>`, above `{children}`, every admin page) shows "`N new orders — tap to refresh`" and on click forces `router.refresh()` from a real user gesture plus resets the count — a guaranteed-to-work escape hatch that never depends on the background transport. The sidebar (`admin-sidebar.tsx`'s `NavList`) shows the same live count as a pulsing red "`N new`" badge on Dashboard/Orders/Kitchen (replacing the static `activeOrders` badge on Orders while there is unseen activity); clicking any of those three links calls `resetActivityCount()` too, since navigating there is a real navigation and shows fresh data regardless of the transport. The count is deliberately NOT auto-reset when the background refresh fires — only a tap or a navigation to one of those three screens clears it.
- **Autoplay:** browsers refuse to start an `AudioContext` before a user gesture on the page; this is accepted, not worked around (per the brief: "do not attempt to bypass browser security restrictions"). The toggle button's own click is a gesture and resumes/creates the shared `AudioContext` immediately (and plays an audible confirmation chime), so sound works as soon as a staff member has clicked anything once per tab — no separate "enable audio" prompt was needed.
- **No duplicate/repeat sounds:** `handleEvents`' dedupe set covers both transports; an order that changes status twice between two SSE events (or two polls) still only produces whichever single event is current (acceptable — this is a notification, not an audit trail; the admin orders list/detail page is the audit trail).
- **Tone is a short repeating pattern (~1.5s), not one blip** (changed 2026-10-03, at the requester's direction — a single quick beep was too easy to miss on a busy counter): `playChime` plays the same two-note (new order) / single-note (cancelled) pulse three times with gaps, still two clean sine tones rather than a harsh buzzer, and still bounded (not a looping alarm) per the "don't make it annoying" rule.
- Tests: `tests/order-events.test.ts` (`openOrderActivityStream` describe block — subscribe-before-catch-up ordering, tenant/branch filtering, reconnect re-sync, no leak on a failed catch-up read; plus static checks that the admin route only sends a keep-alive `setInterval`, and the watcher's poll fallback is gated on `readyState === EventSource.CLOSED`, not unconditional).

### Reverse geocoding migrated from Google to OpenStreetMap Nominatim (2026-10-14)

Google's Geocoding Service started failing (`REQUEST_DENIED: You must enable Billing on the Google Cloud Project`, see the troubleshooting entry above). Google Maps itself (map display, marker drag, Places search) is unchanged — only the "turn these coordinates into an address" step moved off Google.

- **`server/services/geocoding.ts#reverseGeocode(lat, lng, signal?)`**: calls Nominatim's `/reverse?format=jsonv2&addressdetails=1`, server-side only (never called directly from the browser). Throttled to one request in flight per 1.1s across the whole process (`config.geocoding` → `NOMINATIM_BASE_URL`/`NOMINATIM_CONTACT`) — Nominatim's public-instance usage policy caps it at 1 req/s and requires a `User-Agent` identifying the app, which only a server-side `fetch` can set reliably (browsers refuse to let JS set `User-Agent`). Isolated in one file + one config section so a self-hosted Nominatim instance is just `NOMINATIM_BASE_URL` changed, no code change.
- **`app/api/geocode/reverse/route.ts`**: GET `?lat=&lng=` → `{ result: {...} | null }`. Thin proxy: validates coordinates (zod), applies the existing `checkRateLimit` helper (20/min per IP, independent of the Nominatim-side throttle, to stop one browser hammering our own endpoint) and never leaks a raw Nominatim error/status to the client — a failure or empty result is always `{ result: null }`, same contract the old `ZERO_RESULTS`/`REQUEST_DENIED` fallback had.
- **`location-picker.tsx`**: `reverseGeocode` now debounces 400ms (so a fast re-drag doesn't fire one request per `dragend`) and fetches `/api/geocode/reverse`; a request id + `AbortController` (aborts the previous in-flight fetch on every new call) means a slow, stale response can never overwrite a newer pin/search/current-location pick. No more `google.maps.Geocoder`/`geocoding` library import — `loadGoogleMaps()` now only loads `maps`/`marker`/`places`. A failed or empty lookup behaves exactly as before: the coordinates are kept, `onResolve` fires with empty address fields, and the customer types area/city manually — checkout/saved-address/branch-resolution code downstream is untouched since `ResolvedLocation`'s shape didn't change. Attribution text ("Address lookup © OpenStreetMap contributors") added below the picker per Nominatim's usage policy.
- **Not changed, by design:** delivery serviceability, distance ranking (`haversineKm`/`sortByDistance`) and branch resolution all key off the picked **coordinates**, never the geocoded address string — a reverse-geocode failure was already harmless to them before this migration and still is.
- **Bug found and fixed same week: a cantonment address resolved to the wrong "city" for zone matching (e.g. a Lahore Cantonment point came back as city `"Lahore Cant"`, not `"Lahore"`).** Nominatim's `address.city` for a cantonment area is the cantonment's own administrative name, not the city it belongs to — and that string is what checkout's city filter (`restaurant1s.city`, case-insensitive) matches against, so a customer physically in Lahore could be told there's no branch in "Lahore Cant". Fixed in `server/services/geocoding.ts#normalizeCity`: strips a trailing `Cant`/`Cantt`/`Cantonment` suffix before returning `city`, recovering the parent city name (`"Lahore Cant"`/`"Lahore Cantt"`/`"Lahore Cantonment"` → `"Lahore"`, `"Rawalpindi Cantt"` → `"Rawalpindi"`); an ordinary city name is untouched. Covered by `tests/geocoding.test.ts`.

### Bug: the order-tracking page's big status headline could say "Confirmed" for a cancelled order

Found 2026-10-03 from a screenshot: `/order/<number>`'s headline (`OrderPass` in `components/storefront/order-live-status.tsx`) showed red **"Confirmed"** with the caption "This order was cancelled." underneath — while the small status badge next to the order number correctly said "Cancelled". Two different data sources disagreed.

**Cause:** the headline rendered `current.label`, where `current` is the **timeline step** the order last reached before being cancelled (`buildOrderTimeline` marks that step `"cancelled"` but keeps its own name, e.g. "Confirmed" — see `shared/order-timeline.ts`), not the order's actual `status` field. The small badge (`OrderStatusBadge status={state.status}`) reads the real column and was always correct; only the headline's fallback label was wrong. Reproduces for any order cancelled after reaching a status whose label isn't "Pending" (e.g. confirmed → cancelled, preparing → cancelled): the headline shows whichever stage it got cancelled at, in red, instead of "Cancelled".

**Fix:** one line — the headline now checks `cancelled` first and renders `ORDER_STATUS_LABELS.cancelled` directly, falling back to the step label only when the order is not cancelled. The stations rail below is unaffected (and correctly still labels each station by its own name — "cancelled at the Confirmed stage" is meaningful there, just not as the giant headline). No other order-status surface had this bug (audited every other badge/label site — admin dashboard/orders/kitchen, My Orders/Current Orders cards, the admin reservation badge — all already read the real `status` field; see the admin reservation-badge fix logged the same day).

### Settings UI: Payments vs. Ordering's self-cancellation checklist looked like a duplicate

Reported 2026-10-03: the Payments card's "Enabled methods" checklist and the Ordering card's "Eligible payment methods" checklist (self-cancellation, section 5 rule 14) both render a grid of `PAYMENT_METHODS` checkboxes and looked like the same control shown twice. They are not — one is "what customers can pay with," the other is "which of those payments are safe to let a customer cancel themselves without a refund." Fixed, presentation only, same two settings sections/save semantics as before:

- `settings/page.tsx` now renders `PaymentsSettingsSection` immediately before `OrderingSection` in the grid (same row, side by side) instead of leaving them diagonally separated by `ServiceFeeSection`/`TaxSection` — proximity alone removes most of the "did I just see this already" reaction.
- `OrderingSection` takes a new `enabledPaymentMethods: PaymentMethod[]` prop (`restaurant.settings.payments.enabledMethods`, read from the same `restaurant` object the page already has — no extra query) and the self-cancellation checklist now only offers methods actually enabled in the Payments card, instead of all `PAYMENT_METHODS` regardless of whether the restaurant even accepts them. A stale `allowedPaymentMethods` entry for a since-disabled method is harmless and left alone (an order's `paymentMethod` is historical; `canCustomerCancelOrder` only ever compares against the specific order's own payment method).
- The block itself is now a tinted callout (`bg-[var(--tint)]`, an `Info` icon, bold heading) with copy that explicitly says "Not a payment setting" and points at the Payments card by name, instead of a plain `border-t` divider that read as "more of the same list above."

## 23. Staff management (admin, `/admin/staff`)

Full CRUD over `team_members`: list, add, edit (name/phone/role), enable/disable, remove. Nav item "Staff" (Business group, `staff.view`/`staff.manage` — both permissions already existed in the catalogue, unused until now; no migration needed). Reuses the exact list+inline-form UX convention every other admin CRUD screen uses (`LocationManager`/`CouponManager` pattern): `components/admin/staff-manager.tsx`, `server/services/team.ts` (new — the repository functions `listTeamMembers`/`createTeamMember`/`updateTeamMember`/`setTeamMemberActive`/`deleteTeamMember` already existed in `repositories/team.ts`, written earlier, called by nothing until this page), `server/validation/team.ts` (added `createTeamMemberSchema`/`updateTeamMemberSchema` next to the existing `staffLoginSchema`).

- **Safety rails already enforced by the repository** (not new): `setTeamMemberActive` refuses to disable the last active owner of a restaurant (`CONFLICT`, friendly message); `deleteTeamMember` refuses to delete any owner at all. The UI also disables the enable/disable and remove buttons on the signed-in staff member's own row (`isSelf`, compared by `userId`) — you cannot deactivate or remove yourself from this screen, even though the repository would in fact allow a non-owner to deactivate themselves.
- **Editing never touches `auth.users`** — only `team_members` columns (role, name, phone, active flag). Always works, in every environment.
- **Creating a brand-new login works through the web UI on hosted Supabase too (2026-10-03), via the Supabase Auth Admin API — not the owner/migrator connection.** `createTeamMember` still tries the direct SQL path first (`insert into auth.users` — works as-is on local/plain Postgres and for `npm run db:seed`/tests); on hosted Supabase that insert fails with Postgres `42501` (`app_service` has no real INSERT grant there — section 17's "auth schema gotcha", DECISIONS.md section 26, both confirmed by a live test). `server/services/team.ts#addTeamMember` catches exactly that code and, when `config.storage.supabase` is configured (the same `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` pair Storage already uses — **no new secret**), falls back to `server/integrations/supabase-auth.ts#createSupabaseAuthUser`, which creates the login through GoTrue's own HTTP Admin API (`POST {supabaseUrl}/auth/v1/admin/users`, service-role bearer token, `fetch` only — no `@supabase/supabase-js` dependency, same hand-rolled convention as `integrations/fcm.ts`/`google.ts`) instead of raw SQL. The returned `auth.users.id` is then attached to a `team_members` row by `repositories/team.ts#attachTeamMemberToUser` (factored out of `createTeamMember`'s second half; never touches `auth.users` itself, so it always succeeds through the normal `app_service` write path). **This is the fix DECISIONS.md section 26 named but never built** ("move user creation to Supabase's Auth Admin API"). The `npm run db:create-staff` CLI (owner/migrator connection) is now only the last-resort fallback — surfaced with an actionable message — for an environment with neither a working direct insert nor Supabase configured (shouldn't happen in practice: Supabase is configured wherever hosted Postgres is). **Still never** route the web action itself through `DATABASE_URL_MIGRATOR` (DECISIONS.md section 9: owner credentials are for an offline script only). Adding a team member whose email **already has** an `auth.users` row (e.g. inviting an existing staff login to a second restaurant) never hits either fallback — `createTeamMember`'s existing-user branch handles it directly, on any database.
- **Password field on create** is a plain temporary password the admin sets and hands to the new hire directly (no email invite flow exists); there is no "send reset link" affordance yet.
## 22. Branch-scoped staff + per-branch menu availability (2026-10-01)

`team_members.location_id` existed since section 2's schema but was write-only (repository set it, nothing ever read it) until this change. It is now enforced in RLS, not app code — the same pattern as `restaurant_id` tenant isolation.

- **`team_members.location_id = null` means HQ/all-branch staff** (owner/admin — see below); a value scopes that staff member to exactly one branch. `db/migrations/0027_branch_scoping.sql` adds `app.member_location_id(restaurant_id)` (mirrors `app.team_role`) and `app.can_access_location(restaurant_id, location_id)` (true when the caller is HQ, the row has no location, or they match), then rewrites the team RLS policies on `orders`, `reservations`, `order_items`, `payments`, `deliveries` (via their `exists (select 1 from orders o where ...)` subqueries — same root cause as `orders` itself, fixed in the same migration) to AND that check onto the existing `has_permission(...)` check. **Same migration also added `order_status_history`'s first-ever team select policy** — it previously had none (only a customer-select and the `orders.manage` insert-check), so staff could never read status history via RLS at all; found while auditing every table keyed off an order.
- **Zero app-layer filtering needed**: `listOrdersForStaff`/`listReservationsForStaff`/etc. are unchanged — RLS alone restricts the rows a branch-scoped session can see, read or write, exactly like `restaurant_id` isolation already worked. Admin list pages just display the branch (`OrderSummary.locationName`, already existed on `Reservation`/`Order`; `orders` list query gained a `left join restaurant1s l` + `location_name` to populate it on `OrderSummary` too).
- **Seed data pitfall (found while building this): every restaurant's seed script previously gave every team member the same single `location_id`, including owner/admin.** Harmless before this change (the column was inert); once RLS reads it, that seed pattern would have wrongly branch-locked every owner/admin account. Fixed in the main `scripts/db/seed.ts` (owner/admin → `null`, manager/staff → a real branch each, as the reference pattern) and corrected on the already-seeded hosted dev DB with a one-off `update team_members set location_id = null where role in ('owner','admin')`. **The four other demo restaurants' seed scripts (`zaytoun`/`blue-hour`/`villa-grand-buffet`/`bun-intended`) still have the old give-everyone-one-location pattern** — not fixed there (out of scope, not the restaurant used for admin testing), but the same fix applies if their owner/admin logins ever need HQ-wide access. `scripts/db/create-staff.ts` gained `--location <branch-slug>` to assign this correctly for any new staff account; omit it for HQ roles.
- **Per-branch menu availability**: new table `menu_item_location_overrides (location_id, menu_item_id, is_available)`, unique per (location, item), RLS mirrors `menu_items` (`menu.view`/`menu.manage` team policies + a public-read policy). `repositories/menu.ts#orderableItemsSql` (the one query shared by `loadOrderableItems` and `createOrder`'s single combined read, section 20) takes an optional third `locationParam` and folds the override into the `is_available` column it already selects — `resolveMenuSelection`'s existing `ITEM_UNAVAILABLE` check then rejects it for free, no new error path. `createOrder` passes its existing `$5` (`input.locationId`) placeholder through — no new bound param needed. Admin: item edit page shows a "Branch availability" toggle list (`components/admin/branch-availability-manager.tsx`) when the restaurant has more than one location, calling `setItemLocationAvailabilityAction` → `services/menu-admin.ts#setItemLocationAvailability` → invalidates the storefront cache same as every other menu write.
- **The storefront's "choose your branch before ordering" flow is section 23** (built 2026-10-01, behind `features.BranchingFeature`). Still not built: a staff-management admin UI to assign `location_id` through the admin panel (staff management UI is excluded entirely so far, per section 17) — branch assignment goes through `create-staff.ts --location`, same as role assignment today.
- **Testing:** `tests/branch-isolation.test.ts` (DB test, same `testDatabase`/seed-fixture pattern as `tests/tenant-isolation.test.ts` — needs the disposable test Postgres per section 12, not runnable against just the hosted dev DB) proves a branch-scoped manager/staff sees only their branch's orders and an HQ account sees all. Manually verified against the hosted dev DB with a rolled-back-transaction smoke script (`begin; set_config('app.current_user_id', ...); ...; rollback;`, same technique `db:verify` uses) since no local Postgres exists here (section 6 gap) — confirmed Gulberg-scoped/DHA-scoped/HQ logins each see exactly the rows they should.

## 23. Multi-branch ordering (`features.BranchingFeature`, 2026-10-01)

One flag in the existing `restaurants.features` JSON (`shared/contract/settings.ts`, default `false`; the admin Settings → Features checkbox "Multi-branch ordering"; saving merges into the stored object, `services/restaurants.ts#updateRestaurantFeatures`, so no other flag is touched). **No migration and no new table**: it connects what sections 20–22 already had. Off or missing = the single-location flow (`getBranching` returns `null`, the provider renders only its children, every gate answers "ok").

```
Home (no add buttons, tiles -> /menu#<category>)
  -> Menu: BranchBar asks for a delivery location (or reuses the remembered one)
  -> setDestinationAction: destination cookie + branches that serve it, nearest first   (snapshot, no DB)
  -> nearest branch -> tray cookie's locationId -> router.refresh()
  -> menu shows that branch's availability, add controls unlock
  -> Cart ("Ordering from <branch>") -> Checkout (branch fixed, address prefilled) -> createOrder (re-checks zone + availability for that branch)
```

- **Every order has the branch that cooks it (`createOrder`, fixed 2026-10-08 audit).** `placeOrder` passes the tray cookie's `locationId`, which `createOrder` only validated when present. With the feature on, a cart cookie without a branch (stale tab, edited cookie, a tray from before the flag) placed a pickup order with no branch, or a delivery order priced from whichever branch's zone matched first, with `location_id = null`.
