- Server: `updatePaymentStatusAction` (orders/actions.ts) → `services/orders.ts#changePaymentStatus`: reads the order as the staff member (RLS: own restaurant, own branch for branch staff), re-checks the rule, and writes with `setOrderPaymentStatus(..., { expectedStatus })` — the update only applies if the payment is still what was read (two clicks at once: one wins, the other gets CONFLICT). Paid sets `paid_at`; back to pending/failed clears it; refunded sets `refunded_at`. The `payment_status` change reaches live order tracking through the existing NOTIFY trigger. No new permission or migration.
- Tests: `tests/payment-status-manual.test.ts` (rule table; real DB: paid then refunded, refunded is final, concurrent clicks, another branch's manager and another restaurant's owner refused).
- Open item: in an automated Playwright run against an isolated production build, admin actions that revalidate (this one, order status on the list and detail page) sometimes saved but the browser did not apply the re-rendered page (spinner kept going, reload showed the new status); the owner reports it synced on the real app. If it recurs, update the control from the action's own result instead of waiting for the revalidated page.

## 29. Legal pages: Privacy Policy and Terms of Service (per restaurant, 2026-10-12)

`/privacy` and `/terms`, linked from every storefront footer's bottom bar (`site-footer.tsx`, built from the current restaurant's slug). No migration, no new table, no DB read.

- **Isolation:** the pages go through `ConfiguredPage` → `requireStorefront(slug)` (the snapshot; any other slug is 404, one restaurant per instance) and `getPageContent(restaurant.id, "privacy"|"terms")`; every value in the document comes from that one `context.restaurant`. Verified live: `blue-hour/privacy|terms` 200 with only Blue Hour's details, `zaytoun/...` 404 on the same instance.
- **Default text = a template filled from the restaurant's own data** (`shared/legal.ts`, pure): `legalFactsFor` (legal name else name, `config.contact.email` else `restaurants.email` like the footer, phone, primary-branch address with repeated parts dropped, country name from the ISO code) + `buildLegalDocument(kind, facts)`. It states only what the storefront really does: `server/services/legal.ts#getLegalDocument` feeds it `getCheckoutOptions` (the exact order types and payment methods checkout offers), the effective online provider (Stripe/JazzCash named only when card/wallet is really offered), email/push channels (`getPushClientConfigFor`), Google sign-in (`googleAuthAvailable`), Maps (`config.maps`), the self-cancellation rule, minimum order, and the reservations/reviews/coupons/loyalty switches; sections for features that are off are left out. No retention periods or promises are invented. The service exists because components may not import `server/config` or the notifications service (architecture test). Bump `LEGAL_TEMPLATE_UPDATED` when the wording changes ("Last updated").
- **A restaurant's own wording:** super admin → `/super-admin/<slug>/website` → the Pages panel lists Privacy Policy / Terms of Service as **Built-in** rows while the restaurant has no such page; **Customize** (`customizeLegalPageAction` → `services/platform.ts#customizeLegalPage`) creates a DRAFT `privacy` / `terms` `website_pages` row whose `rich_text` sections hold the current template text (`shared/legal.ts#legalDocumentSections`, one section per heading, bullets as `• ` paragraphs, built from that restaurant's rows: `getPrimaryLocation` + `website.config.contact.email`), then opens it in the page editor; the storefront keeps the template until it is published. Once customized the text is static (it no longer follows feature changes). Same row can be written by SQL (`website_pages`: `slug`, `title`, `description`, `is_published`, `sections` = `[{"type":"rich_text","enabled":true,"title":…,"body":…}]`; body is plain text, blank line = paragraph, ≤ 8000 chars; restart or wait for the snapshot refresh). The slugs are deliberately NOT in `RESERVED_PAGE_SLUGS`. `ConfiguredPage` `ownContent` (a render hook; the legal page wraps the sections in the same short hero with the page's own title/description): visible sections and no `page_content` section = those sections ARE the page and the template is not rendered; with a `page_content` section the template sits there and its title/subtitle/image override the hero, as on menu/locations. Its `seo.title`/`description` feed the metadata. The page's `[pageSlug]` route is shadowed by the static routes, so an older CMS `privacy` page now renders through this path with the same sections.
- **UI:** `components/storefront/legal-page.tsx` (`LegalPage`, `legalPageMetadata`; title "Privacy Policy" / "Terms of Service" — the layout's title template adds the restaurant name), `PageHero size="sm"` on the brand-tinted night ground, then a sticky "On this page" rail (lg+) beside numbered sections with anchor ids, a cross-link to the other document, breadcrumb JSON-LD; `LegalPageSkeleton` for both `loading.tsx`. Theme tokens only. Checked at 375/1280 px: no horizontal overflow.
- **Tests:** `tests/legal.test.ts` (DB-free: only the given restaurant's name/contact/address appear, contact-email precedence, country name, checkout's methods and order types, provider only when offered, ordering off, cancellation rule, features off, processors only when configured, minimum order, unique anchors, address de-duplication).
- **Not built (ask first):** a consent line at sign-up/checkout linking these pages; a structured per-restaurant editor for the legal text (today: the generic section editor); the template is a sensible default, not legal advice — owners should have it reviewed for their jurisdiction.

## 30. Storefront templates (TEMPLATE_ID: 1 Classic 2D, 2 3D, 3 placeholder) (2026-10-12)

One template per deployment, chosen by `TEMPLATE_ID` (server env, read at runtime, so one build serves any template; restart after changing it). One instance serves one restaurant (section 15), so this is effectively the restaurant's template: cafés keep **1**, burger / Gen Z brands (Bun Intended) run **2**. Per-restaurant selection from the database is NOT built; if one instance ever serves several restaurants, move the id onto the restaurant (e.g. an entitlement or `websites.config`) and keep the same registry.

- **Selection:** `shared/templates.ts` (`TEMPLATE_IDS`, `TEMPLATES` with key `classic | depth | slot-3`, `resolveTemplateId`) → `server/config#template` (the only reader; unset, empty or anything but 1/2/3 → 1 plus one `[config]` warning per process, never a startup failure) → `web/template.ts#getActiveTemplate()` (server-only).
- **Templates own presentation only.** Routes, data, sections and copy are identical. `components/storefront/templates/registry.ts#TEMPLATE_SECTIONS` maps a template to section-type overrides; `SectionRenderer` uses the override when present, else the Classic component. The storefront layout sets `data-template="<key>"` on `.theme-root` for every template except 1 (so Classic's markup is byte-for-byte what it was) and mounts `DepthTilt` only for 2. Template CSS lives at the end of `globals.css`, scoped to `.theme-root[data-template="depth"]` (plus the `depth-*` classes only the 3D hero uses).
- **Template 2 (3D):** `templates/depth/hero-3d-section.tsx` (server; same hero content: eyebrow, title, subtitle, both CTAs, live opening line, highlights as a tilted marquee band; brand-lit stage + perspective grid floor) → `hero-stage.tsx` (client: capability check, IntersectionObserver pause, pointer/scroll fed through a ref, poster cross-fade) → `burger-scene.tsx` (loaded with `next/dynamic` `ssr:false`; R3F `Canvas`: a procedural burger from primitives — lathe buns, noisy flat-shaded patty, melting cheese plane, ruffled lettuce ring, tomato slices, 46 instanced sesame seeds — floating, turning, leaning to the pointer, squishing on tap, layers fanning apart on scroll; brand/accent confetti; radial-texture soft shadow, no shadow maps). Everywhere else: extruded section headings, dish cards (`article[data-dish].flex`) and gallery photos (`figure.group`) raised and tilting toward a mouse pointer (`depth-tilt.tsx`, one delegated rAF listener writing `--tilt-x/--tilt-y`), `.reveal` sections swinging up with `rotateX`.
- **Per-restaurant model:** hero section `modelUrl` (optional, `.catch(undefined)`; super-admin page editor field "3D model URL"): an https URL (must allow CORS) or a site path like `/images/<slug>/burger.glb`. Loaded with three's `GLTFLoader`, auto-centred and scaled; while loading or on any failure the built-in burger shows (`ModelBoundary`). Other templates ignore it. No DRACO/HDRI (both would fetch from external CDNs).
- **Fallbacks:** the server renders a poster (the hero photo, else the cover, as a tilted sticker card); the scene replaces it only when `canRender3d()` passes: WebGL present, not software-rendered (SwiftShader / llvmpipe / Basic Render — found in testing: CPU-only WebGL made page loads hang), no Save-Data, not ≤ 2 GB memory or ≤ 2 cores. `prefers-reduced-motion` = one still frame (`frameloop="demand"`), no tilt, static marquee/floor (global reduced-motion rule). Off-screen = `frameloop="never"`. `webglcontextlost` = back to the poster.
- **Deps (exact pins):** `three@0.186.1`, `@react-three/fiber@9.8.1` (the R3F line for React 19; peer `react <19.4`), dev `@types/three@0.186.0`. No drei. R3F 9.8 logs a harmless `THREE.Clock … deprecated` warning with three 0.186 (inside R3F, not our code).
- **Template 3:** registry slot only; renders Classic until designed. To design it: add overrides to `TEMPLATE_SECTIONS[3]` and styles under `[data-template="slot-3"]`.
- **Add a template:** an id in `TEMPLATE_IDS`/`TEMPLATES`, an entry in `TEMPLATE_SECTIONS`, scoped CSS, a line in `.env.example`.
- **Verify:** one isolated build (`NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_RESTAURANT=<slug>`), then several `next start -p 31xx` with different `TEMPLATE_ID`. Done 2026-10-12 on bun-intended: 1/2/3/unset/"9" → none/depth/slot-3/none+warning/none+warning; home, menu, cart, reviews, locations, reservation, privacy, terms, current-orders, orders, sign-in, checkout all 200 with no page errors on 1, 2 and 3; 3D canvas at 1280 and 390 (~61 fps on GPU), reduced motion still frame, `--disable-webgl` and SwiftShader keep the poster. Tests: `tests/templates.test.ts`.

## 31. Menu page layout (per restaurant, super admin) (2026-10-12)

`page_content.menuLayout` (`pageContentSectionSchema`, `shared/contract/sections.ts`): `"editorial"` (default; alternating photo/rows paper chapters and dark card-grid chapters, chosen by category index) or `"grid"` (every category a single light `tone-paper` section, dividers replaced by a gold accent line + spacing, category description shown, `DishTile` cards in a 2/3/4-column grid). Set in super admin → Website → Pages → Menu → the "Page content" section (its first field). It lives on the menu page's own section, not in `website.config` (it was briefly `config.menu.layout`; moved 2026-10-13 so it sits with the page it controls). The field is only shown on the menu page: `Spec.page: "menu"` in `section-editor.tsx`, filtered by the `pageSlug` prop `PageForm` passes to `SectionsEditor`; other pages' page_content sections ignore the value. `ConfiguredPage` passes it to the page as `heading.menuLayout` (no page_content marker → editorial; the seeds give every menu page one). No migration (JSONB sections; missing/unknown values parse to editorial). Rendered in `app/(site)/menu/page.tsx` (`isGrid` flag), one wrapping `<section>` not one per category (avoids doubled `section-y` padding stacking into a large gap). Search/filter results reuse `isGrid` too; the buffet band is unaffected.

Grid-layout cards use `DishTile`'s `inlineAdd` prop (the other `DishTile` placements keep the floating + over the photo — home sections, editorial layout, dish sheet). The card itself (photo, badge, name, description, meta) is the normal `DishTile` card; only its footer differs. **Footer redesigned 2026-10-18 from the "Blue Hour Menu Cards" design (owner kept only this part; an inset-photo card top was tried and reverted):** hairline, then a row of brand-coloured display-font price ("From" above it when `requiresSelection`) and the cart control (`components/storefront/grid-add-bar.tsx`): not in the cart → brand `+ Add` pill (adds 1 via `useLocalCart().addLine`, fly-to-cart + toast); in the cart → brand-filled pill `[trash|−] n [+]` bound to the dish's plain cart line (no variant/add-ons/note) via `updateQuantity`, trash at 1 removes it; dishes needing options → outlined `Customize ›` link to their page; branch gate → full-width hint pill. The row `flex-wrap`s, so on 2-column phone cards the control drops under the price. Brand tokens only. Same cookie, cart-full and branch-gating rules.

`PageHero` has an opt-in `eyebrowRule="double"` (dash both sides of the eyebrow, vs. the default single leading dash) — the menu hero uses it; every other `PageHero` caller is unaffected since the prop defaults to the old behavior.

Test: `tests/menu-layout.test.ts`.

## 32. Hero `layout: "split"`, the `stats_band` section and the floating header (per home/page section, super admin) (2026-10-13)

Both live in `shared/contract/sections.ts`, same JSONB `website_pages.sections` column as every other section — no migration.

- **Hero split layout:** `heroSectionSchema.layout` (`"cinematic"` default = today's full-bleed photo + dark scrim; `"split"` = light `tone-paper` ground, left text, right a floating `cutoutImage` — a *different* field from the regular `image`, must be a transparent-background product shot or it looks wrong). Rendered by `SplitHeroSection` in `components/storefront/sections/hero-section.tsx`; `HeroSection` branches to it when `layout === "split"`. Button styles are hardcoded (filled ink pill + outline) for this layout regardless of the stored `primaryCta.style`/`secondaryCta.style` — that fixed pairing is what makes it read as this layout. No cutout image set → the text column takes the full width instead of leaving a gap.
- **`stats_band` section type:** a full-bleed photo band with a centre `exploreCta` button (reuses the existing `cta` field shape), a caption, and up to 4 admin-typed `{value, label}` stats (same shape as `about.stats` — not computed from real data). Component: `components/storefront/sections/stats-band-section.tsx`; registered in `section-renderer.tsx`'s `RENDERERS` and fixed to `"night"` tone in `section-rhythm.ts` (it's always a photo band). Renders nothing if it has no photo, caption, stats, or button.
- **Admin:** both are ordinary `FIELDS`/`HINTS` entries in `components/super-admin/section-editor.tsx` (reusing the existing `image`/`cta`/`rows`/`select` field types — no new admin UI code).
- Test: `tests/hero-stats-band.test.ts`.
- **Header over a light hero:** the split hero's `<section>` carries `data-hero-light`; `SiteHeader` checks `main [data-hero-overlay] [data-hero-light]` and, while transparent over it, uses `tone-paper` (dark type) with no photo scrim. Before this, any first-section hero got the dark gradient + white type, which was unreadable over the split hero.

**Header style (`website.config.navigation.style`, 2026-10-13):** `"classic"` (default, unchanged: full-width bar, centred nav, hover-slide icon pills) or `"floating"` (an inset rounded panel, `--radius-panel`; nav beside the logo with the active page in the brand colour, the accent on a dark bar; round search / orders / account buttons; the cart a filled brand-colour disc with an ink count badge). Super admin → Website → Navigation → "Header style". Same `SiteHeader` component, branched on `floating`: same links, `data-tray-target` cart (fly-to-tray still lands), profile drawer, mobile menu sheet, branch pill + phone branch strip, announcement and "ordering paused" notice (all inside the panel). Search links to the menu page, whose hero holds the existing search field; no new search feature. The panel's outer gutters + inner padding add up to `container-page`'s padding so the logo lines up with page content; the outer wrapper is `pointer-events-none` so the gap around the panel doesn't block clicks. `--header-h` still measures the whole header (gutter included), so heroes and sticky rails offset correctly. Pair with the split hero for the reference look; it also works over the cinematic hero (frosted dark panel). No migration (JSONB config). Test: `tests/menu-layout.test.ts`.

`menu_categories.icon` already existed end-to-end (DB column, `MenuCategory.icon`, `categorySchema`, `createCategory`/`updateCategory`) but had no admin UI and wasn't rendered by `StationNav` — only by the home page's `menu_categories` section. Filled the gap, no schema change: icon picker in `CategoryEditForm` (`components/admin/category-manager.tsx`) is `components/admin/icon-picker.tsx` — a Radix Popover opened from a trigger button, showing every `ICON_NAMES` icon (from `components/storefront/icon.tsx`, ~48 food/restaurant lucide icons, not a plain `<select>`) as a 6-column grid of icon buttons; picking one closes the popover. `Station.icon` on `StationNav` (both `index` and `rail` variants, icon shown only when a category has one set), wired from `group.category.icon` in the menu page's `stations` array. Applies to both the editorial and grid menu layouts, since both use the same `StationNav`.

## 33. Presentation variants: the full catalogue (super admin, 2026-10-13)

Every variant is presentation only: same data, same links, same cart (`useLocalCart` cookie), same `data-tray-target` fly-to-cart, same drawers, same branch gating; each is one value in a JSONB field (no migration) with an unknown value falling back to the default. Mockups of the newer ones: design canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/QTor8tHjpdM1GsCi6e4ZUH.

| Where | Field (super admin) | Values |
|---|---|---|
| Header | `website.config.navigation.style` (Website → Navigation → Header style) | `classic` (default), `floating`, `centered`, `minimal` |
| Cover / hero | hero section `layout` (Pages → any page → Hero → Layout) | `cinematic` (default), `split`, `framed`, `collage`, `dishes` |
| Menu page | `page_content.menuLayout` (Pages → Menu → Page content → Menu layout) | `editorial` (default), `grid`, `list`, `sidebar`, `rows`, `cards` |
| Order-type doors (Delivery / Pickup / Dine-in) | `order_type_switch.layout` (Pages → Home → Order type switch → Layout) | `band` (default), `cards`, `photo`, `capsule`, `split`, `float` |

- **Headers** (`components/storefront/site-header.tsx`): `centered`/`minimal` are composed in `tailoredBar` and use the classic header shell (full-width, transparent over a hero, light-hero aware), so the announcement bar, phone branch strip and paused notice are unchanged. `centered`: nav split either side of the logo (first half left), small-caps tracked links with the sliding `nav-mark` hairline, outline "Cart" pill with an accent count chip; phones get the menu button on the left. `minimal`: logo (+ an italic monogram ring when there is no logo image), branch pill, brand cart disc and an ink "Menu" button that opens the sheet at every width (all links live in the sheet). The `tiered` style (live-hours strip above the bar) was removed 2026-10-14; a stored `tiered` falls back to `classic`.
- **Heroes** (`components/storefront/sections/hero-variants.tsx`, branched from `HeroSection`): `framed` (photo in a `--radius-panel` frame with the live-hours badge, headline + buttons beneath; light ground, `data-hero-light`), `collage` (headline beside a mosaic of `image` + up to three `galleryImages`; 1–4 tiles, duplicates shown once; light ground). `galleryImages` and `dishSlugs` use `lenientList` in `sections.ts`: bad entries (a blank row the editor added) are dropped one by one and extras past the max are cut. Root cause it fixes (2026-10-13): the first version used `.max(2).catch([])`, so saving three extra photos silently threw away all of them and the collage showed only the main photo, `dishes` (night ground; headline + buttons, then a `Rail` of `DishTile`s — `dishSlugs` (≤3) else featured items, `browseOnly` follows `BranchingFeature` exactly like `featured_items`). Primary button is always the filled brand button, secondary the outline one, in these layouts.
- **Menu** (`app/(site)/menu/page.tsx`): `list` = a printed menu in two CSS columns (`break-inside-avoid` per chapter), dish lines from `components/storefront/menu-list-item.tsx` (name, dotted leader, price, description, meta, the real `QuickAddButton` compact; no photo, so fly-to-cart falls back to the plain landed event); `sidebar` = `StationNav variant="index"` sticky on the left from `lg` (the top rail becomes `lg:hidden`), each chapter opens on its photo (`chapterPhoto`, scrim, `tone-night` header) or a plain rule when it has none, dishes as `DishTile layout="row"`; `rows` = each category a `Rail` of `DishTile` cards with a dish count. Search results, the buffet band and every add control are untouched in all of them.
- **Menu hero + search card (2026-10-18, owner's spec), `components/storefront/menu-hero.tsx` (+ client `menu-hero-controls.tsx`), replaces `PageHero` on the menu page only.** Banner: the photo is `w-full h-auto` at every width (never cropped — fixed heights cropped a banner with baked-in text twice, owner complaints 2026-10-18), so its height follows the upload's own proportions; wide uploads (~1920x640) look best. **Phone banner height is a super-admin setting (2026-10-19):** `page_content.mobileBannerHeight` (`MOBILE_BANNER_HEIGHTS` in `shared/contract/sections.ts`: `original` = whole photo, no crop · `short` 3:1 · `medium` 2.6:1 (default, also for absent/unknown values) · `tall` 2.2:1 · `extra-tall` 1.8:1), super admin → Website → Menu page → the page's content section → "Phone banner height". Phones only: a fixed frame, `object-cover` centred, so a wide banner gives up its LEFT/RIGHT edges (centre logo kept); md+ always shows the whole upload. `PHONE_FRAME` in `menu-hero.tsx` holds the static class per option (Tailwind must see them whole). Zaytoun's ~4:1 banner at 390px: original ~95px, short 125, medium 144 (360: 133, 430: 160), and 2.3:1 already cut the food at both edges, so pick tall/extra-tall only with a less wide upload. Test: `tests/page-content-section.test.ts`. After adding a field like this, restart `next dev` (snapshot keeps the old schema, section 11). `next/image` with nominal 1920x640 dims, for the srcset only. It starts BELOW the header (no `overlay`/negative margin: running under the header hid the top ~80px of the picture). The search row sits below it at every width, never over the photo (it covered baked-in text). Night gradient (left-to-right + bottom fade on md+, bottom fade on phones) built from `--color-night` via `color-mix`, its opacity = `page_content.photoDarkening` (0 - 1; empty/0 = no shade at all, the photo as uploaded — owner's request, set it when the hero carries a title). Content bottom-left: H1 = `page_content.title` (display font, 44/64px), tagline = `subtitle` (md+ only). Nothing is hardcoded: a blank field renders nothing (no "The Menu", no computed dish-count line; no eyebrow and no branch pill, both removed at the owner's request — the header already shows the branch). Search row: md+ = a white card as wide as the 1280px container, compact (40px controls, 6px padding; the 60px spec version read too tall, 48px still too tall), with a tinted search field, a "Sort" select and a brand "Search" button; phones = a white field + a 48px brand "Sort and filter" button opening a `Sheet` of sort links (`SORTS` in the page, each link keeps every other filter). Placeholder = `page_content.searchPlaceholder`. Checked 360/390/768/1024/1440: no horizontal scroll.
- **Grid card add control on phones (2026-10-18):** in the 2-column phone grid (~160px cards) `GridAddBar` (Add / stepper / Customize / gate) is full width under the price (`h-10`); from `sm` it sits right of the price as before. The gate button shows a short label ("Set location" / "Choose branch" / "Not available here") with the full sentence as `aria-label`/`title` — the sentence wrapped to 3-4 lines in a phone card.
- **Home / Published switches (super admin page editor, 2026-10-18):** the page that IS the home page has both switches disabled with a hint, because `platform.ts#savePage` refuses to unset or unpublish the home page ("Make another page the home page instead of unsetting this one." / "The home page must stay published.") — a site needs a landing page. To change the home page: open the page that should be home, tick "Home page", save; the old home loses it automatically (`updatePage` clears the others, the unique index `website_pages_one_home_idx` allows one). Then the old page's switches are free (untick Published to hide it). **Menu as home (2026-10-18):** when the Menu page is the home page, `/` renders the menu itself (`(home)/page.tsx` renders `MenuPage` when the home row's slug is `menu`, with the same search params) and `getPageContent(..., { includeHome: true })` (used by `ConfiguredPage` and `configuredPageMetadata`) lets `/menu` keep its own `page_content` heading/photo/layout; before this `/` showed only the page's other sections (a lone CTA) and `/menu` lost its hero. `/` and `/menu` then show the same page; turn the redundant "Menu"/"Home" link off in Website → Header & navigation. Only the Menu page is supported as home this way (reservation/reviews/locations as home still show just their other sections).
- **Deleting uploaded images (super admin, 2026-10-19):** the media library dialog (`components/super-admin/image-upload.tsx`) shows a trash button on every UPLOADED image (hover on desktop, always visible on touch; two-step "Delete this image for good?"). Chain: `deleteWebsiteImageAction` → `platform.ts#deleteWebsiteImage` → `repositories/media.ts#findMediaUsage` (refuses, naming where, while the URL is still in a page's sections/config/seo, the website config/theme/seo, the restaurant logo/cover, a dish or a category) → `deleteMedia` (the `media` row) → `integrations/storage.ts#deleteStoredMedia` (the file in Supabase Storage / `.uploads/`; a failed storage delete is logged, never thrown, since the row is already gone). `LibraryImage.id` is the media id; images that only exist inside a page (seeded, no row, `source: "website"`) get no delete button. Supabase's CDN can keep serving a just-deleted public URL for a short while; the object itself is gone. Checked end-to-end against the hosted DB (upload → in library → in-use delete refused → delete → row + file gone → out of library).
- **Phone bottom bar is configurable (2026-10-19):** `website.config.navigation.dock` = `{ enabled, tabs? }` (schema in `shared/contract/settings.ts`, `DOCK_TABS`; super admin → Website → "Phone bottom bar": a show/hide switch, tick up to 5 tabs, ↑ ↓ to order them). Tabs: `home /`, `menu /menu`, `about /about`, `contact /contact`, `book /reservation` (still needs reservations on), `reviews`, `locations`, and `account` = the smart slot (Track while an order is live, else My Orders when signed in, else Sign in). `enabled: false` hides the bar on phones. `tabs` absent (the default, nothing saved) = Home, Menu, Book, Account minus links switched off in Header & navigation (`disabledLinks`); once `tabs` is saved it wins and the header switches no longer matter. Tab labels reuse the header link's label for that address (a renamed link is named the same in the bar). Bad data never throws (unknown tab / more than 5 → default list). `MobileDock` is `components/storefront/mobile-dock.tsx`; test `tests/dock-config.test.ts`. The `STOREFRONT_CACHE` snapshot refreshes every 5 min, so a direct DB edit shows after that (saving from super admin invalidates it at once). Before this the tabs were hardcoded, so a link switched off in the header (Home) still showed in the bar.
- **No success toast on add to cart (2026-10-19, owner's request):** `QuickAddButton`, `GridAddBar` and `ItemCustomizer` no longer pop "<dish> added to your order" / "n × <dish> is in your cart". The feedback is the fly-to-tray animation, the header cart count and the grid card's own `[− n +]` stepper. Errors still toast (cart full, not available, location/branch needed). `ReorderButton` still toasts "Added to your cart" (it can skip unavailable items, which the customer must be told about).
- **Auth pages redesign (2026-10-19, owner's design canvas: SignInDesktop / SignInMobile / AdminSignIn / AdminSignInMobile boards).** One shell, one set of fields:
  - **`components/storefront/auth-shell.tsx`** — `AuthShell` (customer `/account/sign-in`, `/sign-up`, `/google-phone`): phones = night band (`--color-night` + faint accent tile SVG) with eyebrow (the restaurant's NAME), `mobileTitle` ("Welcome back"/"Join us") and line, form on a canvas panel overlapping by 30px, perks strip at its foot; md = the panel as a centred 440px card; **lg+ = one 1080px split card**: night panel (eyebrow, big serif `panelTitle`, `ACCOUNT_PERKS` icon list) left, form right under its own `title` h1 ("Sign in"). Two h1s exist, one per breakpoint (`lg:hidden` / `hidden lg:block`), so only one is ever rendered. The wrapper carries `data-auth-split`; forms use `lg:in-data-auth-split:flex|hidden` to put "Continue with Google" FIRST (+ "Or with email") on the desktop split and AFTER the button elsewhere (and inside any future dialog). `StaffAuthShell` (`/admin/login`): desktop full-height split (night aside "Staff portal / The kitchen, behind the pass." + form column with "Admin access" chip); phones/tablets = whole screen night, form on a raised canvas card, safe-area padding. The form renders ONCE (an earlier draft rendered it per breakpoint = duplicate ids). Staff copy only claims what is true (no "trust this device", no "every sign-in is recorded", no staff forgot-password link — none exist). Display font there is `--font-heading` (admin's `--font-display` is the sans).
  - **`components/storefront/auth-fields.tsx`** (client) — `AuthField` (54px, leading icon, label + aside, error/hint wired via aria-describedby), `PasswordField` (44px show/hide, `aria-pressed`), `AuthSubmit` (56px, spinner + working label, `tone="night"` for staff), `AuthDivider`, `GoogleButton` (official G, white, #DADCE0 — Google branding rules), `AuthStep` (icon disc + title for verify/reset steps), `AuthTextButton`, `PasswordRule` ("At least 8 characters" ticks green), **`CodeInput`**: six boxes mirroring ONE transparent `<input autocomplete="one-time-code" inputMode="numeric">` stretched over them, so paste ("123 456" keeps all digits), iOS/Android code suggestions, backspace and screen readers behave like a normal field. Used by `SignInForm`, `SignUpForm` (per-field client checks mirroring `signUpSchema` + server `details` mapped under fields; non-field errors in a danger box), `VerifyEmailForm` (auto-submits a complete code, "Resend in 0:42", success pill; also used at checkout and in the guest OTP dialog), `ForgotPasswordForm` (3 steps), `GooglePhoneForm`, `AdminLoginForm`. `PhoneInput` gained `fieldClassName` (applied to the country trigger AND the number) for the 54px fields.
  - **"Keep me signed in" is real:** `signInSchema.remember` (default true) → `setCustomerSession(token, maxAge, { persistent })`; unticked = no cookie `maxAge` (ends with the browser session). Known limit: a later re-sign of the session (email verified, profile changed) writes a persistent cookie again.
  - Digits in code boxes / order ids use the body sans: the display serifs (Playfair) have old-style figures that look uneven in boxes and ids.
  - Checked in Chrome via a same-origin iframe harness (the window is maximised, so `resize_window` is ignored) at 320/390/768/1280/1440: no horizontal overflow; empty/invalid submits show inline errors without a request; password toggle; reset → code step (paste "1 2-3" → 1,2,3). `/admin/login` was viewed on `127.0.0.1` (no cookies there, so no redirect); Next dev does not hydrate on that host, so interactions were tested on the customer forms that share the components. `AuthDialog` (`auth-dialog.tsx`) is not mounted anywhere (dead code, old styling).
- Test: `tests/menu-layout.test.ts` (every new value parses; unknown → default; too many hero extras are dropped, not the hero).
- **Order-type doors** (`components/storefront/sections/order-type-switch-section.tsx`, 2026-10-15): one component, `switch (section.layout)`; every door still links to `/menu?orderType=…` and `enabledOrderTypes` still drops types the restaurant turned off. `band` = the original dark strip (title screen-reader only, joins the hero edge); `cards` = ticket stubs (2026-10-15 redesign: details above a perforated tear line whose notches are `--color-canvas` circles clipped by the card's `overflow-hidden`, the action verb + ink arrow disc pinned below; no numerals, shadow only, no border); `photo` = tall photo tiles (`deliveryImage`/`pickupImage`/`dineInImage`; a door without one shows the cover photo spread across all doors as one triptych, not three identical crops; on phones the tiles are a swipe row (`snap-x`, 82% wide) so the triptych still reads, from md a grid); `capsule` = one rounded selector bar (stacked panel below `lg`, a row from `lg` — at md it was cramped to 4-line blurbs); `split` = heading left, numbered rows right. Other fields: `tone` (`auto` = `ORDER_SWITCH_TONE[layout]`, else `night`/`paper` forced; read by `section-rhythm.ts`, which no longer hard-codes this section as night), `showEta`, `showHeading` (needed because clearing the title field just restores the schema default), per-door `deliveryText`/`pickupText`/`dineInText` (empty = built-in line). Active/inactive = the section's own Show switch in the editor's row (+ the per-type checkboxes). **Selected door + phones (2026-10-16):** each door is `OrderTypeDoor` (`sections/order-type-door.tsx`, client): `data-selected`/`aria-current` when it equals `useLocalCart().cart.orderType` (same value the header bar and checkout read; with an empty tray that is the default type, and it only survives a full reload once the tray cookie exists), and its click calls `setOrderType` before the menu link opens. Every layout styles `group-data-[selected]` (accent ring/fill, the arrow swapped for a check via `DoorMark`). The delivery time is an `EtaTag` beside the label, never appended to the blurb (that made the delivery row one line taller than the others). Phone layouts are their own arrangement, not a shrink: cards put icon + label + time in one grid row (`grid-template-areas` switch at md; 987→725px tall at 375), split narrows the numeral column and hides its inline icon below sm, photo tiles are 80% swipe cards at 16.5rem. Measured at 320/360/375/430 with 3, 2 and 1 types: door heights equal, no overflow. Every door shares `PRESS` (accent focus-visible ring offset on the canvas + `active:scale-[0.985]`). Test: `tests/section-rhythm.test.ts`. Browser-checked all 5 (zaytoun + bella-napoli themes, 1440/820/390, hover + keyboard focus) at 1440/390 plus forced tones; super admin edit → save → storefront, and switch off → doors gone.
- **`float` doors (2026-10-18, Blue Hour-inspired):** light cards (theme surface, SHADOW ONLY — no border under it, per the impeccable craft floor's "ghost card" rule — `--radius-card`, compact 12/14px padding on phones; refined 2026-10-18 to match the owner's Blue Hour mock-up: thin accent-ring icon disc, 18px serif title, grey time chip, description in 82% ink, no arrows on phones — only the selected card shows a check, from sm a hover arrow) stacked on phones/tablets and in one row from `lg` (three columns at `md` squeezed the copy into one-word lines). Right after a hero the section's top 2.25rem is `--color-night` (`[[data-after=hero]>&]` gradient) so the cards rest on the hero's bottom edge, the heading becomes `sr-only` and the bottom padding is small; elsewhere it is a normal paper section with its heading. Each card: a brand-accent ringed icon disc (filled when selected), serif title, a chip from REAL data only — delivery = `settings.delivery.defaultEtaMinutes`, pickup = "Ready in N min" from `settings.ordering.preparationTimeMinutes`, dine-in none; both follow `showEta` — the 14px blurb, and the arrow → check `DoorMark`. Selected = accent border + 1px ring. Same `OrderTypeDoor`, so the cart is still the one source of the selected type; `enabledOrderTypes` still hides disabled types. Tone auto = muted (the soft grey ground under the cards). The cinematic hero CTA row is `gap-x-6` + primary `px-6` below sm so "Order ahead" and its text link share one line from 360px. Verified on Zaytoun and on Blue Hour (seeded into the local DB with `DATABASE_URL_MIGRATOR` pointed at it; its hero copy "Coffee worth slowing down for / Order ahead / See the menu" is already DB content on the cinematic hero) at 320/360/375/390/414/430/768/1024/1280. A chosen type now survives a full reload even with an empty cart (see section 20, `encodeCart`).
- **Door order + no pre-selection (2026-10-18, owner's request):** the doors appear in the order of the saved `order_type_switch.orderTypes` array. The super-admin field is an ordered list now (ticked types first with their position and ↑ ↓, unticked below; ticking appends), because the order used to be the invisible order in which boxes had been ticked. **No door is selected on a first visit:** the cart always carries a type (the restaurant default), but `OrderTypeDoor` marks itself selected only when `useLocalCart().orderTypeChosen` is true, i.e. after `setOrderType` ran (a door click, or a menu/dish link with an explicit ?orderType=). Remembered per restaurant in localStorage `rp_order_type_chosen_<slug>`, read in an effect (false on the server and first render, so no hydration mismatch). The chips are real settings: delivery = admin Settings → Delivery → "Default ETA (minutes)", pickup = Settings → Ordering → "Preparation time (minutes)"; both behind the section switch now labelled "Show times". Browser-checked: reorder → save → storefront order; fresh visit none selected → click → selected, survives reload.
- **Logo per ground** (`navigation.logoOnLight` / `navigation.logoOnDark`, Website → Header & navigation, two upload tiles): the header shows `logoOnLight` while its tone is paper (light header tone, or transparent over a light hero) and `logoOnDark` while it is night, each falling back to `restaurants.logo_url`; resolved in `(site)/layout.tsx`, picked in `SiteHeader` as `logo`. Why: a logo `<img>` cannot recolour itself, and Zaytoun's `logo.svg` has cream lettering and no background, so it vanished on a light header (Bella Napoli, Blue Hour and Bun Intended carry their own dark badge, so they read on both). Zaytoun now has `public/images/zaytoun/logo-on-light.svg` (dark lettering), set in its seed and in the hosted DB (2026-10-13).

## 34. Distance-based delivery pricing (per branch, admin, 2026-10-13)

Fixed fees by distance slab ("0–1 km → Rs 100", "above 1–3 km → Rs 120", …), configured per branch under admin → Locations → edit a branch → "Delivery pricing by distance". Separate concern from delivery ZONES (`delivery_zones`, section 25): a zone still decides WHETHER an address is served (drawn area / radius / area name, city as a hard boundary); distance pricing only decides WHAT IT COSTS once a zone has already matched, and can additionally refuse an address a zone would otherwise serve when it is beyond every configured slab (the same way a zone's own `radiusKm` already can).

- **Storage, no migration:** `restaurant1s.settings` (JSONB, existed since 0003 but was never read/written by `createLocation`/`updateLocation` until now) gets a `deliveryPricing: { slabs: [{maxKm, fee}, …] }` key. A slab's lower bound is implicit — the slab before it's `maxKm`, 0 for the first — so the stored shape itself cannot express a gap or an overlap; only ascending upper bounds + fees are stored. Missing/invalid/legacy `settings` reads as "not configured" (`parseDeliveryPricing`, never throws) — unconfigured branches keep the zone's own flat fee exactly as before this feature existed. An empty slab list is rejected by the schema (`min(1)`); "not configured" is expressed by omitting the key entirely, not by an empty array — so it is never mistaken for free delivery.
- **Pure logic:** `shared/delivery-pricing.ts` — `deliveryPricingSchema`/`DeliverySlab` (zod: positive ascending `maxKm`, non-negative `fee`, ≤12 slabs), `parseDeliveryPricing` (tolerant reader), `matchDeliverySlab(pricing, distanceKm)` (the slab whose `maxKm >= distanceKm`, i.e. the boundary itself belongs to the LOWER slab — exactly 1 km is the 0–1 km slab, not the next one — or `null` when beyond every slab). No DB, no React: tested directly in `tests/delivery-pricing.test.ts` (every boundary in the brief's example table, above-max, malformed config, overlap/gap rejection).
- **Distance = straight-line (haversine)**, the same measure a zone's own `radiusKm` coverage already uses (section 25). No road-distance/Directions API is wired into this project (Maps JS API is used client-side for the picker/autocomplete only, never server-side for routing) — adding one is a real, billable third-party dependency this change deliberately does not introduce; straight-line slightly under-estimates real road distance, which is why slab boundaries are an admin's own choice, not an exact-kilometre promise.
- **Authoritative fee resolution:** `resolveDeliveryFee(flatFee, branch, customerPin)` in `server/repositories/deliveries.ts` (beside `servingZones`/`matchDeliveryZone`, the existing zone-coverage code it composes with, never replaces). Overrides `flatFee` with the matching slab's fee ONLY when the branch has a pin (`latitude`/`longitude`), the customer gave a MAP PIN (not just a typed address — `address.latitude`/`longitude`) and the branch has `deliveryPricing` configured; any one missing is an unchanged no-op (the zone's flat fee, exactly as before). Returns `{fee, distanceKm, tooFar}` — `tooFar: true` (distance measured but beyond every slab) is a hard refusal, not a fallback to the flat fee.
- **Wired into `createOrder`** (`server/repositories/orders.ts`): the zone-matching SQL now also selects `l.settings as branch_settings`; after `zoneRecord` is matched (availability, unchanged), `resolveDeliveryFee` decides the fee that goes into `zone.deliveryFee` fed to the pricing engine (`domain/pricing.ts`, itself unchanged — it already only ever reads whatever `deliveryFee` it is given, never the client's). `tooFar` throws `PricingError("DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE", …)` before anything is written — same transaction, same "nothing client-submitted is trusted" rule every other price in this engine already follows. This is where the fee is actually charged and persisted (`orders.delivery_fee`, the existing column — no schema change) and where it is enforced server-side regardless of what the browser sent.
- **`priceTray`** (`server/services/cart.ts`, the checkout-preview path) gained the same `resolveDeliveryFee` call for forward-compatible symmetry (section 7 of the brief), but it is currently DORMANT: its only caller, `(site)/checkout/page.tsx`, has never passed `address`/`branchOf` (confirmed before this change — the preview total was already just "the tray's branch's first zone", not truly address-matched, a pre-existing gap this feature does not fix). Known limitation, left alone deliberately rather than wiring live-address-change recalculation into the checkout page: the user had just reverted an unrelated checkout redesign and asked for the page's behaviour left alone; the pre-submit summary shows the branch's zone fee as it always has, and the distance-based fee is correctly calculated and charged the moment the order is placed (`createOrder`, above) — the general principle this app already follows for coupons, tax and minimums ("createOrder re-derives every number from the live database; only those are charged", section 20). Wiring a live preview would need the typed/picked address to survive a page refresh (it currently lives only in `checkout-form.tsx` React state, never persisted), which is a materially bigger, separate change.
- **Multibranch:** no new feature flag (per the brief, `restaurants.features.BranchingFeature` already gates whether a branch is chosen at all). Off: the one primary location's `deliveryPricing` applies automatically, same as its zones today. On: each branch's own `settings.deliveryPricing` applies only to that branch's own matched zone — `branches` (both in `orders.ts` and `cart.ts`'s `branchOf`) is keyed by `location_id`, so one branch's pricing cannot leak into another's order.
- **Admin UI: inside the Add/Edit Zone form itself** (`components/admin/delivery-zone-manager.tsx#ZoneEditForm`, final placement 2026-10-13 — two earlier placements, a Locations-page card then a separate Delivery-Zones-page section, were both moved per explicit feedback; this is where an admin is already looking at one branch's delivery setup). `components/admin/delivery-pricing-editor.tsx` holds the reusable `DeliveryPricingEditor` (add/remove/edit rows: "up to (km)" + "fee", a derived read-only range label per row, inline order-violation warning) — a standalone component with no page-specific wiring, so it was easy to relocate each time. Distance pricing is still BRANCH data, not zone data (one config per branch, several zones can share it) — `ZoneEditForm` re-seeds it from `parseDeliveryPricing(branch.settings.deliveryPricing)` whenever the "Location" select changes (`changeBranch`), and one Save submits both: `saveDeliveryZoneAction` then, only once that succeeds, `saveDeliveryPricingAction(locationId, slabs)` — two separate server actions/tables under one button, not a combined payload, so a failure after the zone saves still tells the admin plainly (toast: "The zone itself was saved; its branch's distance pricing was not").
  - **Action + service, `delivery.manage` not `locations.manage`:** `saveDeliveryPricingAction(locationId, slabs)` in `delivery-zones/actions.ts` → `saveLocationDeliveryPricing` in `services/delivery-zones.ts`, beside `saveDeliveryZone`/`removeDeliveryZone` and using the same `assertBranchAccess(actor, locationId)` branch-scoping those already use (a manager only their own branch) — distance pricing is a delivery-fee concern, the same permission and scoping its zones already have, not a location-editing concern. It merges into the branch's EXISTING `settings` (reads the current row first) so other keys that bag holds (e.g. dine-in tables, per its own migration comment) are never silently wiped; `slabs: null` deletes the `deliveryPricing` key entirely (zones fall back to their own flat fee).
  - **The actual root-cause fix stays in the repository layer**, not the admin form: `server/repositories/restaurants.ts#createLocation`/`updateLocation` now write the `settings` column at all — the column and the `RestaurantLocation.settings` model field already existed, `settings` was simply never part of `LocationInput` or either SQL statement before this change. That fix is reused by the new service function; it is not specific to this page.
- **Tests:** `tests/delivery-pricing.test.ts` (23 tests, no DB: every slab boundary and representative distance from the brief, above-max, missing/invalid/legacy config, overlapping/gap-adjacent slabs rejected, fixed-fee-not-per-km, availability vs. fee kept separate, malformed config degrades instead of throwing). **Not run this session:** the DB-backed suites (`create-order-sql.test.ts`, `order-zone.test.ts`, a full `createOrder` integration test for this feature, admin authorization) — `npm test` needs a running Postgres per `tests/helpers/db.ts` (section 6, "DB suites need Postgres"); none was started in this session. Recommended before shipping: `npm test` (or the project's usual DB-test command) end to end, plus a browser check of the admin slab editor and a real order placed with a map-picked address inside and outside the configured range.

## 35. Tax rate by payment method (card vs. cash on delivery, admin, 2026-10-14, migration 0039)

Tax (section 21's sibling: `settings.tax`, already existed as one flat `enabled`/`rate`/`included`/`applyOnDeliveryFee`/`label` config) now has **two extra configurable rates that apply by the order's payment method** instead of always the flat rate: card (`card_online` + `card_terminal`, default 8%) and cash on delivery (`cash_on_delivery`, default 16%). Every other method (cash, wallet, bank_transfer — not asked for by this feature) still uses the flat `rate`.

- **Schema:** `shared/contract/settings.ts#restaurantSettingsSchema.tax.byPaymentMethod = { card: { rate }, cashOnDelivery: { rate } }`, both `.default(...)` — a restaurant configured before this change gets the 8%/16% defaults the first time its stored JSONB is parsed (zod fills a wholly-missing key with its default), no migration/backfill needed; it's JSONB, not a column.
- **One rate resolver, one caller:** `server/domain/pricing.ts#taxRateForPaymentMethod(tax, method)` is the only place that reads `byPaymentMethod`; `calculatePricing` is its only caller (`taxRate = taxEnabled ? taxRateForPaymentMethod(settings.tax, input.paymentMethod) : ZERO`). `PricingInput.paymentMethod` is optional — omitted (nothing chosen yet) falls back to the flat `settings.tax.rate`, same number this flow used before this feature.
- **Three callers now pass it through**, so checkout preview, server order creation and the receipt can never disagree about which rate applied: `repositories/orders.ts#createOrder` (already knew `input.paymentMethod` for the `payment_method` column — this was the only one-line change needed there), `services/cart.ts#priceTray` (new optional `paymentMethod` option), and `checkout/page.tsx`'s first SSR price (passes `paymentMethods[0]`, the same method the form defaults to, so first paint is already correct).
- **Admin:** `components/admin/settings-sections.tsx#TaxSection` gained two more rate inputs (Card / Cash on delivery) alongside the existing flat-rate field (relabelled "Other payment methods rate"); same `SectionForm`/`updateSettingsAction("tax", ...)` plumbing as every other settings section, no new action or route.
- **Checkout "recalculate immediately" on switching payment method:** `checkout-form.tsx` keeps the server-rendered `pricing` prop as its initial state, then a `useEffect` keyed on `paymentMethod` calls a new server action, `checkout/actions.ts#repriceCheckoutAction` (re-runs the exact same `priceTray` the page used, nothing duplicated client-side), and replaces the displayed `CheckoutPricing` (now carries `taxRate` too, shown next to the tax line: "Sales tax (16%)"). Guarded like `location-picker.tsx`'s reverse-geocode: a request id ref means a slower, older reprice response can never overwrite a newer one, and the very first mount is skipped (the SSR price already matches the form's default method). This is a preview only — `placeOrderAction` → `createOrder` is the one authoritative calculation, never trusting what the browser displayed.
- **Historical orders stay correct when admin changes the rates later** — not new work, already true before this feature: `orders.tax_rate`/`orders.tax_amount`/`pricing_breakdown` are written once at order creation and never re-derived from current settings.
- **Root-cause fix found while testing scenario "failed card payment → switch to COD" (migration 0039):** the checkout form keeps ONE idempotency key for its whole mount (0033) and resubmits it unchanged on every "Place order" click, including a retry after a failed online payment. `createOrder`'s replay path returned that failed order completely unchanged — same `payment_method`, same stale tax — instead of re-pricing with the new method, because nothing happened to actually charge it. Fixed at the root: the unique index `orders_idempotency_key_uidx` (and the replay lookup query) now excludes orders whose `payment_status = 'failed'`, so a resubmit after a failure inserts a fresh order (new id/order number, correct method, correctly recomputed tax) while a still-pending or already-paid order keeps replaying exactly as before (protects against real double-submits/double-charges, unchanged). Covered by two new DB tests in `tests/order-idempotency.test.ts`.
- **Tests:** `tests/pricing.test.ts` ("tax by payment method" — card/COD rates, fallback to flat rate for other methods and when no method is known, a switch changing both tax and total without double-taxing, tax-disabled zeroes regardless of method, `taxRateForPaymentMethod` directly). `tests/order-idempotency.test.ts` (two new cases: a failed order's retry with a new method creates a fresh order; a non-failed order still replays unchanged even if a different method is sent). `tests/checkout.test.ts`'s existing COD order fixture updated (232.00/1782.00, was 72.50/1622.50 under the old flat 5% — COD's new default 16% is the intended behavior, not a regression). Full DB-backed suite run locally (embedded Postgres, section 28) after these changes: all pass except three pre-existing, unrelated reservation-concurrency/booking-window failures (fail identically on a clean checkout of this branch — not caused by this feature, not investigated here).
- **Not built (ask before adding):** per-branch tax rates (tax is restaurant-wide, like before); a rate for dine-in specifically (dine-in's `cash`/`card_terminal` methods use the flat rate, same as pickup) — only card vs. COD was asked for.

## 36. Centered header: branch bar as the header's second row (2026-10-14)

`navigation.style = "centered"` (`site-header.tsx`, `tailoredBar`): a true-centered logo, links split left/right, account + cart on the right. The branch/location is the bar's own **second row** (`data-branch-strip`, `HeaderBranchPill variant="strip"`, `border-t`, container-aligned, hides on scroll-down), shown at every width for this style; other styles keep their inline pill from `lg` up (the strip stays `lg:hidden` for them).

- **Why it lives inside `<header>` and not as a sibling box (an earlier attempt, reverted the same day):** every hero slides under the header with `-mt-[var(--header-h)]`, and sticky rails (menu categories, checkout/order asides) sit at `top: var(--header-h)`. `--header-h` is the `<header>` element's height (ResizeObserver). A card rendered *after* `</header>` is outside that number, so the hero was pulled up over it and sticky rails slid under it. Anything that must reserve space under the header has to be inside the `<header>` element so `--header-h` includes it (and follows it when it collapses).
- **Centering:** `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto_minmax(0,1fr)]` (plain `1fr` has a min-content floor, so a wide side pushes the logo). Inline links appear from `xl` (1280px), the hamburger shows below it: with 6 links (3 per side) the right side collided with the logo at 1024px. Checked in a browser at 320/375/430/768/1024/1280/1440, dark and light, no horizontal overflow.
- Dark/light come from the existing `tone-night`/`tone-paper` tokens (header and strip both read `--color-ink`, `--rule`, `--color-brand-accent`); nothing hardcoded. Over a hero the header is transparent and forced to the night tone until scrolled (existing behaviour), so light mode shows once the page scrolls.
- Floating header: "My Orders" icon removed; the signed-in avatar uses `AccountInitial variant="medallion"` (dark disc, accent hairline, serif letter) so it no longer matches the cart button.
- Verifying a header style locally: set `websites.config.navigation.style`/`tone` in the **local** throwaway DB, run `next dev -p 3100` with `NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify` and `DATABASE_URL*` pointing at it, drive Playwright through Edge (`chromium.launch({ channel: "msedge" })`; the bundled browser is not installed) and pick a branch in the location sheet first (the bar renders nothing until a branch is chosen). In git-bash prefix node with `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` or `/menu`-style args become Windows paths.
- Centered style, signed in: the left hamburger (`MenuButton`, the two-line icon) stays visible on desktop too and opens the same left `Sheet` the phone menu uses (nav links + account); signed-out visitors see it only below `xl`, where the links move into it.
- **Branch strip blinked when scrolling stopped (fixed 2026-10-14):** collapsing the strip shrinks the header, which moves the page and nudges `scrollY`; that nudge read as a scroll the other way and flipped the strip straight back, repeating. `site-header.tsx`'s scroll effect now locks the strip for its 450ms transition and re-baselines `last` afterwards (state kept in `stripHiddenRef`). Browser-checked: one hide, one show, no repeats.

## 37. Root-level URLs: one deployment = one restaurant (2026-10-16, migration 0040)

The `/r/[restaurantSlug]/` segment is gone. Each deployment serves the ONE restaurant named by `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_RESTAURANT` (its own instance, usually its own database) at root-level URLs. **No redirect layer** for the old URLs (testing phase, no customer links to keep): `/r/<slug>/...` is a plain 404.

| Old | New |
|---|---|
| `/r/<slug>` | `/` |
| `/r/<slug>/{menu, menu/<dish>, cart, checkout, reservation, reviews, locations, privacy, terms, <page>}` | `/{same}` |
| `/r/<slug>/order/<number>` (+ `/events` SSE) | `/order/<number>` (+ `/events`) |
| `/r/<slug>/{orders, current-orders, account/*}` | `/{same}` |
| `/r/<slug>/checkout/pay/{stripe,jazzcash}` | `/checkout/pay/{stripe,jazzcash}` |
| `/r/<slug>/admin/...` (+ `/admin/login`) | `/admin/...` |
| `/super-admin/...`, `/api/...` | unchanged |

- **Files:** `src/app/r/[restaurantSlug]/(site)/*` → `src/app/(site)/*`; `.../admin/*` → `src/app/admin/*`. The old root `page.tsx` (redirect to `/r/<default>`) and `admin/[[...path]]` (redirect shim) were deleted. `(site)/layout.tsx` and `admin/layout.tsx` set `export const dynamic = "force-dynamic"`, as does `(site)/apple-icon.tsx`: they used to be dynamic implicitly through the `[restaurantSlug]` param; at the root Next would prerender them at build time (reads the DB during `next build`, freezes menu/theme into the build — the build failed on `/apple-icon` until this was set).

## 38. Notification/toast architecture + rapid-click batching (2026-10-17)

One global toast system already existed and needed no new library: `sonner` (`src/app/layout.tsx`'s single `<Toaster position="top-center" closeButton visibleToasts={3} />`), with every mutation a server action returning `{success, data}` / `{success, error}` — each component toasts its own result once, there is no second interceptor layer, so there was never a double-toast-per-event problem. Sonner's own stacking already shows one prominent toast with a collapsed peek behind it; `visibleToasts={3}` just bounds that peek. Row controls defer their success toast until the server-confirmed change is actually visible on screen via `useSettledToast` (`src/components/admin/use-settled-toast.ts`) — errors still toast immediately, so failures are never hidden or delayed. Every button that triggers a mutation already disables itself for the transition's duration, which already prevents the "double-click → duplicate identical toast" case structurally.

The one real gap: pages with many independent per-row actions and no bulk-select (Payments' Mark Paid/Unpaid/Refunded/Failed, Orders' per-row status buttons, Reservations, Review moderation) would pop one toast per row when several rows were actioned in quick succession. Fixed by an opt-in `batch` argument on `useSettledToast`'s returned setter: `later(soloMessage, { group, label, plural })`. Calls sharing a `group` within 600ms update the *same* sonner toast (same `id` — reuses sonner's own re-render-in-place instead of a bespoke queue) and the message becomes `"{count} {plural} {label}."`, or `"{count} {plural} updated."` if the batch's outcomes differ. A solo action still shows its original full message (e.g. "Payment for AB12 marked paid."). Wired into `payment-status-control.tsx` (group `payment-status`), `order-status-control.tsx` + `order-next-step-button.tsx` (shared group `order-status`, since both change the same thing), `reservation-status-control.tsx` (`reservation-status`), and `review-moderation-control.tsx` (`review-status`). The message-composition branch is pure and unit-tested DOM-free in `tests/admin-toast-batching.test.ts` (`composeBatchMessage`), since this project has no React/DOM test setup (`vitest.config.ts` runs `environment: "node"`) and adding one just for this would be a new dependency for one hook.

Left alone (not worth the churn for the gain): per-type default durations (success ~3s vs error ~6s+) — sonner's `toastOptions` only takes one global `duration`, not one per toast type, and the ~30 scattered `toast.error(...)` call sites all read fine at the single default; a confirm dialog (`useConfirm`, already used for refund/mark-unpaid) is the existing pattern for anything needing a decision, never a toast.

**Follow-up bug found and fixed the same day: a Mark Paid / bulk status click could hang forever client-side with no toast and no visible update, even though the write had already succeeded server-side** (confirmed by opening the same page in a second tab — the DB was already correct). Reproduced live against the admin: clicking "Mark paid" on a Payments row (or the Orders bulk "Out for delivery" button) left the button's spinner running indefinitely; a fresh tab showed the row already updated. `read_network_requests` during the hang showed ~10–20 extra `GET .../?_rsc=...` requests firing alongside the real action — one per `<Link>` visible on the page (every sidebar nav item, every status tab, every order-number link in the table) — all returning 503. Next's default `<Link prefetch>` eagerly re-renders the **target** of every on-screen link, and every admin route is `force-dynamic` + DB-backed, so "the sidebar has 12 links and the Orders table has 20 rows" means 30+ real RSC re-renders fired on every page load/re-render, each needing its own DB round trip on the hosted pooler. Those starve the browser's 6-connections-per-origin (HTTP/1.1) budget and the DB pool, so the actual Server Action POST — the one the user is staring at — gets stuck queued behind low-value prefetch traffic.

**Fix:** `prefetch={false}` on every admin `<Link>` that is either (a) always on screen (sidebar nav + breadcrumbs in `admin-sidebar.tsx`, status tabs in `admin-status-tabs.tsx`, pager prev/next in `admin-pagination.tsx`, the "back" link in `admin-page-header.tsx`, the Panel header link and status-grid tiles on the dashboard home `admin/(dashboard)/page.tsx`) or (b) rendered once per table/list row (order number links in `orders/page.tsx`, `payments/page.tsx`, `customers/page.tsx`, `customers/[customerId]/page.tsx`, `reports/page.tsx`, the menu item name/edit links in `menu/page.tsx`). None of these benefit from prefetch anyway — they're authenticated, `force-dynamic`, always-fresh admin pages, not a storefront where sub-100ms navigation matters. The bulk-action's own `toast.loading(...)` (added in the same change, see above) also helps here independently: even on a genuinely slow write, the wait is no longer silent.

**Gotcha hit while debugging this:** the server on :3000 turned out to be a **production** `next start`, not `next dev` — give away: JS chunk URLs are content-hashed (`webpack-<hash>.js`, `main-app-<hash>.js`) and there is no `/_next/.../webpack-hmr` request. Source edits do **not** take effect until that server is rebuilt and restarted; editing files and reloading the page (even a hard reload) will keep serving the old `.next` output indefinitely. Per section 11/CLAUDE.md, never `next build`/restart a server the user has running — verify with the isolated `NEXT_DIST_DIR=.next-verify next build` instead and ask the user to rebuild+restart (or get explicit permission before doing it for them).

**The prefetch fix above was real but not the whole story — the deeper bug was `src/server/db/database.ts`'s two `pg.Pool`s had no `.on("error", ...)` listener.** After rebuilding with the prefetch fix, the same hang still reproduced; `read_network_requests` now showed the actual action POST itself (not a prefetch) coming back `503`, and the dev-server's listening PID on :3000 had silently changed between checks earlier in the same session — i.e. the Node process was crashing and being restarted by something, not just running slow. node-postgres's `Pool` emits `'error'` whenever a client **sitting idle in the pool** loses its connection (the hosted Supabase pooler closing it from its side, a network blip) — entirely independent of `idleTimeoutMillis`/`connectionTimeoutMillis`, which only cover the pool's own proactive housekeeping. With zero listeners, Node treats that as an unhandled exception, which can crash the whole process. Whatever request was in flight at that instant gets no response and hangs forever client-side (no toast, no error — the connection is just gone), while a write that already committed stays committed, matching "it's correct on refresh" exactly.

Fixed with a module-level `onPoolError(pool, applicationName)` in `database.ts`, called right after each of `runtimePool`/`servicePool` is constructed, that just logs via `logger.error("db", ...)` instead of leaving the event unhandled. This is the standard, required pattern for every long-lived `pg.Pool` — see node-postgres's own docs ("you must add an error listener... or your application will crash if an error is encountered by a client"). If a "server works, then randomly stops responding to everything until restarted" report comes up again anywhere that uses `Database`/`getDb`, check first whether the process actually died (PID on :3000 changed) before chasing it as a slow-query or client-side issue.
- **Restaurant resolution:** `web/instance.ts#instanceRestaurantSlug()` (= `config.app.defaultRestaurantSlug`) replaces every `params.restaurantSlug`. Services still take the slug (it is the DB lookup key). `server/config`: `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_RESTAURANT` must look like a slug, and in **production both it and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` are required** (no silent `bella-napoli` / `localhost` fallback that would serve the wrong restaurant or mail localhost links); dev/test keep the defaults. Test: `tests/config.test.ts`.
- **URL helpers:** `shared/utils.ts#adminPath(sub)` → `/admin<sub>` (no slug argument); `isAdminPath(path)` replaces `adminSlugFromPath`. `shared/return-to.ts` lost its slug argument: `safeReturnTo(returnTo)` accepts only a same-origin path and **rejects whitespace/control characters** (browsers strip tab/LF/CR while parsing, so `"/\t/evil.example"` would become the protocol-relative `//evil.example` — the old `/r/<slug>/` prefix check had hidden that), `//`, `\`, schemes, and `/admin`, `/super-admin`, `/api`. Tests: `tests/order-access.test.ts`, `tests/admin-orders.test.ts`.
- **Admin security:** the staff cookie (`rp_admin_session`) and `rp_admin_branch` are scoped to path `/admin`. `web/session.ts#adminSlugFromRequest`: a Server Action on `/admin…` (path from the middleware's `x-rp-pathname`) checks membership in THIS deployment's restaurant, so a super admin homed elsewhere still writes here (browser-checked: a super admin homed at bella-napoli advanced a zaytoun order; bella's history untouched). The middleware now runs on every page (`matcher` excludes `_next/`, `api/` and files with an extension), not only `/r/*`. The 404 page themes itself from the instance restaurant. Super admin's restaurant list / tabs show "Open admin" only for the instance restaurant (other rows would open this restaurant's admin).
- **Server Actions still take a slug argument from the browser** (25 actions; their signatures predate this change). `web/instance.ts#assertInstanceRestaurant(slug)` refuses anything but this deployment's restaurant as NOT_FOUND, applied at the choke points: `web/storefront.ts#getStorefrontContext` (every `requireStorefront*`), `requireRestaurant(...)` in `order/actions.ts` and `reviews/actions.ts`, `web/session.ts#getCurrentStaff` (an explicit other slug = no session) and `signInStaffSession`. With the storefront cache ON only this restaurant is loaded anyway; with it OFF this guard is what stops a crafted action aimed at another restaurant in the same database. Test: `tests/instance.test.ts`.
- **Bug fixed in the same change:** `auth-service.ts#signInStaff` stamped `last_login_at` on a super admin's HOME row while scoped to the restaurant being signed into; the 0029 cross-restaurant guard (`RB403`) refused it and the whole sign-in failed with "That belongs to another branch". With one `/admin/login` per deployment a super admin can no longer pick their home restaurant's login page, so this became a blocker; the write now runs in the member's home restaurant. Test: `tests/staff-signin.test.ts` (fails without the fix).
- **Generated links:** email/push links (`notifications.ts`, base `${siteUrl}`), Stripe `success_url`/`cancel_url` and JazzCash `pp_ReturnURL` (built per request in `checkout.ts`, so **no provider dashboard change**), Google OAuth callback, JSON-LD/canonical/OG (`web/seo.ts`), manifest (`start_url`/`scope` `/`). The Stripe webhook (`/api/internal/payments/stripe/webhook`) never moved.
- **Stored links:** migration `0040_root_level_storefront_links.sql` rewrites `/r/<slug>/x` → `/x` and `/r/<slug>` → `/` in `websites.config/seo` and `website_pages.sections/seo/config`, for every slug in `restaurants` (nav, footer, announcement and CTA hrefs typed in the editor). Idempotent; verified on a seeded local DB (2 configs + 12 pages rewritten, 0 left, second run no-op). Seeds, `scripts/load/*` and `e2e/customer-flow.spec.ts` use root paths.
- **Manual steps on deploy:** run `npm run db:migrate` (0040); set `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEFAULT_RESTAURANT` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` per deployment; in Google Cloud Console change the Authorized redirect URI to `<NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL>/account/google/callback`; staff must sign in again (cookie path changed). After pulling, delete a stale `.next/types` (it still names `src/app/r/...` and breaks `tsc`/`next build` until the next build regenerates it).
- **Known, unchanged:** `/menu/<unknown>`, `/<unknown-page>`, `/order/<unknown>` render the 404 page with `noindex` but HTTP 200, because their `loading.tsx` starts streaming before `notFound()` (same as before the move). `e2e/customer-flow.spec.ts` "a guest cannot check out" predates guest checkout (2026-10-10) and is stale.
- **Verified 2026-10-16** (production build on :3100, local DB, zaytoun): 50-route matrix (storefront 200s, old `/r/*` 404, admin guards → `/admin/login`, CSV/activity 401, payment returns with bogus/forged input → `/current-orders?payment=error`, webhook without/with bad signature refused); admin + super admin browser flow 33/33; customer flow 19/19 (hostile returnTo dropped, COD pickup order placed for zaytoun, tracking page + SSE, stranger cannot read the order). Same three suites again with `STOREFRONT_CACHE_ENABLED=false`: identical. Full vitest: 663 pass except the 2 pre-existing reservation failures (`reservation-concurrency`, `reservations-reviews` slot inventory — they fail identically on the untouched branch).

## 38. Database round trips, cache invalidation across processes, images (2026-10-16)

**Measured first** (production build + a SQL spy patching `pg` `Client.prototype.query`, section 15; local DB, zaytoun): every storefront page (home, menu, dish, reviews, locations, reservation, custom pages, cart) runs **0 SQL** — the snapshot works. DB-bound pages run 1–5 statements; earlier audit (section 28) already showed Postgres is not the bottleneck under load (Node render is). On the hosted pooler what costs is **round trips** (~0.3 s each), so the changes target those:

- **`Database.read` is a read-only transaction whose commit is not awaited** (`server/db/database.ts`). Every read was 3 sequential trips (begin+context, statement, commit); `begin read only` means nothing can be written, so the result is final when the handler returns and the commit finishes in the background (the connection is released only after it, or dropped on failure). Through a proxy adding 100 ms each way: **669 → 532 ms per read**. Writes (`write`, `asRuntime`, incl. customer-scoped RLS writes) still await their commit. Postgres now refuses any write inside `read()` ("cannot execute … in a read-only transaction"): a scan found none in app code; two RLS tests that deliberately wrote through `read()` now use `asRuntime`. Test: `tests/db-read-only.test.ts` (rejects writes; 200 reads through a 2-connection pool, no leak; context isolation).
- **Admin order status counts once per request:** layout (sidebar badge) and dashboard/orders page each ran the same `select status, count(*)`; now `web/admin.ts#getAdminOrderStatusCounts` (React `cache`, primitive args). Dashboard 5 → 4 statements, orders 3 → 2.
- **Order lines + add-ons in one statement** (`repositories/orders.ts#getItemsForOrders`, add-ons as JSON per line, `unit_price::text` so `money()` sees the driver's string): `/orders`, `/current-orders`. Verified identical to the old two queries on 142 orders / 183 items / 171 add-ons.
- **Customer + saved addresses in one statement** (`repositories/customers.ts#getCustomerWithAddresses`): checkout, profile drawer. Identical on all 16 customers with addresses.
- **Awaited round trips per page view, before → after:** signed-in checkout 7 → 4, `/orders` 8 → 5, `/current-orders` 14 → 9, admin dashboard 12 (≈15 before the count de-dup) → 8, admin lists 6 → 4, admin menu 12 → 8.
- **Indexes:** none added — existing ones cover every hot query (`orders_customer_idx`, `orders_restaurant_status_idx`, partial `orders_active_idx`, `order_items_order_position_idx`, `order_item_addons_item_idx`, `customer_addresses_customer_idx`, reservations/reviews/payments composite indexes). Add one only with a plan showing a seq scan at real volume.

**Cache engine** (`server/cache`, unchanged design: single-flight load, atomic swap, invalidation queued behind a running load, old snapshot kept on failure):
- **Cross-process invalidation:** `invalidate()` used to reload only the process that handled the admin write; other `next start` processes served the old menu/photos/settings until their own refresh (prices are always re-read when the order is created, so money was never wrong). Now the cache's `onInvalidate` hook sends `pg_notify('storefront_changed', <process id>)` (`repositories/storefront-events.ts`) and every process listening on `DATABASE_URL_LISTEN` (the same session-mode connection live tracking uses; one extra LISTEN connection per process) calls `invalidate({ broadcast: false })`; a process ignores its own id; a feed reconnect invalidates too (a notification may have been missed). Without `DATABASE_URL_LISTEN` behaviour is exactly as before. Verified with two production processes and a 10-minute refresh interval: an admin rename on A appeared on B **509 ms** after commit. Tests: `tests/storefront-events.test.ts` (real LISTEN/NOTIFY), `tests/storefront-cache.test.ts`.
- **Config never breaks startup:** `STOREFRONT_CACHE_ENABLED`, `STOREFRONT_CACHE_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS` (1 s–24 h, default 5 min) and `STOREFRONT_CACHE_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_MS` (1 s–10 min, default 60 s) fall back to their default with one `[config] … must be …; using …` warning on an invalid value (was: a thrown config error, which the production startup hook turned into `process.exit(1)`). Same names, defaults and valid-value behaviour. Test: `tests/config.test.ts`. All three are now in `.env.example` (they were missing).
- **Invalidation coverage audited:** every service write to snapshot data calls `invalidate()` (menu-admin, restaurants incl. locations/settings/features, coupons, delivery zones, platform website/pages/entitlements, review moderation). `repositories/menu.ts#reorderMenuItems` has no callers (dead code), so it needs none; a new review is `pending` and invisible until moderated (which invalidates).

**Images: there are no image bytes in the database.** `media` holds metadata + a `url`; menu items, categories, the restaurant (logo/cover) and website sections store URLs only. Remote images are Supabase Storage URLs; local uploads (`integrations/storage.ts` without Supabase) are files in `.uploads/` served by `/api/media/[...path]` with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (file names are random UUIDs, so a new upload is a new URL) and resized/cached by Next's image optimizer. Every image URL the storefront renders already lives in the snapshot, so it follows `STOREFRONT_CACHE_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS` and is invalidated (now across processes) when an admin changes a photo. Nothing image-related reads the database per request, so no binary image cache was added (it would duplicate the browser/CDN/optimizer caches in Node memory).
- **Bug fixed:** `validation/menu.ts` required `imageUrl` to be an absolute URL, so a dish or category whose photo was stored locally (`/api/media/…`) or seeded (`/images/…`) could not be saved again ("Invalid url"). It now accepts an http(s) URL or a same-site path (one leading `/`, no `//`, backslash or whitespace). Test: `tests/menu-image-url.test.ts`.

**Not changed:** `requireRestaurant` (services/restaurants.ts) deliberately reads the database for writes (cancel order, submit review, JazzCash return) so feature flags/status are current; `listAllRestaurants` in super admin reads all restaurants per call (one or two rows per database). `DB_POOL_MAX` stays 10 (section 28: ≤ 7 active connections at 1,000 users).

## 39. Email design system (all transactional emails, 2026-10-17)

**Inventory (every email the app sends — there are no others):** `templates/email-verification.ts` → verification code (account sign-up AND guest checkout share it, so its copy says "on the screen where you asked for it") and password-reset code (both sent synchronously from `services/customer-auth.ts#sendVerificationCode`); `order-confirmation.ts` (order confirmed), `order-completed.ts` (order complete + review request), `reservation-emails.ts` (request received, reservation confirmed) — the last four through the outbox (`services/notifications.ts`).

**One shell + blocks** (`templates/layout.ts`): `renderEmailLayout({ brand, preheader, eyebrow, heading, bodyHtml, cta, secondary, footerNote })` and blocks `paragraph`, `sectionTitle`, `panel`, `details` (label/value rows), `codeBlock`, `divider`, `button` (bulletproof table cell), `textLink`, plus `textFooter` for the plain-text part. Table layout + inline styles, `max-width:600px`, serif headings (Georgia stack) + system sans body, no web fonts, `color-scheme: light`. Receipt (`order-summary.ts#itemsTableHtml`): quantity column, name + options, right-aligned price, hairline rows, only the charges that are > 0, total under a brand-coloured rule — all the order's stored amounts, never recalculated; money via `formatMoney` with the restaurant's `currencySymbol` + `locale`. **No copy button on codes:** email clients strip JavaScript, so a real one-tap copy button is impossible, and a "copy" link would put the code in a URL (logs, history). Instead `codeBlock` sets `user-select:all` (one tap selects the whole code where supported) and every code subject starts with the code, so Gmail/iOS can offer their own "Copy code" / autofill.
